# Permissions and ownership in FileCat

**Decision, 19 November 2026.** Add one FileCat-owned **Permissions and ownership…** command. Capture the selected object before opening the editor. Use native Windows descriptors, Linux POSIX ACLs, Darwin ACLs, or the server's exposed attributes behind that command. FileCat owns the draft, authorization handoff, job, verification and return to the workspace. No editor, helper or native write has been qualified by this planning exercise.

## 1. Delivery and baseline

Implementation starts at **I1** in [the work packages](#8-ordered-work-packages). The [support matrix](#2-support-matrix) gives the intended user-facing coverage. Use [the acceptance ledger](#9-acceptance-ledger-risks-and-decisions) to decide what can ship.

The first useful milestone is an exact-subject inspector plus a reviewed, unelevated edit of an ordinary NTFS file on Windows x64 and of mode/access ACLs on Ubuntu x64. Ship the inspector on a lane once its read gate passes, and each edit only after its own write and interaction gates. The next local milestones deliver directories and volume roots, privileged repair, audits, flags, and Apple-silicon editing. The provider milestone adds qualified server operations. The work and acceptance tables define completion for each lane. A missing package or mandatory system approval remains visible in the product and in its release notes.

Choose the FileCat editor on Windows as well. Native ACE ordering, rights and access checks cost implementation and maintenance, but this gives one exact-subject review, multi-selection, consistent keyboard and assistive technology, and accountable writes. ACLUI's property pages require a data provider. FileCat implementing `ISecurityInformation::SetSecurity` would still own the supplied section merge, authorization and verification. The Shell Properties verb supplies neither a FileCat-bound transaction nor proof of its effects. Keep Properties independent. Before switching from a dirty review to external Properties, offer Stay, Discard and open, or Apply then open on verified completion. `ShowProperties` returns launch success and has no sheet-close notification. Mark the snapshot stale and re-inspect on workspace reactivation or explicit Refresh, reporting observations only. No ACLUI callback, ShellHost extension or Explorer window is a delivery dependency. [Microsoft access control editor](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/secauthz/access-control-editor), [SetSecurity callback](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/aclui/nf-aclui-isecurityinformation-setsecurity).

**Author-recorded checkout.** Local `main` at `3c74139d2318bd8303c26e1ba6de6f7584288e55`, 347 commits behind the locally recorded `origin/main`. No repository `AGENTS.md` was found. Inspection used working files: six tracked changes included Windows ARM64, README, NTFS/EFS planning/evidence, installer and WindowsEfsKeys work; untracked planning/evidence and EFS interaction work was also present. Installer/EFS UI changes remain relevant to future trust/modality regression, not evidence of an ACL broker.

**Review check, 19 November 2026.** The same HEAD and working changes were present. The local `origin/main` ref had moved to `4c9092225` (358 commits ahead and still moving); implementation starts from current mainline, not this HEAD. Mainline keeps the SDK and package pins but adds, in `2f786b07b`, two privileged routes that §§3 and 6 now govern: Windows **Open FileCat as administrator** (a whole high-integrity FileCat launched from the running executable, portable included; such a process skips interrupted-job review at `JobJournal.cs:777`) and the Linux/macOS **Permissions and times → Review permissions as administrator…** action, `App/Services/UnixPermissionApproval.cs` (pkexec, or osascript `do shell script … with administrator privileges`, re-running the FileCat executable as root on a client-built rwx/sticky plan). Mainline also maps the palette keyword "permissions" to `tools.openAdministratorWindow` and adds disk-space analysis rows as another item origin. Revalidate HEAD, mainline and working changes before each package.

`global.json` and local `dotnet --version` give SDK 10.0.101, roll-forward disabled. `Directory.Build.props` targets net10.0 (local 10.0 runtime patches include 10.0.15). `Directory.Packages.props` pins Avalonia 12.1.4, MVVM Toolkit 8.4.0, SSH.NET 2026.0.0 and FluentFTP 55.0.0. RID declarations include win-x64, win-arm64, linux-x64, linux-arm64 and osx-arm64. Published targets in [CAPABILITIES](../CAPABILITIES.md) and [implementation status](../IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md) are Windows 11 x64/ARM64, Ubuntu 24.04/26.04 x64 GNOME, macOS 26+ Apple silicon. A build RID alone does not qualify a release.

### Source observations

Paths below are relative to the checkout. `Core`, `App`, `Remote` and `Platform.Windows` in path references denote the corresponding `src/FileCat.*` projects. New services, verbs and jobs in subsequent sections are proposals.

| Area | What exists and where to join it |
|---|---|
| Commands | `Core/Commands/CommandRegistry.cs` registers Properties (Alt+Enter), Attributes and separate storage actions. `App/ViewModels/MainMenuModel.cs` has File → Inspect. `MainViewModel.Commands.cs::PropertiesPathForFocus/ShowPropertiesForPath` recognizes Computer drive roots on Windows, calls `WindowsShellServices.ShowProperties` (owned Shell verb), or opens the Unix FileCat record. `Views/ContextMenuFactory.cs`, `Views/PanelView.axaml.cs` and `MainViewModel.LocationContext.cs::ExecuteLocationContextAsync` have distinct item, Shell, place and captured-background routes. |
| Selection and preparation | `Core/Listing/SelectionSnapshot.cs`, `Core/Search/ResultSetProvider.cs` and `WorkingSets.cs` represent selected rows and originals. `App/ViewModels/MainViewModel.Preparation.cs::PreparationScope` tracks listing generation and transfers/releases selection. `MainViewModel.MutationPreparation.cs` checks a mutation baseline on scheduled I/O. `MainViewModel.Scope.cs::CommandScope` carries tab, dialogs and view. `Core/FileSystem/SourcePathReview.cs` checks selection metadata, identity and final path. None captures a security revision. |
| Details and presentation | `MainViewModel.FileRecord.cs::ShowFileRecordForPath` creates a modeless `ReportWindow` from a path. `Views/ReportWindow.Details.cs` owns its reader, refresh/cancellation and extra-data/target actions. Add an exact-subject adapter as specified in the [Details plan](FILECAT_FILESYSTEM_DETAILS_AND_FEATURES_PLAN.md). Windows `WindowsFileRecords.SecuritySection` asks for owner/group/DACL/label and formats SDDL. `WindowsFileRecords.Log.CompareSecurity` compares stored/live representations. `Core/Records/AccessText.cs` and `UnixFileRecords.cs` supply wording, not lossless edit sources. Read live OS security without the report's raw storage paths. |
| Unix editor | `MainViewModel.Tools.cs::ChangeAttributesAsync` enters `MainViewModel.Permissions.cs::ChangeUnixAttributesAsync`. Tri-state grid, touched bits, three/four-digit octal, set-ID/sticky, times and macOS Finder Hidden already exist. A rejected octal parse leaves the last box values live at submission. `UnixPermissions.Apply` implements conditional execute inside folders. `JobModel.cs::AttributeChangeSet` and `Executors.cs::AttributesExecutor` change by path, skip links, walk with `IgnoreInaccessible=true`, and apply folders last. Retain the behavior users rely on while replacing the permission-writing path. |
| Identity and providers | `FileSystem/Ownership.cs::Principal` includes kind, ID and authority. `PrincipalNames.cs` separates lookup from identity. `UnixOwnership` enables Linux x64, gates Darwin. `WindowsOwnership` enables x64, gates ARM64. `App/Services/AppServices.cs` wires metadata and resolution. Keep editor qualification separate from columns, sort, Find and passive metadata. `Resources/ResourceProvider.cs`, `Location.cs`, `Entries.cs` describe coarse navigation. `FileSystem/ComputerProvider.cs::DriveTag.RootPath` identifies a displayed root. `Network/UnixNetworkProvider` returns `Location.FileSystem` for mounted shares. Capture or recover mount and authority independently. |
| Native primitives | `Core/FileSystem/LinuxPosixAcl.cs` copies ACLs on held fds, conflating absent/unsupported on one read path. `MacExtendedAttributes.cs` and `FileSystemOperations.cs` carry transfer policy. `Core/Archives/ArchiveUnixOuterMetadata.cs::LinuxAcl/MacAcl`, `ArchiveUnixUpdateMetadata.cs` and `Archives/Unix/UnixArchiveFile.UpdateMetadata.cs::ApplyAcl` capture/restore staged metadata, including external Darwin ACLs. `UnixArchiveNative*.cs`, `UnixFiles.cs` and `UnixFiles.Child.cs` supply ABI and descriptor-relative precedents. Share tested primitives without adopting archive admission or copy normalization. |
| Binding and observations | `LinuxPinnedDescriptors`, `HiddenData/UnixHiddenData.Inventory.cs`, `Records/UnixFileRecords.Details.cs`, `UnixFileRecords.MacVolumes/MacProtection/Zfs.cs` and `MacMaterializationPolicy` establish bounded metadata reads, returned-field validity, mount evidence and thread lifetime. `Platform.Windows/Elevation/NtFile.cs`, `SecureFileOps.cs` and `WindowsStorageTransforms.Traversal.cs` provide held root/relative child patterns. Compression eligibility and its restrictions on links/volumes are feature-specific. |
| Execution and authority | `Core/Jobs/JobModel.cs`, `Executors.cs`, `JobJournal.cs` and `UndoRunner.cs` define dispatch, durability and guarded inverses. `MainViewModel.Operations.cs` refreshes and unmarks completed roots. `OperationCenterViewModel.cs` and `Services/OperationReport.cs` present results. `ElevationModel.cs`, `Platform.Windows/Elevation/` and `FileCat.PrivilegedHost/` have a closed v1 protocol, native bootstrap and consent. Even the dedicated ReadDevice verb conveys no security-writing authority. `App/RecoveryProcessCensus.cs`, `Recovery/Unix/UnixDevices.cs` and `DBus.cs` address census or read-only drive access. |
| Remote | `Remote/Sftp/Channel.cs` lists entries without following the leaf, while `RemoteStat` describes a followed stat. Both omit security-field presence. `SshNet.cs`, `Connections.cs::SftpLease`, `SftpProvider.cs` and `SftpJobs.cs` supply pinned transport, generation, trust, registry and reconnect machinery. FTP shares this interface through `Ftp/FluentFtp.cs::FtpChannel`. Best-effort timestamps and repeat-on-reconnect are unsuitable for security changes. |

**Inherited evidence.** The [ownership acceptance record](../validation/column-ownership/ACCEPTANCE-20261116.md) covers enabled readers and identity presentation. [Held Windows traversal](../validation/filesystem-details/2026-11-17-held-traversal.md) concerns compression. [Linux held mount observations](../validation/filesystem-details/2026-11-18-linux-mount-details.md) cover ext4 and a ZFS dataset. The [Darwin thread policy record](../validation/filesystem-details/2026-11-19-macos-details-materialization.md) distinguishes ordinary APFS evidence from the disabled live provider fixture. The [Darwin event-only descriptor probe](../validation/filesystem-details/2026-11-18-darwin-ordinary-attributes.md) observed a readable file and a FIFO reader side effect, and open-by-ID refusal for FileCat. These are evidence for narrow premises only. No new runtime tests, installs or permission probes were run here.

## 2. Support matrix

The OS/model column describes possible platform semantics. The FileCat column defines proposed deliverables. **Validated release coverage for the new editor is zero at this baseline** across all rows. Existing mode editing, report reads and transfers have their own narrower evidence. Gate IDs refer to the single ledger in §9. Each stage admits only the operations proved on the target, package and ABI. Section reads, writes and authorization have independent states.

| Target and OS/model | FileCat inspection and change | Authority, stage and release qualification |
|---|---|---|
| Windows NTFS, fixed or removable | Owner, primary group, ordered DACL, inheritance/protection, audit ACEs, qualified access calculation. Same model on removable NTFS. Root is a directory with volume-wide risks. | User token first. Installed broker for additional rights. W1 file baseline, W2/W3 directories/root, audit and privileged work. G-WIN/G-PRIV/G-TREE include removal and a disposable NTFS volume. |
| Windows ReFS | Windows descriptor sections actually returned. Offer each owner/DACL/audit/inheritance or access operation only when independently admitted. | Same Windows routes. W2, G-WIN on disposable ReFS. An NTFS test or filesystem capability flag alone cannot enable ReFS. |
| Windows UNC and mapped SMB | Server-exposed descriptors and inheritance. Bind session, share, actual resolved server/volume and returned file identity. File calculation may omit share policy. | Server account/ACL rights. No local UAC retry. N1/G-MOUNT require independent server reads, protected children, referral/remap/disconnect and unsupported SACL. |
| Ubuntu ext4, XFS, Btrfs with POSIX ACLs | UID/GID, mode/special bits, access ACL, directory default ACL. Immutable/append flags when the mount implements qualified ioctl(s). Mode-only mount has no extended ACL editor. | L1/L2 for user and permitted groups, L3 for broker authority. G-LINUX/G-FLAG, ext4 full lane and XFS/Btrfs focused equivalents. For mode-only prove persistent per-inode permissions and truthful unsupported ACL state. |
| macOS APFS or HFS+ | UID/GID, BSD mode and special bits, ordered UUID ACL, creation inheritance and editable user flags. Evaluate Ignore ownership independently for eligible volumes. Ordinary documents bind through §5's parent-relative lane in folders only the user and root can change. | D1/D2 ordinary, D3 installed trusted helper (blocked by DEC-03, §6). G-MAC/G-MACAUTH/G-VOL, APFS primary and HFS+ secondary image. Show projected identities and disable misleading writes on ownership-ignored volumes. |
| FAT/exFAT, read-only/offline media, synthetic mode | Show format, provenance, projected owner/mode and constraints where observable. Ordinary FAT/exFAT lacks a persistent per-item descriptor. Read-only permits available reads. Offline means unavailable with last observation dated. | I1/N1, G-MOUNT. A friendly mode or successful no-op never unlocks editing. |
| Mounted SMB/CIFS, GVfs/FUSE or NetFS SMB | Identify mount, remote share and client projection. Native Windows descriptors through qualified Windows routes. On Unix offer only demonstrably authoritative POSIX/Darwin operations. Otherwise report translated fields or unavailable server ACL adapter. | Current mount credentials and server policy. N1/G-MOUNT, one kernel CIFS lane and one userspace or NetFS projection, checked at server. No local-root substitute. |
| NFS, NFSv4, ZFS | Check protocol, mount and dataset ACL policy. NFSv3 with real mode/POSIX ACL or a ZFS POSIX ACL dataset can enter qualified POSIX paths. NFSv4 ACEs and ZFS NFSv4 ACLs have native order/flags and policy-dependent mode interaction. | N1/G-MOUNT. This release exposes corroborated fields and model reason on rich NFSv4 routes, with no rich setter. A reader must preserve every ACE if it claims a full listing. Root squash, idmapping and dataset aclmode/aclinherit are separate constraints. |
| Other FUSE/unknown model | Record type, mount provenance, returned fields and uncertainty. Each independently qualified native projection may opt into its own operations. | I1/N1, G-MOUNT. Missing adapter or no authority is visible. No trial writes to decide support on the user's file. |
| SFTP | Server-returned mode/special bits and paired numerical UID/GID. Changes are server-path-verified through `lsetstat@openssh.com` (§5); the protocol exposes no object identity. Basic transport offers no general audit, effective-access, extended ACL, flags or inheritance UI. | R1/G-REMOTE, account bound to verified SSH server. Independent mode and group gates. Include a server denial case, a server without lsetstat and Windows OpenSSH (both inspection only). |
| FTP and FTPS | LIST/MLSD visibility with provenance. UNIX.mode or trustworthy permission bits distinct from transfer rights. No setter in this release: SITE CHMOD follows links by path. | R2/G-FTP, authorized connection and FTPS trust where used. Inspection only. |
| Search, Find and working-set originals | Resolve the stored original item/provider/session and apply its row above, including mixed authorities. An archive selected as a disk file is its outer file. | U1, G-UX/G-BIND. Stale/deleted originals show their own errors. |
| Archive member, archive-as-folder background, Registry, MTP, recovery, raw device, Computer background | Explain the object type. Where appropriate offer a separately identified outer-file review. Virtual records have no filesystem ACL write route. | U1, G-UX. A This PC drive row resolves to its mounted filesystem root. No physical disk, partition or raw security-record actions. |

Configured rights are available per readable section. Object auditing and a Windows token-based estimate belong only to the Windows route. Unix routes offer the credential-scoped explanations in §5. Remote protocol observations alone establish no hypothetical user's effective access. On translated mounts corroborate native reads with an independent client or server. [Linux CIFS checks](https://docs.kernel.org/admin-guide/cifs/usage.html), [NFSv4.1 ACLs](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8881.html#section-6), [OpenZFS dataset properties](https://openzfs.github.io/openzfs-docs/man/master/7/zfsprops.7.html).

## 3. Entering and using the editor

**One command ID.** Register `file.permissionsOwnership` under File → Inspect and in item and background actions. Index permissions, security, ACL, owner, access and inheritance for the command palette, ranked above the mainline commands that share its keywords (`tools.openAdministratorWindow` for "permissions"; Settings and Analyze folder for "owner"/"ownership"), or drop those keywords there. Make it bindable under Settings → Keyboard. Keep Alt+Enter for Properties, Alt+Shift+Enter for extra data, Ctrl+Alt+Enter for records and Ctrl+Shift+I for row inspection. No new default key or permanent toolbar button. Support the ordinary FileCat menu and actions reachable from the Windows Shell-menu integration. Native authentication uses OS appearance and an owning window where supported.

| Invocation | Capture before asynchronous admission |
|---|---|
| Focus or marks | Use FileCat's mark-or-focus selection. Show selected total and hidden-mark count. Offer include all or visible only before binding the immutable scope. `ContextMenuFactory.BuildItems` dispatches against the live VM on click. Capture entry indices, hidden bits, original items, navigation generation and mark revision when opening the item menu. Carry the capture through Shell → FileCatActions fallback and release it on dismissal. Filtering hidden items operates on that capture. Use `MainViewModel.StorageTransforms.CaptureStorageItems` and its origin checks as patterns. Capture each Computer row's `EntryData.Tag` before `ItemRef.FromEntry` drops `DriveTag`. Label actual subjects. Never drop a refused item silently. |
| Background | Capture panel, tab, exact location and originating generation at menu opening. The background means the current folder, including mounts absent from Computer. Reject a changed listing during preparation. |
| Drive row or Places drive menu | Resolve its captured `DriveTag.RootPath` on every OS. Capture mount evidence when listing/creating the menu subject, then require the same live mounted root at preparation and apply. Missing evidence calls for a refreshed drive listing. A path now naming the underlying directory, new media or a different mapping cannot inherit the old review. |
| Find, working set or Details | Read the original ItemRef or explicit report subject, with identity evidence from its observation. A path-only old report must reacquire and compare original evidence or offer a clearly new review. Register Find's command in `Views/FindWindow.cs::ListCommands`, `FileItems` and scoped `Run`/availability paths. Bind a completed snapshot of result originals, never the actively growing search. For Details open a **one-item review** through the owning visible workspace's dialog service. Do fresh admission, then transfer subject/leases into review ownership. Closing Find, refreshing the report or changing marks cannot redirect submitted work. No live owner means the link is disabled with a reason. Keep the Details action unavailable until this adapter exists. Disk-space analysis rows use Find's original-item adapter or keep the command unavailable there. |

**Layout.** Lead with recognizable target names, selection count and scope; keep kind, model and authority identifiable. Full paths and canonical IDs are copyable, with unresolved or disambiguating IDs prominent. Mixed scope has per-item reasons. The initial Access view shows owner/group and the native mode grid or rules. Offer Ownership, Inheritance/defaults, Flags, Auditing and Access explanation where meaningful. Rule rows show principal, type, rights, scope, explicit/inherited state and known source; expand for custom masks, GUIDs and opaque fields. Keep repeated principals separate. A preset shows affected bits and requires deliberate replacement for rights outside it.

Reuse `DialogContracts.cs`, `Views/OverlayDialogService.cs` and `Controls/DialogLayout.cs`. Wide views pair rules with selected-row fields; narrow views stack them. Give the virtualized list a finite viewport and deliberate scroll ownership. Keep target/scope summaries bounded and actions reachable; large diffs belong in the scrolling body, not an unbounded pinned region. Review begins with plain-language changes, affected counts and exceptions, then expandable per-object old → new details. Place scope beside its change. Wrap/scroll full identities without tooltip dependence. Reuse `Formatters.SafeName`/`DescribeHiddenNameCharacters` and `OperationIssueDialog`'s labelled **Copy exact…** pattern throughout review/results; escaped display must not alter underlying values. Preserve selection, focused row and scroll during asynchronous resolution or refresh when the item remains present.

**Visual contract.** Reuse `Themes/Styles.axaml`, `Services/ThemeManager.cs` and `Services/ReadabilityPolicy.cs`. Bind colors, `FcFontFamily`, typography, input sizes and Compact/Comfortable spacing dynamically. Preserve intentional theme fonts; use established title/body/secondary hierarchy and wrapping instead of shrinking text. Scope, consequences and validation use readable body text. Theme, density and OS high-contrast changes update the open editor and popups without losing draft, focus, selection or scroll. Custom subscriptions/renderers follow existing lifetime and motion policies.

Allow/Deny, inherited/explicit and mask-limited are labelled permission data; reserve success/error/warning colors for outcomes and qualified warnings. Draft additions/removals/changes use words and localized emphasis distinct from selection. Keep read-only values and refusal reasons readable/copyable. Ordinary Apply uses the shared primary style; destructive reset uses the reviewed danger treatment. Check actual foreground/fill pairs on cards, selected rows, badges and diff surfaces, including hover/focus/invalid states. Use matching `FcOn*`/focus foregrounds; an explicit muted child foreground must not defeat selection contrast. Apply the existing text/focus contrast criteria to actual composited surfaces, not assumed panel backgrounds. Icons accompany text; color alone never carries state.

**Keyboard and validation.** Use named controls, headings, mixed-state announcements, predictable Tab/arrow navigation, visible focus, Space toggles and keyboard row add/edit/remove. Enter consumed by principal suggestions or rule editing performs only that local action, never also outer Apply/OK. Announce meaningful loading, validation, conflict and result transitions without moving focus or repeating every keystroke/lookup update. Restore focus to the invoking control or workspace on close.

**Interaction states.** Load sections independently; unavailable sections explain the affected action and supported next step. Opening/reviewing or discarding an unsent draft writes nothing. Dirty shows per-component changes. Keep literal input and parse result together; untouched mixed and deliberately cleared differ. Incomplete/invalid octal, identity, scope or mask disables Apply/OK and their Enter submission. Show field-associated errors and a keyboard-accessible summary leading to the first problem; disabled-button activation cannot reveal them. Keep focus while correcting, and reparse visible controls on submission, focusing a problem if that check fails. Exact octal touches all 12 mode bits. A checkbox edits only that bit, and returning to mixed clears that touch.

**Unix editor migration.** U1 replaces the Unix Attributes permission draft and validation. The §9 owner decision retires the legacy administrator action and the legacy mode writer for unbound ordinary and root processes. At preparation the service records each captured item's binding and the dialog shows which mode changes can be reviewed. A refusal stays attached to that item. Mode edits for recursive scopes await the corresponding G-TREE gate, including explicit budgets and mount boundaries. Directory, executable-file or pre-held-descriptor evidence does not qualify ordinary Darwin documents.

An independently requested timestamp or Finder Hidden change retains its existing scope. When a qualified binding covers those fields, submit the reviewed permission component first, then timestamps and Finder Hidden on the same identity. The Finder Hidden step patches only `UF_HIDDEN` against a fresh flag read and checks the identity, mode and flags after the call. Unmigrated Finder Hidden uses its no-follow parent-relative route. When mode changes require further review or authority, preserve the draft and allow an independent timestamp or Finder Hidden submission. Run a later component after the preceding component commits or already matches. Only unmark roots for which every submitted component is verified or already matching.

Apply queues reviewed changes once, prevents duplicate submission and stays open on fresh results. Clean OK closes; dirty OK applies and closes only on verified success. Before submission, Cancel/Esc offers discard for a dirty draft; discarding later edits never undoes earlier writes. While applying, Close can detach to Operation Center; Stop requests cancellation between calls. Header close/Esc follows the current close action; labels, tooltips and accessible help must accurately distinguish discard, detach and stop. Preserve unsent intent after validation, lookup or authorization cancellation. Conflict refresh may retain intent for a new diff, never silently reapply it. Partial/failure/uncertain results distinguish completed work, remaining work and uncertain effects, with **Review current state** and expandable diagnostic codes/details. Ordinary deltas need one Apply; broader scope and privilege review precise consequences.

**Scope.** Model five distinct intents: (1) selected object itself, default; (2) change what new children inherit, or a POSIX directory default; (3) let an identified native operation propagate to existing descendants; (4) explicitly visit a reviewed descendant set with a delta; (5) replace/reset descendant rules from a specified source. Each has its own availability and explanation. If a single API combines (2) and (3), present one combined scope or refuse it. A matching root never short-circuits (3)–(5). For (5), show explicit entries lost, ACL/protection/mask affected, protected-child policy and exceptions. Defaults keep protected children. A reset that changes a protected child must identify it for separate explicit review. Root reviews identify the mounted volume and distinguish its directory, propagation, and volume ownership policy.

For an explicit reset, the native adapter supplies the derivation. On Windows, preview each child's replacement inherited block and explicit ACEs to be removed. For POSIX, copying a directory default into an existing file is a new access ACL change: map base rows/mask, specify conditional execute and approval of any resulting expansion. For Darwin, derive ordered inherited copies with per-child propagation/inherit-only flags; preserve explicit rules for a delta and list the ones removed by a reset. Disable reset if the adapter cannot compute the exact native child transformation or keep the OS from applying another implicit pass. A single checkbox cannot express these operations.

**Mixed changes and example journeys**

- A marked NTFS file and directory show their own rights mapping. A rule delta identifies the native section and full original ACE bytes, ordinal/occurrence, principal, flags, GUID/condition and order in that object's baseline. Only an unambiguous match can update/remove. Adding a rule has its own insertion decision. Ambiguous/opaque/heterogeneous matches lead to per-item editing. A POSIX entry key is ACL type + native tag + numeric qualifier. Bulk editing uses a per-object fresh baseline and preserves other differences.
- A Linux project folder can give a group `rwx` in **Defaults for new children** while leaving its current access and existing tree intact. Show the default mask and the effects of requested creation mode. An owner repairing a mode-`000` file can inspect its ACL and add only owner read without opening its contents. Request admin only when normal authority cannot perform the operation; for denied local ownership a scoped privileged read can precede a new, exact write review.
- An APFS document in the user's own folder is edited without being opened; a document in a folder other accounts can change shows why this command cannot bind it. An APFS file shows an unresolved UUID as a copyable principal. Locked is a separate change. To change permissions on a locked object the user reviews an explicit unlock and its effects, or cancels. On an external volume, Ignore ownership has a separate policy review.
- On SFTP the user sees observed mode, numerical owner/group and connection identity, and the review says the server identifies items by path. Changing only group shows the paired unchanged UID the protocol may need. An unavailable pair prevents that change. Server denial stays associated with the server account.

## 4. Service, subject lifetime and concurrency

Introduce `IPermissionService` at the Core platform/provider boundary, composed in `AppServices`. Keep native readers/writers beside Windows, Unix and remote implementations. ADR-01's provider capabilities remain a cheap eligibility hint. A scheduled `Inspect(subject, sections)` returns an immutable snapshot and per-operation decisions. `Prepare(snapshot, draft, scope)` builds the actual diff, expected binding, security baseline and write prerequisites. `Apply(review, jobContext)` revalidates, writes and observes via the same adapter. Use one `JobKind.Permissions` and the existing executor registry, scheduler, decisions, journal and Operation Center. A helper consumes a strictly smaller versioned wire representation, never serialized VM objects. [ADR-01](../adr/ADR-01-resources-and-capabilities.md), [ADR-03](../adr/ADR-03-native-operations-and-streaming.md), [ADR-10](../adr/ADR-10-metadata-scheduling.md).

| Value | Required information and invariant |
|---|---|
| Subject and binding | Exact provider, raw original item/name/ordinal or mounted-root locator, display path, authority/session generation, link policy and kind. Hold an object/parent or provider lease with independently verified identity, mount instance/volume and root membership. The path is for display and namespace revalidation. |
| Snapshot | Section-specific observation (value, absence, unloaded, denied, unsupported, failed, stale/gone, time and API). Retain raw native bytes/flags/order and validated editable projections. Carry separate owner/group, access, audit, defaults/inheritance, flags and policy where applicable. Distinguish native absence from a known empty list, and a missing adapter from a read-only mount or lack of credentials. |
| Operation capability | Per component × action × target × scope. Include model, native authority needed, package and ABI qualification, preservation/verification limits, reason and fallback. Volatile denial remains distinct from permanent lack of a setter. Acquire sections requiring additional authority only through explicit privileged inspection. Ordinary authorized lazy loading, refresh and retries remain unelevated. |
| Identity | Use `Principal(kind,id,authority)`, immutable independently of the label. Add Darwin UUID as a distinct kind with normalized 16 bytes and UUID comparison. Preserve previous ordering/equality and serialization of SID/UID/GID. Keep server account/host/profile epoch or Unix mapping context with its IDs. Validate SID structure with native conversion and round-trip; UID/GID range and namespace representability at submission. |
| Delta and review | Typed native operations such as patch exact ACE, set touched bits, change POSIX defaults or assign one owner. Identify required and preserved sections, original row occurrence, expected before-state, known normalization/side effects, exact consent scope, origin and transfer of lifetime. Never submit a dump of displayed text or generic xattr values. Order compound changes so that no intermediate state grants a right absent from both the reviewed before and after states (restrictive changes first, then ownership, then remaining additions); if no such order exists, the review shows the temporary widening. |
| Result | Per object and component record attempted call, known prior/expected/observed state, read-back quality, native error and whether further work was attempted. Distinguish verified committed, matching, partial, failed-before-change, uncertain, skipped and conflict; Darwin's lane adds "applied; the folder changed during the call", which counts as changed with that note. Keep operation verdict separate from scoped observations about other principals. |

Resolve names on demand (debounce 250 ms, two outstanding per authority, 25 suggestions and a bounded timeout). Accept validated canonical SID/UUID/numeric ID directly even if lookup finds no label. Exact ambiguous input requires choosing an authority-qualified result. Retain unresolved known IDs. No domain enumeration on open, no local NSS or account substitution for SFTP/FTP IDs. For authority-less existing Unix identities preserve existing semantics. Cancel stale lookups by generation, and discard late UI publication.

**Binding sequence.** Capture source, bind it off the UI thread, inspect, transfer ownership into a review, authorize if requested, revalidate object *and security*, transfer once into job, read back, release in a finally after native return. During menu/preparation a changed origin invalidates the action. After transfer the reviewed identity wins over subsequent navigation. For direct selections detect rename/replacement of the reviewed name and request fresh review. An exact handle protects against a last-moment name swap after validation (on HFS+, `exchangedata` can still move a regular-file descriptor; §5 limits that). Darwin's parent-relative lane and SFTP server-path changes (§5) have no such handle; they detect and report swaps within their stated residuals. If a bound object loses its name following commit, report that fact and observed handle identity. Never rebind automatically to the name's new occupant. On Windows screen reparse tags, offline/recall attributes and sync-root membership through qualified no-recall metadata handles before opening a security handle or enumerating a subtree. For unknown hydration effects return unavailable. A user may separately request materialization and start a new review. Test a registered provider with a fetch sentinel. Walk from a verified volume/share root with handle-relative no-reparse opens, qualify mount-point crossing as a separate binding. On Unix anchor at a verified mount, walk directory-fd-relative segments with no-follow, check mount and type at each boundary, and validate the final entry. On Darwin anchor at the containing volume (`fstatfs` volume ID and mount point), accept a volume change only at a firmlink into the same APFS volume group, and open ancestors with `O_SEARCH | O_NOFOLLOW`, so traversal needs only search rights. A deliberately resolved alias or link target receives its own exact-subject review.

Recheck removable media, mount instance, network authority/referrals, hard-link identity and section-specific state after elevation/reconnect and immediately before each component. Compare the whole ACL/control block if setting an ACL, and owner/group, mask/mode, flags or policy whenever the delta depends on them. Preserve unrelated sections through narrow API flags. An unread unrelated SACL need not block a DACL-only call with proved isolation. Changes to timestamps/size alone cannot identify a security conflict. A changed baseline returns conflict and a new diff, never a silent merge. The native read–compare–write interval still allows other writers. Serialize FileCat writes to a bound identity, minimize the interval, avoid writes of untouched sections, read back on the same handle and report interference. Do not promise cross-process atomic compare-and-swap.

**Limits and scheduling.** Proposed first limits are 1 MiB native data/section (or a smaller native maximum), 4,096 rows per object, 64 MiB per review, 128 live handles/leases per review, 100,000 distinct identities for a reviewed tree and 256 levels. Apply the 64 MiB budget to retained memory and spool combined. Use the tighter provider budget. Stream/spool a bounded immutable manifest in protected app state if necessary. Evict and re-open only with strong generation/object proof. If the route needs a retained handle and exceeds the limit, split into fresh review batches before any write. Show overflow as incomplete and disable dependent actions. One active writer per device/authority, at most two independent inspection workers per authority under the device scheduler; cap pending review requests at 32 per workspace and coalesce obsolete ones. Menu construction does no native filesystem/account work. Keep workers, leases, pooled buffers and native allocations owned until a blocking call really returns. UI cancellation detaches display, preserves late mutation results in the job and stops later calls. Apply normal name-service/authentication/auditing side effects only as part of the relevant route. Passive work performs no elevation, payload fetch, raw-device access or extraction.

**Explicit tree work.** Review a bounded captured manifest of object identities, per-object baseline/delta and ancestry; the consent names this set. Do not discover new mutation targets during apply. Recheck ancestry/mount and membership when opening each captured child. Exclude later arrivals and report known drift, vanished entries, unreadable branches and unknown subtrees. Require a new review to include them. Default to a single filesystem and never enter symlinks, junctions, reparse points or nested mounts. Normalize overlapping roots and deduplicate by strong object identity within the budget. Call out that a hard-linked inode changes through every name, including unknown aliases elsewhere. If identity/boundaries cannot be established, disable the tree request. Apply children before directories for restrictive changes; if traversal would require temporarily widening a parent, report the blocked branch. Separate a deliberate parent grant and a later fresh tree review. No implicit owner takeover, Full Control, unlock, deny removal or inheritance reset. For OS propagation the manifest and its counters do not describe what the OS visited.

## 5. Platform operations

### Windows

Use `GetSecurityInfo` on a held file/directory handle with `SE_FILE_OBJECT`; read owner/group/DACL, protection/control and audit data by independent section. Keep a validated self-relative binary snapshot, SID bytes and exact ACL revision/ACE order. Track absent DACL, present NULL (unrestricted discretionary access), present zero-ACE (no discretionary grants), nonempty and unreadable separately. NULL/absent remain distinct representations during preservation. Never synthesize NULL to recover from an error. An explicit change from an unrestricted ACL to a concrete one needs a lockout preview. Retain primary group unless deliberately changed with its own admission. [Security descriptor DACL states](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/securitybaseapi/nf-securitybaseapi-setsecuritydescriptordacl), [Windows file access rights](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/fileio/file-security-and-access-rights).

Offer Read, Read & execute, Modify and Full control as explained presets, plus native advanced bits. Map generic access to file/directory masks before comparison. Distinguish read/list, write/add file, append/add subdirectory, execute/traverse, delete on object versus delete-child on parent, attributes, extended attributes, synchronise, read security, write DACL and write owner. ACEs keep type, flags, mask, object/inherited-object GUIDs, conditional data, resource bits and unresolved SID. Untouched bytes remain in order. Editing one known entry replaces only that occurrence. For a new ACE propose insertion into a known canonical explicit block, preserving inherited order. For noncanonical/opaque boundaries offer explicit insertion with order preview only if semantics can be proved, otherwise disable that addition. Never silently sort or collapse entries. Deny and allow evaluation is ordered and depends on which rights are still requested. [ACE ordering](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/secauthz/order-of-aces-in-a-dacl).

Show object/container inherit, inherit-only, no-propagate and inherited bits separately. Inherited entries are read-only at that child. Show verified inheritance origin if available, otherwise “source unknown”. Protecting a DACL prompts to copy inherited ACEs as explicit at their existing relative order or remove them with a loss preview. Enabling inheritance retains explicit entries, replaces the inherited block from the current parent per native rules and previews it. Disabling inheritance is a separate protected-state change for each of DACL and SACL. If unreadable parent, unknown ACE transformation or unavailable inheritance source makes the proposed merge indeterminate, block that conversion. No invented origin or automatic deduplication.

**Folder writes have two backends to qualify.** W2 qualifies a section-limited handle `NtSetSecurityObject` call for object-only ACL changes, including whether filesystem, redirector and protection flags can normalize or propagate. Ship that button only when sentinel descendants and concurrent controls establish its scope for the exact API and target model. On failure remove the object-only choice on that route.

Use `SetSecurityInfo` on a verified handle opened with explicit required rights and normal sharing for the separate **allow Windows to update inheriting descendants** intent. The API can propagate inheritable DACL/SACL entries to existing children, preserves explicit ACE order, and propagation depends on sharing and handle access. Refuse combined ancestor/overlapping FileCat traversal and OS propagation. W3 never runs native propagation of a change that is not strictly restrictive into a folder the requester can add entries to: a standard user who may modify another user's file can hard-link it there (Microsoft documents that creating a hard link needs only `WRITE_ATTRIBUTES` on the target), and propagation would then rewrite that file. Offer reviewed traversal instead, where §6's link-count rule applies per object. Do not use exclusive sharing or MAXIMUM_ALLOWED as an accidental scope switch. If only propagation-capable directory writes are qualified, preview them as such even for a one-folder selection. A matching root with requested repair needs a separately proved propagation trigger or explicit reviewed traversal/reset; otherwise return unavailable before any call. Test protected, inaccessible, nested and linked/reparse children and volume roots. If traversal boundaries/extent cannot match consent (including links or another mount), disable that native scope for the target. One native call supports no per-child counts, cancellation guarantees or all-child verification. Report root observed and descendant effects unverified unless independently established. [SetSecurityInfo](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/aclapi/nf-aclapi-setsecurityinfo), [NtSetSecurityObject](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/nf-ntifs-ntsetsecurityobject).

Separate `READ_CONTROL`, `WRITE_DAC` and `WRITE_OWNER`. Taking ownership chooses the requester's assignable SID/group with `WRITE_OWNER` or appropriately enabled `SeTakeOwnershipPrivilege`. An alternate administrator's `SeTakeOwnershipPrivilege` does not entitle that token to assign the original requester as owner. If the reviewed original SID is not assignable by the acting token, require the separately approved, qualified `SeRestorePrivilege` assignment or refuse. Test both paths. Never infer the new owner from the elevated token. For audit inspect/edit use `ACCESS_SYSTEM_SECURITY` and enabled `SeSecurityPrivilege` for the narrow open/call. Show success/failure audit flags, mask and inheritance. Preserve integrity labels, resource attributes, central-policy and unrecognized SACL ACEs, including their order/control. Do not set a SACL whose unread data cannot be preserved. A denied SACL leaves a readable DACL available. Audit policy determining whether an event is emitted remains machine-controlled. [SACL access](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/secauthz/sacl-access-right), [owner assignment contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/aclapi/nf-aclapi-setnamedsecurityinfow).

**Authorized baseline read.** W3 must qualify metadata-only held-handle acquisition of `READ_CONTROL` with the acting token's scoped `SeBackupPrivilege` and applicable backup-intent semantics when an ordinary DACL read is denied. Request only needed security/identity rights and preserve held-relative binding and no-recall safeguards. Check actual privilege availability/enablement, restore privilege state after the qualified scope, and report denial if this route fails. SACL admission remains separate. Never take ownership, relax the DACL or read file contents merely to obtain the baseline. **Authorized write.** When the reviewed DACL denies the acting administrator `WRITE_DAC`, W3 opens the bound object once with backup intent, without following reparse points or triggering recall, requesting `READ_CONTROL | WRITE_DAC` (plus `WRITE_OWNER` for a reviewed owner change, and `ACCESS_SYSTEM_SECURITY` under `SeSecurityPrivilege` for a reviewed SACL edit; never generic write or `DELETE`). Keep `SeBackupPrivilege` and `SeRestorePrivilege` enabled on the helper thread's impersonation token through the open, the set call (where owner assignment is checked) and the read-back on that same handle, then restore them. State in consent that the DACL is overridden, and refuse before writing if either privilege is unavailable. Never take ownership to gain access. See [Privilege Constants](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/secauthz/privilege-constants), [backup open semantics](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-createfilew) and [GetSecurityInfo](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/aclapi/nf-aclapi-getsecurityinfo).

**Effective access.** Start with a real unelevated requester token and an actual descriptor. Map the requested rights and initialize the resource manager with `AUTHZ_RM_FLAG_NO_AUDIT` for an ordinary unelevated calculation and an Authz context from that token. Pass applicable object-type lists and claims. Inspect the per-result error and granted mask in `AUTHZ_ACCESS_REPLY`; a successful API invocation alone gives no grant. Treat unhandled callback ACEs as unknown. See [Authz resource manager](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/authz/nf-authz-authzinitializeresourcemanager). Report principal, server, token/groups/restrictions, grant mask, evaluation time and which constraints were evaluated. A different SID-only context is an estimate. Missing claims, resource attributes, restricted/deny-only groups, privileges, owner rules, mandatory integrity, central policy or share ACL can change results. Mark affected rights unknown; do not present a local allow as remote share authorization or as authority to delete in the parent. Cache only against descriptor, token/context and mount/connection revisions. [AuthzAccessCheck](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/authz/nf-authz-authzaccesscheck).

### Unix mode, ownership and access explanations

For a file, `r/w/x` controls reading, writing and executing content; interpreter, mount and policy still matter. For a directory, `r` lists names, `x` searches/traverses known names and `w` with search changes entries. Rename/remove depend on parent(s), search permission and sticky-directory rules (entry owner, directory owner or qualified privileged exception). File write permission alone cannot predict deletion. A replacement save can create a different object with different rights. Directory setgid normally influences the group of new children; distinguish this from a setgid executable, default ACL and existing children's ownership. Explain executable setuid/setgid and `nosuid`. Label exact root-file execute and recursive conditional `X`: by default new execute bits reach directories and already-executable descendant files. Offer exact recursive execute only as an explicit increase with diff, leaving ordinary documents untouched in conditional mode. [inode](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/inode.7.html), [path resolution](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/path_resolution.7.html), [unlink](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/unlink.2.html).

Distinguish recorded grants, a current-process check for a specific right, and hypothetical access. `UnixPermissions.CanWrite` uses `access`, which uses real IDs with specific capability semantics. Use a qualified effective-ID check only with its credential/group context and bound path/object, and explain its point-in-time limits. A hypothetical POSIX ACL calculation can display the DAC result with supplied groups and owner/mask; absent supplementary groups, mount options or mandatory policy make an overall verdict unknown. Display observed read-only, noexec/nosuid, namespace, idmap, immutable flags or security labels as observed. Root squash, LSM, capabilities, TCC or SIP may be possible constraints; `EPERM` alone does not identify the cause. No create/delete/execute probes. [access(2)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/access.2.html), [Apple filesystem protections](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/Security/Conceptual/System_Integrity_Protection_Guide/FileSystemProtections/FileSystemProtections.html).

### Linux POSIX ACLs and flags

Show two sections, **Access now** and directory-only **Defaults for new children**. Require one USER_OBJ, GROUP_OBJ and OTHER, unique named user/group qualifiers and one mask for extended named entries. Validate sizes, tag lengths, IDs, bits and kernel ordering. A mask can also exist without named entries. Base rows are mandatory. Numeric UID/GID zero is valid, an out-of-range ID, `-1`/all-ones setter sentinel or unmappable ID is not a principal. A missing account label preserves the numeric identity. Evaluate owner first, then matching named user constrained by mask, then matching groups per POSIX's requested-rights test and mask, otherwise OTHER. A matched class that lacks rights does not fall through to Others. A named user with zero grants is not a Deny ACE. [POSIX ACL access and creation rules](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man5/acl.5.html), [acl_valid](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/acl_valid.3.html).

The simple Group checkbox row edits **group-class limit** when an access mask exists, else GROUP_OBJ. The ACL grid edits the owning-group entry itself. Show both configured and mask-limited rights on every affected named user and group. Preserve an existing mask by default. A grant masked off shows its actual result and offers explicit mask expansion with all affected rows in the diff. For the first named entry derive the least new mask realizing the old effective group permissions and reviewed new grant; preview any unavoidable widening before Apply. Never run blanket recalculation. Removing named entries, collapsing to base access, removing a directory default and setting mode are different operations. When collapsing access, default the retained group bits to the *effective* owning-group permissions; show the alternative and any expansion. Removing a mask while names remain is invalid. Mode changes update owner, OTHER and class limit coherently, with one authoritative ACL/mode projection. Changing the default mask does not change current mode. Empty/default removal means remove the entire default ACL. Never set an empty access ACL. [ACL mask recalculation](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/acl_calc_mask.3.html), [default/access setters](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/acl_set_file.3.html).

A child's initial POSIX ACL comes from the parent default restricted by the caller's creation mode; absent a default, umask filters that mode. Do not guarantee execute on new documents or retroactively change children. There is no recorded inheritance source on a later POSIX access ACL. Darwin combines creation modes/umask and inherited ACEs separately. Never change process-wide umask in the editor. [Linux umask](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/umask.2.html), [Darwin umask](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/System/Conceptual/ManPages_iPhoneOS/man2/umask.2.html).

**Bound implementation.** Pin with `O_PATH | O_NOFOLLOW | O_CLOEXEC` relative to verified directories. Query statx on the live pin, require returned masks for type, mode, UID, GID, inode and mount identity, plus device/mount-table correlation; missing fields are unknown. Use checked UAPI parsing for the two fixed `system.posix_acl_*` xattrs only, little-endian version, exact header and entry sizes, defined tags/qualifiers and legal permissions. Retain original bytes, reject unknown versions/tags on dependent writes. Distinguish ENODATA (absence on a qualified interface), EOPNOTSUPP, EACCES and I/O errors on the still-held subject. Access absent falls back to observed mode; directory default absence has no entries. A three-entry access ACL equivalent to the mode is stored as mode only, so read-back treats ENODATA with the matching mode as committed. Use get/set/remove on only the selected fixed attribute. The access/default distinction is required even if selecting libacl for validation: `acl_set_fd` alone cannot write a directory default. [Linux ACL xattr UAPI](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/master/include/uapi/linux/posix_acl_xattr.h), [acl_set_fd](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/acl_set_fd.3.html).

For owner-authorized mode-000 repair use a metadata pin, never require a content-readable fd or temporary widening. On the Ubuntu 24.04 GA kernel lane (6.8) qualify `fchmodat2(pin,"",mode,AT_EMPTY_PATH)` and `fchownat` empty-path, with correct ABI and rights. Qualify later xattr-at exports/syscalls independently for get/set/remove, availability and seccomp. For older xattr APIs run a short-lived native metadata worker with the pin held and a verified **own procfs fd directory** as its private cwd: a separate single-threaded process, or a dedicated thread that first calls `unshare(CLONE_FS)`, because threads otherwise share one working directory. Use only the decimal live fd name, following its kernel magic link for fixed-name ACL operations or chmod fallback on ordinary files/directories. Validate a trusted procfs root, own PID/namespace view, relative descent without substituted mounts, filesystem magic, and that the numeric entry resolves to the pinned mount/inode/type; keep the pin and proc directory open throughout. Reusing `LinuxPinnedDescriptors.ReopenReadOnly` would reintroduce a read-access dependency. Confine symlink ownership to separately qualified no-follow fd semantics. Special nodes get no content open or ioctl fallback. If API/procfs/security policy denies both safe routes, keep that operation unavailable with reason. Never reinterpret a seccomp denial as permission to escape the policy. Compile wrappers against the target ABI and headers, verify exports on the minimum libc, and gate per RID. [Linux chmod](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/fchmod.2.html), [O_PATH semantics](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/open.2.html), [chown](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/chown.2.html).

For immutable/append-only, use `FS_IOC_GETFLAGS/SETFLAGS` on a verified suitable ordinary descriptor, preserve every unrelated bit, and surface the privilege requirement (including `CAP_LINUX_IMMUTABLE` where required). Distinguish an unavailable data-fd ioctl from mode/ACL availability. An explicit clear of protection is its own first step; setting protection comes last. For a compound edit, after any restrictive mode or ACL part (§4's no-widening order), apply chown/permitted chgrp, re-read set-ID, mode and file capability state; chown can clear security.capability and set-ID. Apply reviewed access ACL/base mode and defaults, then only expressly requested special bits. Do not restore privilege-bearing bits or capabilities incidentally from an old snapshot. If the user requests set-ID together with chown, explicitly review the new owner's implications and verify the kernel result. Prepare derives the kernel's set-ID side effects from the baseline and the acting credential (kernel group list, `CAP_FSETID` or root): on Linux an ACL or mode write by a non-member of the file's group clears set-group-ID silently; on Darwin every chown or chgrp clears both set-ID bits, even for root and unchanged IDs, and a mode write by a non-member that sets or merely preserves set-group-ID fails. The review states these effects, disables set-ID requests the kernel would drop or refuse, and needs explicit acceptance before any write that clears an existing set-ID bit. Stop the remaining components if an unexpected change breaks their preconditions. Only after verification set requested restrictive flags. Report intermediate successes if later work fails. Avoid path utilities such as setfacl which can alter mode before returning an error. [Inode flags](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/ioctl_iflags.2.html), [ID mappings](https://docs.kernel.org/filesystems/idmappings.html), [setfacl failure behavior](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man1/setfacl.1.html).

### Darwin ACLs, flags and ownership policy

Use Darwin's ordered extended ACL alongside BSD mode/UID/GID. File and directory aliases differ: read-data/list, write-data/add-file, execute/search, append/add-subdirectory, delete/delete-child, read/write attributes and extended attributes, read/write security and change-owner. Show allow/deny, file/directory inheritance, inherit-only, limited propagation, inherited marker, and ACL-level flags. Preserve unknown permissions, tags, flags, order and repeated UUIDs. BSD mode and other policy can decide rights that ACL evaluation leaves unresolved. An absent extended ACL and a present empty ACL with flags are distinct observations. Neither implies Windows empty-DACL semantics. A parent's inheritable ACEs affect creation; changing existing children requires a separately reviewed traversal. Darwin has no POSIX mask/default table. [Apple filesystem security model](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/FileManagement/Conceptual/FileSystemProgrammingGuide/FileSystemDetails/FileSystemDetails.html).

Use `acl_get_fd_np(ACL_TYPE_EXTENDED)` or the qualified no-follow observation, `acl_size/acl_copy_ext` and bounded native duplication. Retain an untouched native snapshot including ACL-level flags on zero entries. Rebuild only edited fields on a clone via Darwin ACL APIs, initialize/insert or delete the one targeted entry and re-fetch entry references after operations that can invalidate pointers. Iterate FIRST then NEXT: Darwin returns zero on success and uses EINVAL at the documented end, so distinguish expected termination in a valid iterator from an invalid handle/argument. Linux iterator return values do not transfer. Copy a qualifier's 16 UUID bytes before freeing its native allocation, with canonical UUID byte order (do not assume .NET Guid array layout). Free native ACL/qualifier handles exactly once. Resolve UUID to a label or user/group ID when meaningful. A synthetic ID from `mbr_uuid_to_id` may not persist. Preserve the UUID as the ACL principal. [Darwin iteration](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/System/Conceptual/ManPages_iPhoneOS/man3/acl_get_entry.3.html), [Darwin UUID mapping](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/System/Conceptual/ManPages_iPhoneOS/man3/mbr_gid_to_uuid.3.html).

Use `acl_copy_int/acl_set_fd_np`, fchown, fchmod and fchflags on a qualified held object; the parent-relative lane below uses the `*at`/`setattrlistat` equivalents. Separate link-object operations from target editing. Apple libc's `acl_set_link_np` uses lstat, rejects a symlink and delegates other paths to a file setter. Disable link-object ACL writes on that API. Separately qualify no-follow link ownership. The `acl_delete_*_np` stubs returning ENOTSUP cannot implement whole-ACL removal. Qualify `fchmodx_np` with a filesec containing only the documented ACL removal sentinel on a bound fd, independent of a zero-entry ACL with flags. Absence errors, including ENOENT, require confirmed continued existence/identity and a documented filesystem-specific result. [Apple libc ACL implementation](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/Libc/blob/main/posix1e/acl_file.c). In all other cases keep unknown/gone. Archive staging's owner-only, ordinary-file and deny-refusal rules are specific to publication. Keep them at their current callers. [Darwin fd/link ACL setters](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/System/Conceptual/ManPages_iPhoneOS/man3/acl_set_fd_np.3.html).

**Acquire without downloading or opening a special node.** Use no-follow `getattrlist`/relative stat with returned masks for passive observations, including volume/fsid, file ID and change/generation evidence where available. Wrap native work synchronously in `MacMaterializationPolicy` on its owning worker, restore on every exit, retire the worker on restoration failure. Never await inside the policy scope. Cached observations, dataless, refusal and user-requested materialization are separate. Darwin has no type-safe metadata-only open: `O_EVTONLY` still implies read access (`FFLAGS`) and opened a FIFO in the recorded probe, and `O_UNIQUE`, `O_RESOLVE_BENEATH` and `O_NOFOLLOW_ANY` do not filter by type. D1 therefore qualifies two binding families on APFS and HFS+, each checked against the observation (fsid, file ID, type, live mount; not the write generation count, which changes with content writes).

- **Descriptors.** Open directories with `O_SEARCH | O_NOFOLLOW` relative to the held parent; it needs only search rights, and a non-directory fails with `ENOTDIR` before the filesystem open. For an already-executable regular file use `openat(heldParent, name, O_EXEC | O_NOFOLLOW | O_CLOEXEC)`: `FFLAGS` drops the access mode for `O_EXEC`, and XNU's open authorization returns `EACCES` for `FEXEC` on a non-regular vnode before the filesystem open, so a substituted FIFO or device is never opened. Verify the compound-open path, no download and every fd setter on the supported ABI. Never add an execute bit to obtain a descriptor. An already-held descriptor from a trusted FileCat workflow qualifies only if its acquisition, type, identity and live mount are proved and the service uses that same open object. On volumes that support `exchangedata` (HFS+; APFS does not), anyone holding read-data and write-data rights on a regular file can move its descriptor onto another file, whose ID, owner, mode and flags come with it. There a regular-file descriptor binds only while no principal other than the acting authority (the requester for D1, root for D3) and root holds or can obtain both rights on the file (its owner and any write-security or change-owner grantee can), so D3 binds HFS+ regular files only when root owns them; re-read the descriptor's file ID and volume after every setter and treat a change as uncertain. The parent-relative lane is unaffected, because identity and metadata stay with the name.
- **Parent-relative lane** for ordinary documents, data-inaccessible files and owner-inaccessible folders. It opens nothing. Admit it only for an unprivileged requester, and only while no principal other than the requester and root can replace or mount over the entry:
  1. The entry is owned by the requester.
  2. `fstatfs` on the held parent shows a local APFS/HFS+ mount with ownership honored, whose mount owner (`f_owner`) is the requester or root (the mount owner can switch Ignore ownership on with a mount update), and the entry's fsid equals the parent's; volume roots bind only through descriptors.
  3. The parent is owned by the requester or root, its mode grants no group or other write (sticky shared folders are excluded), and its ACL grants add-file, add-subdirectory, delete-child, write-security or change-owner to no other principal; for a directory entry, which can be renamed over through rights on itself, neither its ACL nor its mode grants add-file, add-subdirectory, write-security or change-owner to another principal. Replacing a file entry needs add rights on the parent, so the file's own ACL does not matter here. Evaluate this as kauth does: expand generic rights, skip inherit-only entries, and count everyone, group and unresolved principals as other.

  XNU always lets an owner rewrite its own folder's security, so stricter exclusivity is unattainable in user folders. First arm an `EVFILT_VNODE` knote on the held parent (`NOTE_WRITE`, `NOTE_ATTRIB`, `NOTE_DELETE`, `NOTE_RENAME`, `NOTE_REVOKE`); every create, link, unlink and rename there posts `NOTE_WRITE`. Before and after every call, re-evaluate 1–3 and re-read the entry with `getattrlistat(parent, name, FSOPT_NOFOLLOW)` (type, fsid, file ID, link count from `ATTR_FILE_LINKCOUNT` or `ATTR_DIR_LINKCOUNT`, mode, IDs, flags, host-order `kauth_filesec`, change time), comparing identity and security fields with the review before the first call (change time alone is not a conflict, per §4) and, before each later call, every field, change time included, with FileCat's read after the previous call. Send one component per call with `fchmodat`/`fchownat` (`AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW_ANY`) or `setattrlistat` (`FSOPT_NOFOLLOW_ANY`; an entry count of `KAUTH_FILESEC_NOACL` removes the ACL). Order the calls so that no intermediate state grants a right absent from both the reviewed before and after states (restrictive changes first); if no such order exists, the review shows the temporary widening. A failed check before a call is a conflict. After a call, a changed identity or type, or an admission condition that the reviewed after-state did not change, yields "uncertain: may have reached another object"; when the reviewed after-state itself changes an admission condition, the item's later components need a separate Apply; a parent event with the same file ID, type and volume before and after yields "applied; the folder changed during the call", never plain committed. The no-follow flags modify a final symlink itself, so the type re-check must catch that. Residual, stated in the review: a process running as the requester, or root, can swap the entry during a call, and a swap restored before the read-back may go unreported; the change still stays within the requester's own authority. This lane is the only Darwin exception to §4's handle binding.

Elsewhere (folders other principals can change, ownership ignored, network or FUSE mounts) a document stays inspection-only on this command with that reason. A pathname setter outside the lane, or an fd opened after a pathname check, does not satisfy the contract. Keep directory, executable, pre-held and lane results separately deliverable. For D1 the FIFO probe rules out `O_EVTONLY`/`O_NONBLOCK` acquisition; D3's daemon open (§6) has a separately gated special-node-open residual. `openbyid_np` (platform binaries or a private entitlement) and XNU's private file-lease entitlement exclude them as general FileCat backends. [XNU open authorization and lease policy](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/main/bsd/vfs/vfs_subr.c), [Darwin open](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/main/bsd/man/man2/open.2), [XNU setattrlistat](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/main/bsd/vfs/vfs_attrlist.c), [XNU directory events](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/main/bsd/vfs/kpi_vfs.c), [Apple dataless policy](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/technotes/tn3150-getting-ready-for-data-less-files).

Show Finder Locked (`UF_IMMUTABLE`) and user append-only where supported. Patch only reviewed user bits against a fresh flags word and verify unrelated flags. System immutable/append, SIP restrictions, dataless, compressed and firmlink state are observations only. Finder Hidden stays in ordinary attributes and uses this shared touched-bit primitive (§3), both to avoid overwriting concurrent flag edits and because `Fs.SetAttributes` maps Unix ReadOnly to a chmod. Use explicit protection clear, owner/group, ACL/BSD mode, then explicitly requested protective set with intermediate checks as on Linux, applying restrictive mode or ACL parts before ownership per §4's no-widening order; derive and show Darwin's set-ID effects as for Linux, and qualify its set-ID/ACL interactions independently. No implicit unlock/relock. If the first step commits and a later step fails, report “unlocked, permissions not verified” as appropriate. [XNU chflags](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/main/bsd/man/man2/chflags.2).

**Ignore ownership** changes enforcement across the volume. Give eligible local data volumes a separate page and single-volume job. D2 qualifies the OS-shipped `/usr/sbin/diskutil` documented enableOwnership/disableOwnership verbs through a root test harness and records whether they need root; unless an unprivileged run succeeds, D3 enables the in-app setter. G-VOL covers the supported OS, persistent effect across remount and independent observation; G-MAC covers the policy display. The documented setting is local to this Mac for that volume. Ignoring ownership changes how IDs are presented and enforced; it does not recursively chown existing files. Test that re-enabling honors the original IDs. Use the target OS's shipped `man 8 diskutil` and `diskutil help` as the primary command reference; record build and verb availability in G-VOL. Bind volume UUID, mount instance, filesystem, device identity and role. Use a fixed trusted binary, two fixed verb choices, OS-validated volume identifier, bounded output and no shell. Qualify identity stability across the utility's own resolution (including removal/reuse/duplicate UUID) or withhold its setter. Re-inspect after every return, invalidate volume-wide caches and require fresh reviews for objects. Exclude system/boot/sealed/backup or ineligible roles. Never edit ownership databases or infer a Disk Arbitration option. If persistence, volume identity or OS restrictions cannot be established in the two eligible/blocked fixtures, ship observed policy only and record the exact failed gate. [Apple external volume ownership](https://support.apple.com/guide/mac-help/mchlp1204/mac).

### Mounted and remote resources

Carry actual mount provenance through `UnixNetworkProvider` or recover it from the held mount table/`fstatfs`, negotiated server/share and mounted root. Kernel CIFS `noperm`, server checks, FUSE/GVfs and NetFS can expose materially different projections. For NFS use the negotiated version, credential/ID mapping and exposed API; for ZFS identify the mounted dataset, `acltype` and policy via a qualified observation. Detect native, translated, synthetic and unidentified separately. A mount hiding an ACL says nothing about server absence. Windows SMB root resolution rejects changed mappings/DFS referrals. Never elevate locally to repair server authorization. Refuse setters where an authoritative read-back cannot distinguish persistent server state from local synthesis. Use representative cross-client read-back at qualification, then a qualified coherent read at execution.

**SFTP.** Extend `IRemoteEntry`/`RemoteStat` with optional, presence-bearing security fields and observation kind. Add explicit security read/patch methods to the connection adapter, separate from the common transfer primitives. SFTP v3's UID/GID are a pair and permissions are a native mask. Missing wire flags mean unobserved, including when the public library projects a default. Authority includes verified host key, endpoint/port, protocol, authenticated account, profile revision and session generation. Account labels are server-supplied or numerical. Never query local accounts for them.

The installed package nuspec points at SSH.NET commit `7b2fd3dbf2c86a80a7b06cea020aa5f821c9902e`. Its `SftpFileAttributes` records original values and emits change flags; changing either ID emits both. The public IDs are signed `int`, and deserialization uses sentinels for omitted fields. `SetPermissions(short)` parses **octal digits**: native 0x1ED (decimal 493) → argument 755; native 0x5FD (octal 2775) → argument 2775. Preserve file-type bits, test 0000/0777/1777/2775/4755/7777 and high IDs. Reject truncation, indistinguishable sentinels and unknown counterpart IDs. Build from fresh observed attributes and audited field presence or use a narrowly scoped wire adapter. Inspect the actual serialized flags/bytes; a newly default-filled attributes object or a stale size/time is unacceptable. Public `SftpClient.SetAttributes`/`ChangePermissions` canonicalize with REALPATH, which resolves a final link, then send SETSTAT, which follows links; never use them for security writes. The public surface has no handle FSTAT/FSETSTAT and no extension requests (`ISftpSession` and its `RequestFSetStat` are internal), and public `GetAttributes`/`Get`, which FileCat's current stat uses, also canonicalize with REALPATH before LSTAT, so they never see a link. R1 therefore starts with a dependency decision: a vendored or forked SSH.NET at this pin (MIT; license, lock and SBOM update) or an upstream release, with narrowly typed requests for `lsetstat@openssh.com` and a raw LSTAT of the exact, uncanonicalized path that keeps wire presence flags. Never use `GetAttributes`, `Get` or `RemoteStat` on this route, and refuse names that do not round-trip through SSH.NET's string API (U+FFFD after decoding). No private reflection. [Pinned SSH.NET attributes source](https://github.com/sshnet/SSH.NET/blob/7b2fd3dbf2c86a80a7b06cea020aa5f821c9902e/src/Renci.SshNet/Sftp/SftpFileAttributes.cs), [pinned SftpClient](https://github.com/sshnet/SSH.NET/blob/7b2fd3dbf2c86a80a7b06cea020aa5f821c9902e/src/Renci.SshNet/SftpClient.cs).

SFTP v3 attributes carry no object identity, and OpenSSH resolves every request by server path. OPEN calls `open(name, flags)` without `O_NOFOLLOW` or `O_NONBLOCK`: it needs read access, follows a final link and blocks the single-threaded server on a FIFO. FSETSTAT accepts only file handles, SETSTAT follows links, and `lsetstat@openssh.com` calls `fchmodat`/`fchownat` with `AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW` without opening anything. Exact-object binding through the write is therefore a protocol limit on stock servers, not a missing adapter. R1 ships **server-path-verified** changes with this stated residual and containment:

- Use the same verified authority and lease, and the reviewed path, never a REALPATH-canonicalized one.
- Require a fresh LSTAT tuple (type, mode, IDs, size, mtime) equal to the review before the first request and to FileCat's own last read before each later one, and compare it again after each.
- Send `lsetstat@openssh.com` only, one component per request, in §4's no-widening order. A group-only change sends UID 0xFFFFFFFF ("unchanged") where the server is qualified for it, otherwise the observed UID.
- Admit only servers that advertise `lsetstat@openssh.com` version 1 and identify as OpenSSH 8.0 or later: an `SSH-2.0-OpenSSH_` banner without `_for_Windows` plus the sftp-server extension list of the release the banner names (the banner names sshd, not the `Subsystem sftp` program). Windows OpenSSH advertises lsetstat, but its compatibility `fchmodat` opens the target for writing without no-follow, its chmod only toggles a read-only bit, and its list omits `fstatvfs@openssh.com`; it and unidentified servers stay inspection-only with that reason.
- Refuse unless every ancestor from `/` to the parent is an LSTAT-observed non-link directory owned by UID 0 or by the leaf's owner, with no group or other write (sticky is not exempt). Refuse links, UID-0 leaves and logins named `root`. Admit a leaf only when its owner owns the start directory, that directory is not owned by UID 0, and, where `users-groups-by-id@openssh.com` is offered, the owner maps to the login name; otherwise the item is inspection-only, because another leaf owner could hard-link a protected file into place on servers without protected hard links.

Review and results say the server identifies items by path. Residual, stated in the review and in R1's release claims: the account's own processes; root authority on the server, including an sftp-server wrapped by sudo or a UID-0 account with another name; and any account that a server ACL lets change an ancestor (NFSv4 ACLs on ZFS or NFS servers, Darwin ACLs on macOS) can still redirect a request, because SFTP shows only mode bits. Hold no handles. Reconnect never resumes a request. On a lost reply, retire the lease, verify the same authority, inspect again and label what can be observed; an unchanged re-read stays uncertain, because a blocked server process can still apply the change. A new approval is needed for any further delta.

On a qualified server this delivers group change within the account's groups and, where the server's no-follow chmod works (glibc 2.39 on Linux 6.6+ through `fchmodat2`, or `/proc` visible to sftp-server; glibc 2.31 and older, and chrooted sessions without `/proc` on older stacks, refuse it), file and folder mode, special bits and mode-000 repair. A FAILURE reply with an unchanged LSTAT reports "server could not apply this mode change" and disables mode changes for that authority for the session. Arbitrary owner changes need root authority, which this route refuses. On Ubuntu servers the user-private-group umask 002 makes user-created folders 0775, so items below them stay inspection-only; release claims say so.
