Apply any restrictive part of the mode change, then ownership, re-read mode, then only expressly requested permission/special bits; ownership-only changes must not restore cleared privilege-bearing bits, and the kernel may clear setgid. Verify between calls and reconcile every requested component after the final write, since a later change can invalidate an earlier verified result. OpenSSH's setters apply permissions before IDs within one request and can apply some attributes and still return an error. Do not start an SSH shell. [OpenSSH server implementation](https://github.com/openssh/openssh-portable/blob/master/sftp-server.c).

**FTP/FTPS.** Parse permission facts only from the fact list before the first space of the raw MLSD line (RFC 3659 §7.2), with octal validation, or record qualified LIST parser provenance: FluentFTP 55.0.0 (source `05c3066a1d5d08d4dba63062ce5142c762daaa5a`) types only a few facts and matches `unix.mode` as any digits, leaving `perm`, `unique` and owner/group facts in the raw line. `perm` describes allowed FTP actions; missing bits never imply 000. FluentFTP's `SetFilePermissions`/`Chmod` send `SITE CHMOD <mode> <path>`, which servers apply by path and which follows links; ProFTPD also rejoins its arguments with single spaces. SITE CHMOD has no no-follow or identity-checked form and ancestor modes are often unexposed, so R2 ships **no FTP permission setter in this release** and shows “server exposes mode; exact-item change unavailable”. This is a scope decision, not a protocol absolute: some servers expose identity facts (ProFTPD's MLSD `unique` is device/inode), but the command still re-resolves the path. Reopen only for a server that guarantees a no-follow or identity-checked setter. Keep certificate pin/validation, plain FTP's explicit consent and `FtpChannel`'s path and CR/LF checks for observations. Every FTPS data connection must present the certificate its session's control connection was accepted with; FluentFTP 55 itself accepts any data-connection certificate. Do not infer CHOWN or ACL support from SITE, or silently downgrade FTPS. [FTP MLST/MLSD facts](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3659.html), [FluentFTP server commands](https://github.com/robinrodricks/FluentFTP/wiki/FTP-Support), [ADR-17](../adr/ADR-17-sftp-engine.md).

## 6. Authorization, installation and architecture decisions

GUI stays unelevated. Ordinary authorized edits use the caller's rights. First inspect only requested sections. When baseline security is unreadable, offer **Inspect with authorization** for that subject/section; the helper returns observations and exits without any write authority. FileCat displays the actual diff. A distinct **Apply reviewed changes** request binds the displayed plan to the helper's own checked subject and security revision. If authority to read is still absent, disable the dependent write. Another administrator authenticating never becomes the selected owner/group. Native approval or cancellation returns to FileCat. System Settings approval for a daemon, TCC access or a server policy change may require leaving the app; report that prerequisite and re-inspect after the user returns. These cases do not count as completed in-app edits.

**Elevated FileCat processes and the legacy Unix action.** In a high-integrity Windows FileCat (any administrator-window launch, installed or portable) or a Unix FileCat with effective UID 0, the command and its Find/Details adapters are unavailable, with a reason pointing to the usual window, as mainline already does for EFS review and disk-space analysis. When a high-integrity token's elevation type is Default (UAC off, or the built-in Administrator), say instead that this sign-in has no standard-user token; CAPABILITIES and the release notes state that exclusion. Such a process acts with another token, may run user-writable code, has no per-plan consent and, on Windows, skips interrupted-job review. No `AdministratorTask`, administrator retry or administrator-window path accepts `JobKind.Permissions`; `AdministratorTask` validation keeps requiring `AttributeChangeSet.ChangesPermissions` to be false. Reconsider only through a G-PRIV subcase. `UnixPermissionApproval` is outside this feature's trust model. It runs the running executable as root, or `$APPIMAGE` whenever inherited `APPDIR`/`APPIMAGE` variables say so in any package; that is user-writable for AppImage, tar, development and user-installed app bundles, or simply chosen by same-user code that controls FileCat's environment. It uses an AppleScript administrator prompt on macOS. Neither OS prompt shows the plan to an alternate administrator. It opens the leaf as root before checking its identity, previews octal modes without POSIX ACL mask effects, has no timeout, records no job outcome, and does not refuse hard-linked aliases, which macOS lets any account create for files it can name (so an approved change can reach a root-owned helper). The canonical command and its jobs never call it, osascript or generic pkexec, and nothing from it counts toward G-LAUTH, G-MACAUTH or G-PKG. The release owner recorded retirement for all packages in §9. L3 and D3 introduce separate qualified authorization routes.

**Common envelope.** A versioned, closed schema admits inspect or apply, adapter, component mask and operation-specific native values validated against object kind, authority and scope. Include nonce, short expiry, requester binding, expected mount/object, native section digest and revision, manifest digest/count, normalized per-object deltas and propagation effects. Bound strings, buffers, cases, steps, elapsed time and handles. Reject extras, unknown versions/rights, repeated/expired requests, ambiguous paths, over-budget data or mismatched review hashes before calling a setter. No arbitrary descriptor replacement, attribute bit tunnel, executable, tool argument list or shell. The helper parses an immutable, bounded copy, independently opens/verifies the peer, object, package and payload, derives consent text from validated values in a fixed field order, escaping every untrusted value with the broker's `DisplayText` rules (no NUL, line, paragraph or bidirectional controls) and showing bytes or code units that do not decode as escapes (or refusing such names, as R1 does), and checks again just before apply. Packages with a privileged helper (W3, L3 and D3, each owning the change) build their installed clients with `StartupHookSupport=false`, and D3's signed app uses the hardened runtime without DYLD or library-validation exceptions. Bind results and cancellation to that same authenticated connection/request. Lose peer → stop before the next step; retain actual in-flight outcome for reconciliation. Cache neither a reusable mutation capability nor an inferred successful result. No administrator password entry in FileCat. Helpers refuse every change that is not strictly restrictive (any added right, any added set-ID or sticky bit, owner or group to a non-root principal, a removed deny, or cleared protection) on a non-directory whose link count exceeds 1 unless the requester already owns it, since another user's file can be hard-linked into the requester's folder; consent shows the link count and that other names are unknown. A Darwin client may open with `O_UNIQUE`, but the helper re-checks the link count on the received descriptor.

| Package/OS | Proposed route, trust and consent |
|---|---|
| Installed Windows x64/ARM64 | W3 extends ADR-14 with versioned `InspectFileSecurity` and `ChangeFileSecurity` messages (typed owner, DACL, audit and inheritance suboperations). Reuse `ElevationPlanCodec`, builder/runner, bootstrap, protected Program Files runtime, protected-path/owner/DACL install checks, protected single-use nonce, bounded plan/report exchange and helper-owned Cancel-default consent. Show canonical identities, old/new masks/owner, root/children effects; UAC only identifies the executable. For v2 require a mutually authenticated local-only pipe. Bind both OS-reported peer process IDs to retained process handles, creation time, token/logon session and installed image; mainline `ElevationBroker.NtfsMetadata.cs`/`ElevatedNtfsMetadataScope` already binds an installed requester this way and is the pattern to extend. The client matches the actual UAC-launched helper process. Bind digest, nonce, privileged read results and cancellation to that connection. The existing path/hash transports bounded bytes, and `CheckRequester` checking a supplied PID alone cannot prove authorship. Do not imply existing Authenticode verification. Enforce the audited bootstrap/path/ACL policy or introduce separately documented signing checks. Reopen with volume GUID + held-relative chain and verify against the user review or transfer an authenticated pinned handle with rights independently checked. Keep section privileges enabled only in the scoped helper operation. Admit a volume root only to these typed security verbs. Negative-test every unrelated root mutation. Local broker refuses UNC, mapped remote authority, unsafe install and mismatched/emulated ABI unless qualified. |
| Windows portable/self-contained, framework-dependent ZIP, dev | Ordinary user edits when the ABI/read-write gate passes. No privileged ACL route through portable or development code. ARM64 package build is precedent, not an editor/privilege pass. |
| Ubuntu installed .deb | L3 first chooses the mechanism toolchain (self-contained .NET under the root-owned `/opt/filecat`, extending `Recovery/Unix/DBus.cs` to send descriptors, or C), then adds a small root-owned, D-Bus-activated mechanism with its `dbus-1/system.d` policy, `system-services` activation and `polkit-1/actions` files shipped as dpkg-owned package files, and polkit actions for inspect and apply with an active graphical agent. Package binary, service policy and libraries under root-owned non-writable paths with a fixed environment. No dynamic loads, paths or runtime options from user app data. Authenticate the unique bus sender via bus credentials tied to the live connection/process, UID, session as polkitd derives it (GNOME starts apps outside the logind session) and trusted installed executable/runtime; bind polkit's decision to that sender and immutable digest. The mechanism generates the normalized consent model itself. The installed client's diff is non-binding, because same-user code can start the genuine client with `LD_PRELOAD` or a .NET startup hook and keep its identity. The binding consent is the polkit message. The installed action's message references mechanism-set details for requester, target, scope, owner/group, set-ID and grants as `$(key)` placeholders, which polkitd substitutes verbatim without rescanning; untrusted values never go into `polkit.message`, which polkitd rescans for `$(`. The message must show every privilege-bearing change, and a plan that cannot be shown there is refused. Check authorization with the authenticated sender's `system-bus-name` subject; the actions set `allow_any` and `allow_inactive` to `no` and `allow_active` to `auth_admin`. Never use a `unix-session` subject: polkitd aborts on it (polkit 124 asserts in its session lookup, 127 in its rules engine). Residual: same-user code can register a per-process agent for the client (`pkttyagent --process`) that receives the request and draws the window the administrator types into, or phish the password outright. Require fresh non-retained authorization per write; another administrator approves from that message alone; split oversized plans or disable that route if the graphical agent cannot display them. Use bus fd passing for actual pins; verify type, mount, namespace and security state server-side. Limit privilege lifetime to one request, stop on disconnect, time out idle service and handle package upgrades/uninstall. If a graphical agent is missing/refuses, return an in-app reason. Do not invoke pkexec, the existing census/device route or the legacy administrator action. |
| Ubuntu tar.gz/AppImage/dev | Ordinary data-owner edits on qualified x64; no root broker from a portable location or install-on-click. Installing the trusted .deb is the named elevated-edit prerequisite. linux-arm64 appears in build tooling but has no supported preview/AppImage lane here; any claimed ABI requires native evidence. |
| macOS installed, appropriately signed app | D3 adds a native `SMAppService.daemon(plistName:)` launch daemon with a plist in `Contents/Library/LaunchDaemons`, signed bundle-relative executable, root-controlled installation under Applications and a Mach/XPC service. Pin allowed client and service code requirements (bundle identifiers, Team ID/designated requirement, audit-token process identity), verify nested signatures/runtime and require an installed protected path. Authenticate each connecting peer and originating audit session; authorize discrete inspect/apply rights with Authorization Services, verify the right server-side against the authenticated request and one-use immutable plan. Restrict externalized authorization data to this connection/session, discard after use, validate every input again. Have the daemon derive the consent summary and call `AuthorizationCopyRights` itself, with interaction allowed and `kAuthorizationFlagDestroyRights` (otherwise authd stores the administrator's credential on the client-created ref, which the client could reuse, for example through `AuthorizationExecuteWithPrivileges`), without `kAuthorizationFlagPreAuthorize`, and with a prompt built from the plan (`kAuthorizationEnvironmentPrompt`). That system prompt is the binding consent; the client's presentation is non-binding, because a startup hook in the genuine signed client keeps its code identity. Refuse privilege-bearing changes the prompt cannot state. A generic/cached authorization alone never confirms a plan: register the inspect and apply rights at installation with `AuthorizationRightSet` (class user, group admin, shared false, timeout 0) and refuse a request if `AuthorizationRightGet` shows a different rule, because an unregistered right falls to the `default` rule, whose credential is shared for 300 s. A client-supplied ref can already hold the right: in least-privileged mode, which the ref's creator chooses, authd keeps a granted right credential without the rule's timeout. Before its interactive call the daemon therefore requests the right on the received ref without `kAuthorizationFlagExtendRights` or interaction, and refuses the request if that succeeds. Split or refuse plans the prompt cannot faithfully present. Installed clients follow the envelope's startup-hook and hardened-runtime rule. Acquire only by XPC transfer of descriptors the client obtained through a D1 descriptor route; by the parent-relative lane run as root, only where no non-root principal can change the parent and, for a directory entry, only one owned by root that grants no non-root principal add, write-security or change-owner rights; or by a daemon open relative to the client-passed, re-verified parent with `O_RDONLY \| O_NONBLOCK \| O_NOFOLLOW \| O_UNIQUE \| O_CLOEXEC` on a thread with dataless materialization off, admitting only a regular file or directory whose fsid, file ID and link count (`ATTR_FILE_LINKCOUNT`, or `ATTR_DIR_LINKCOUNT` for directories) match the review before any setter. `O_UNIQUE` refuses hard-linked objects before the open. Residual: a substituted single-link FIFO or character/block device may reach its open routine before post-open type rejection; these flags are not a type filter and non-blocking does not mean side-effect-free. G-MACAUTH must qualify containment for every special-node class this acquisition can reach on safe, authorized fixtures, or keep this daemon-open route disabled. Rejecting the descriptor after opening does not undo an open side effect. [XNU open path](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/main/bsd/vfs/vfs_vnops.c). Protected folders need Full Disk Access for the daemon, an external prerequisite. Anything else stays unavailable with its reason. Compare actual mount/fsid, UUID, vnode identity, ownership-enabled state, user/group interpretation and fresh ACL. A Linux peer-credential recipe cannot authenticate a Darwin client. |
| Current ad-hoc macOS bundle and dev builds | `eng/package-macos.sh` deep/ad-hoc signs the macOS 26+ arm64 app and separately signs its sandboxed archive worker. This does not establish a root broker. Keep qualified ordinary edits. Release decision DEC-03 in the [release blockers](../release/1.0.0/FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_BLOCKERS.md) (ad-hoc, non-notarized preview; Apple credentials not needed) currently precludes D3: a Service Management daemon and its Team-ID code requirements need an Apple-issued identity, hardened runtime with inside-out signing (not `--deep`), notarization and a root-installed distribution such as a signed installer package. **DEC-03 exit:** only the release owner (DEC-01) superseding DEC-03 with that identity and distribution unblocks D3. A development-signed qualification on the test Mac, run only with the owner's approval and an Apple Development identity (DEC-03 currently records Apple credentials as not needed), is evidence only; the ad-hoc package stays ordinary-only. Confirming macOS ordinary-only keeps D3 Blocked with that decision recorded. Until D3 ships, macOS release notes list the unmet operations (arbitrary chown, items of other users or root, system flags and volume ownership) and the cross-platform feature stays incomplete. Register with system Service Management, expose status/requiresApproval and System Settings instructions, recheck after approval. Unregister/stop old daemon for update/removal, reject protocol/version mismatch and verify signatures again after replacement. Test revoked/moved/user-writable app, helper downgrade, denied login-item approval and alternate admin. Do not inherit the archive sandbox as a privilege mechanism. |

On Linux confirm caller and mechanism have compatible user and mount namespaces, mount IDs/instances, idmap and fsuid/fsgid interpretation before any write. Use the mount carried by the passed fd, explicit ID domains and once-only mapping, and reject unverifiable namespace equivalence. Separate an ordinary permitted chgrp from arbitrary chown (`CAP_CHOWN` as applicable) and check kernel group membership. `fchownat` with `AT_EMPTY_PATH` on the pin needs no authority beyond chown(2)'s own rules (the extra capability for empty paths applies to `linkat`), so owner/group edits need no procfs alternative. A directory fd or matching dev/inode pair in another mount view gives insufficient evidence. On Darwin qualify fd passing, mount ownership policy and audit credentials independently under actual installed signing. Helpers cannot waive TCC, SIP, read-only/sealed volumes or server policy. [polkit mechanism/subjects](https://www.freedesktop.org/software/polkit/docs/latest/polkit.8.html), [pkexec agents and arguments](https://www.freedesktop.org/software/polkit/docs/latest/pkexec.1.html), [Apple Service Management migration](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/servicemanagement/updating-helper-executables-from-earlier-versions-of-macos), [Apple peer code requirements](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/technotes/tn3127-inside-code-signing-requirements), [Apple authorization](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/Security/Conceptual/AuthenticationAndAuthorizationGuide/Authorization/Authorization.html).

**L3 toolchain.** Use a native C mechanism with libsystemd sd-bus. It receives D-Bus UNIX_FD values and duplicates each admitted descriptor with close-on-exec for the lifetime of the reviewed request. This keeps the root service independent of the managed runtime and uses sd-bus for message decoding. The Ubuntu 24.04 fixture (`dbus 1.14.10-4ubuntu4.1`, `libsystemd 255.4-1ubuntu8.18`, `polkitd 124-2ubuntu1.24.04.4`) returned `ProcessID`, `UnixUserID`, `UnixGroupIDs`, and `LinuxSecurityLabel` for a live ordinary client. It omitted `ProcessFD`. Stable peer-process binding on that bus is an implementation and qualification item because the sender's numeric PID can be reused. [sd-bus descriptor ownership](https://www.freedesktop.org/software/systemd/man/255/sd_bus_message_read.html), [D-Bus connection credentials](https://dbus.freedesktop.org/doc/dbus-specification.html#bus-messages-get-connection-credentials)

Amend [ADR-12](../adr/ADR-12-platform-adapters-and-packaging.md) for these explicit platform services and qualified installed Unix packages; amend [ADR-14](../adr/ADR-14-privileged-broker.md) for per-plan security inspect/apply, pre-write inspection, exact root exception, cross-platform authorization and the editor's refusal inside administrator windows. Retain [ADR-06](../adr/ADR-06-worker-isolation-shell-host.md) isolation for Shell extensions, [ADR-16](../adr/ADR-16-keyboard-model.md) command reachability and ADR-17 transport trust. Amend ADR-01/03 capability and dispatch examples, ADR-04 journal record kinds and ADR-10 invalidation as the packages land. A new signing arrangement is a release dependency; this plan grants no signing or deployment approval.

## 7. Execution, outcomes and interactions

Use the existing job lifecycle. Register the reviewed request with the provider executor, obtain per-device/authority admission, flush an intent before the native call and attach observed outcomes afterwards. Keep the progress label at the actual action/path. Count examined, changed, matching, skipped, failed, unattempted and uncertain with explicit denominators only for captured entries. Record conflict separately. Track partial component success separately from a fully changed item and avoid double counting terminal item categories. An inaccessible subtree has an unknown number of children. Opaque propagation reports one call and its observed root, with no fabricated child progress, totals, bytes or successful completion for unseen children.

Before reporting a compound request fully applied, reconcile all requested components and preservation/side-effect dependencies after the last write. Earlier component checks are historical evidence, not proof of the final state. A later invalidation, mismatch or unavailable final read remains partial/uncertain as appropriate, even when every individual call returned success.

| After the call | Result shown and next action |
|---|---|
| Requested values and required preservation verified | Committed, including named native normalization or reviewed side effects. A previously matching object with no requested descendant work is matching. |
| Changed object, authority, mount, security baseline or ambiguous rule before mutation | Conflict, no write to that object. Re-read and review the current diff. |
| Rejection demonstrably before any mutation | Failed-before-change or skipped with cause. A cancellation before dispatch leaves it unattempted. |
| Some components observed committed before an error, mismatch or stop | Partial with individual component evidence (including observed unintended change). Stop dependent steps. A negative syscall/protocol reply alone cannot prove a prior state. |
| Apparent success with denied/missing/mismatched read-back, broken transport, crash or unreported propagation | Preserve evidence of submission and known effects. Report uncertain for unproved effects; independently verified components stay verified. Do not convert missing verification to unchanged. |

Hold any rights needed for verification across potential self-lockout when the OS permits, independently from mutation privileges, and disclose read-back context (including broker vs original user). After cancellation stop new calls; a noninterruptible call can still complete. No automatic rollback, replay, resume of a manifest, or automatic undo. A saved descriptor cannot establish identity, authority, concurrent edits or propagation reversibility. **Review current state** from history builds a fresh diff for a deliberate corrective job. Do not offer bulk descriptor export/import in this release.

Extend `JobJournal`/recovery with small versioned security intents and outcomes on the existing append-only `.fcj` channel. Record request ID, operation/component mask, bounded target identity, reviewed/observed digests, selected changes, outcome/counts and limited diagnostic samples. Never store full descriptors, SACL contents, credentials or polkit/XPC tokens in ordinary logs. Use caller-private permissions/ACL, reject links/untrusted inherited access, redact account and server details in copied summaries by default. Retain private samples up to 64 items/1 MiB per job, plus bounded pending intent/outcome data sufficient to detect uncertain calls. Use segmented/budgeted jobs if durability cannot be guaranteed within limits; refuse before the next write rather than lose the pending intent. Flush before calls, tolerate torn tail, and inherit 200 finished files/30-day pruning with bounded interrupted-job review. Raw before/after data is short-lived review memory. This requires no compression history database or restoration engine. [ADR-04](../adr/ADR-04-operation-journal.md).

Invalidate owner/group/permission/flag columns, matching details windows, principal/access snapshots and provider/Find caches by affected identity and mount/policy generation. Refresh after uncertain effects as well. Content mtime is insufficient. For panel-origin jobs preserve focus, retaining failed/unattempted marks and clearing only explicitly submitted completed roots in the same still-valid selection context. For detached/report work pass no panel-origin unmark callback; bypass the generic `MainViewModel.Operations` completed-source unmark route. Refresh without touching unrelated marks/focus. For unknown propagation scope invalidate affected mount/subtree lazily, without implying a recursive re-read succeeded.

Keep ACL-preserving copy, archive staging/rebuild, external editing and hex save semantics in their existing executors. In-place editing retains inode security subject to native effects; replacement may inherit a different ACL/owner. Recheck security before publishing when those operations rely on reviewed metadata, and invalidate stale permission reviews upon replacement. No changes to all transfer inheritance policies. Keep ordinary Read-only, Finder Hidden, timestamps, storage compression/EFS, generic xattrs and share configuration independent. Do not edit SELinux/AppArmor policy, grant file capabilities, modify machine audit policy or change arbitrary mount options. Only the named permission-affecting flag and macOS volume ownership actions fall within this extension.

## 8. Ordered work packages

Execute by dependencies. P0 reaches the first editing milestone, P1 completes local platforms, P2 qualifies provider/package coverage. All packages except D3 (Blocked) start Not started at this planning baseline. Track two things per package: append the implementation state to its §8 ID cell; its acceptance column lists its routes, and §9 records their subcases. **Implementation state** is Not started, In progress, Implemented or Blocked (awaiting an owner decision). Implemented means code and focused automated checks exist; it is not releasable, and it never moves back because a subcase fails, waits or reopens. **Qualification** is tracked per route in the acceptance column; every route listed there is essential unless the row marks it optional. A package is Qualified when every essential route's subcases have passed and only passed routes are enabled. An optional route may close with a recorded failure and stay off. A failed or Prerequisite essential subcase leaves the package unqualified, with the missing fix or resource named, to revisit when that changes. A package is eligible when its dependencies are Implemented, their runnable subcases on the lanes it uses have run, and it has runnable work left; a Blocked package is eligible only for work its decision allows. Q1 is an exit criterion of every shipping package, not a separate state. A package that owns a setter but not its authority implements it behind a disabled capability and owns the observation, denial and preservation subcases; the authority package (W3, L3, D3) owns the authorized subcases and enables the setter. Unrelated open subcases of a shared gate neither block independent implementation nor reopen an accepted slice; apply §9's reopening rule.

## 9. Acceptance ledger, risks and decisions

One ledger for this feature. **Open** means unrun for the new workflow. **Prerequisite** means a named external resource or approval is required. Existing receipts and test sources are reuse points, never new passes. Mark subcases by OS, ABI, package, backend and operation as evidence arrives. Record commit, versions, authority, exact invocation, observed effect, skips and artifact beside the row. Do not convert a skipped native lane into a pass. Run future writes only on authorized, owned temporary trees or disposable volumes with independent cleanup access; never use home, system drives or a project checkout. Test root changes on disposable mounted volumes, including failed ones. **Blocked** names an owner decision. Tags name the route class, (read), (ordinary) or (authorized), and may name the owning package, as in (ordinary, D2). (read) subcases are a gate's subcases that perform no write, whatever other tag they carry; every package's reader ships under I1's rule. A subcase belongs to the package that implements the route it exercises; the ledger records that owner when work starts, and no subcase is dropped.

**Reopening is per subcase.** Every run records its OS build and runtime, and any code change reruns the automated suites. A native, screen-reader or package subcase reopens only on its row's trigger, when its lane moves to a new OS feature release, kernel series, libc or .NET minor version, or SDK pin, or after a failed run. Additive work (a new adapter, principal kind, binding family, result kind, package row or helper) adds its own subcases. Reopening removes the affected route's release claim until the subcase passes again. When the reopening came from a change to the route's own code, a later build enables the route only after that pass; an OS or runtime move alone leaves shipped behavior unchanged. Other passed subcases stay passed. G-UX subcases are headless (run once in U1, then regression), per OS for each new editor or consent surface (screen reader, native rendering, high contrast), or per route (entry, authorization and result journey).

**Owner decisions, 20 November 2026.** (1) Keep the ad-hoc macOS preview. DEC-03 remains in force and D3 is Blocked. (2) Retire the legacy Unix administrator action. Retire the legacy mode writer for Unix subject classes the new editor cannot bind, in both ordinary and root FileCat. Independent timestamp and Finder Hidden changes remain in scope. (3) Approval of a development-signed D3 run (§6) remains absent.

**Execution baseline, 20 November 2026.** Branch `codex/permissions-acl` starts from `b0a2171e355591a77821200afb69384fa88a4314`, verified against GitHub `main`. The original checkout has unrelated tracked and untracked changes. The implementation branch contains a separate copy of this plan. SDK 10.0.101 and the checked-out dependency pins govern the first runs.

The Linux inode-protection source archive in `.tmp/acl-validation/l2-flags.tgz` has SHA-256 `fd983dde6f8f375aef25aa271de02a8654b1976c336223e19fbf195681aa4395`. Receipts in the same directory are `ubuntu-l2-flags-ordinary-core.trx` (`64600b5c0d1dd17bc96918e9bcfef2bf18501ef9535d376ad10fa0464ff38206`), `ubuntu-l2-flags-ordinary-app.trx` (`afc4605fba6326c9b35814e65c2678a291a6ae508ca0b83d726b0acc0fcfbda3`), `ubuntu-l2-flags-cap-core.trx` (`b8ff95b18b041e9bddc2ee87f9e1528e25687dc0259545ea020401a4fcdad7af`), and `mac-l2-flags-app-regression.trx` (`5973c4b21a30e4685ed63e41601e4d932a16569186bb3fdb4184c81c6ac4f8fc`). The capability-scoped App rerun used the same production sources and the test-only inspector wait fix (test source SHA-256 `3ba251d2eca05b4de86bc036e50f8da9d05ceb44db5222bfca577d23995969b6`). Its receipt `ubuntu-l2-flags-cap-app.trx` has SHA-256 `230dff441db4b27d447c810111590466b660e96a619ca90a254bb9b7831baa37`.

An Ubuntu local Unix socket probe corroborated the accepted peer UID and GID and obtained its process descriptor using `SO_PEERPIDFD`. Polling the held descriptor detected the peer's exit. The probe used a private socket and removed it afterward. Binding this proof to a live system-bus request and its consent digest is still open under G-LAUTH. The receipt is `.tmp/acl-validation/ubuntu-l3-peer-pidfd-probe.log`. [Linux 6.8 socket UAPI](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/include/uapi/asm-generic/socket.h)

| Package and subcase | Execution evidence, 20–21 November 2026 | Qualification still required |
|---|---|---|
| W3 G-PRIV/G-AUDIT/G-UX/G-BIND/G-RESULT (installed broker through real UAC, mapped approver) | On 21 November, the isolated Windows 11 Pro 26200 x64 guest installed the unsigned candidate built in the guest from `a3e5f64d0`, then from `f83255229` (source archive SHA-256 `515d3292f9aa883f24e2d40241d7e053f36f834380cbdcfdcf654f8b0ca02321`). The installed FileCat ran as the medium-integrity administrator `aclw2` with `FILECAT_QUALIFY_WINDOWS_PERMISSIONS_BROKER=1`. A test-only LocalSystem worker answered the real UAC prompt on the secure desktop ("unknown publisher", FileCat.PrivilegedHost.exe) and the helper's Cancel-default task dialog, recording each prompt's text first. The helper consent named the requester SID, the exact volume-GUID path, the requested sections or the typed before/after rule and the hard-link count. Results: authorized inspection of a file whose DACL denies the requester `READ_CONTROL` showed owner, group, the deny ACE and protection with the file unchanged; UAC decline and helper-consent cancel left the descriptor unchanged; removing the deny ACE was verified and independent SDDL showed only that ACE removed, with owner, group, protection, control `0xBC04` and content unchanged; an owner change from Administrators to the requester was verified; an added read right on a hard-linked file owned by another account was refused before approval; an elevated FileCat refused the command with its usual-window message. The installed native section probe passed the denied DACL read, the complete audit read with its integrity label, omission of unrequested sections, file-ID mismatch refusal and no leaked thread token. The first audit addition on a labelled file wrote the correct SACL but reported uncertain, because Windows stores label and policy ACEs after audit ACEs on a SACL write while the projection had appended the new entry after the label. Commit `f83255229` inserts new audit entries before inherited, label and policy entries and refuses SACLs whose policy entries precede audit entries; the rerun reported Verified changed with `(AU;SAFA;SD;;;BU)` added and the label, original audit ACE and control preserved. Receipts are under `.tmp/acl-validation/w3-journeys/windows-receipts/` (`journey-*.json`, `journeys-summary.json`, `native-read.json`, captures). A later round on the same guest installed candidates from `fed82a7b0` and `a42e6b792` (source archive SHA-256 `cb8ab3c2d5f6a23e6e4c54b268ae4517fd38ab552cccb5c0aace53d2d6a08360`). The ordinary opens requested `SYNCHRONIZE`, which NTFS grants only through the DACL, so another account's file with no entry for the requester could not be bound, and an owner who had locked themselves out saw the DACL as denied. Commit `fed82a7b0` retries a denied ordinary open without `SYNCHRONIZE`, using the parent-granted `FILE_READ_ATTRIBUTES` and the owner's implicit `READ_CONTROL` and `WRITE_DAC`. Commit `a42e6b792` clears a regular file's sync-root check through its screened parent when the attributes-only handle cannot be queried. In the guest, the ordinary-token native class passed 24 cases with 5 fixture skips, including a new owner-lockout inspection and verified repair. The installed app then offered and completed authorized inspection of a file with no entry for the requester. With Administrators denied `WRITE_DAC` on a SYSTEM-owned file, the helper changed the DACL under restore privilege, kept the owner and verified the result, and its consent said it overrides the file's discretionary permissions. Killing the requester while the helper's change consent was open left the file unchanged, and the next launch listed the interrupted permissions operation for review. With the standard user `AclW3Standard` signed in, UAC asked for administrator credentials; the worker entered the administrator's password. The helper consent named the standard requester and, for the owner change, the approving administrator's SID. The authorized inspection succeeded; the owner change from Administrators to the requester used restore privilege and was verified, with group, control `0xBC04`, DACL and content unchanged; and an audit addition on the labelled file was verified with the label kept. Additional receipts are `.tmp/acl-validation/w3-journeys/receipts.sha256` and `.tmp/acl-validation/w3-lockout/receipts.sha256` (native result `b3a3621e815faa21b3d9781241b00f033231014babb885b594c6ffa0edeab732`). On 22 November the same guest upgraded the installed candidate in place, without uninstalling, from 1.0.0 (`a42e6b792`) to 1.0.1 (`d859dc6d7`), 1.0.2 (`a7514e949`) and 1.0.3 (`24f6b20a8`). Each upgrade replaced the helper with the published binary, kept a single uninstall entry, the protected install-root ACL and disabled startup hooks, and the guest-built helper-protocol tests passed (30 of 30) after each one. Commit `d859dc6d7` makes the helper check the approving token's privileges before it holds the target or asks for consent. With the Administrators group stripped of backup and restore rights, `aclw2` approved a standard requester's inspection through the credential prompt: every section said that the approving administrator's token lacks the backup privilege, and no consent appeared. With backup but no restore, the requester's DACL change failed before change without a change consent. Both descriptors were unchanged, and the stock rights were restored. The round found two defects. Windows attaches the Cloud Files filter on demand (only `C:` on the host, only the manually attached `R:` in the guest). On other volumes `CfGetSyncRootInfoByHandle` answers `ERROR_INVALID_FUNCTION`, and the inspector refused every item there because sync-root membership could not be ruled out. Commit `a7514e949` treats a repeated `ERROR_INVALID_FUNCTION` on the same handle as outside every sync root. Format leaves volume roots without `SE_DACL_AUTO_INHERITED` (control `0x8004` on every guest fixture and both host data volumes), and the root route admitted only auto-inherited roots. Commit `24f6b20a8` admits both forms and requests auto-inheritance only where the root had it. Under the ordinary token the native class passed 26 cases with 4 ReFS and SMB fixture skips, including a new case on a volume without the filter and the exact root inspection; the native root-write test passed protected and unprotected roots in both forms. Commit `26bff35fb` compares the existing children as stored, because `GetSecurityInfo` infers the inherited entries of a descriptor without `SE_DACL_AUTO_INHERITED` from its parent's current DACL. Through the installed broker the requester opened a freshly formatted root from its background menu, inspected it with authorization and added a read rule for INTERACTIVE. FileCat verified the change; an independent raw read showed the eight original entries in order, the new entry at the end of the allow block and control `0x8004`. Swapping the volume mounted at that letter between review and Apply was refused with "The original selection or workspace changed", and neither volume changed. Authorized inspection of a requester-owned folder holding another account's hard-linked file offered no change, owner or audit action: the helper has no folder or propagation route. FileCat run as the built-in Administrator refused the command with "This sign-in has no standard-user token". A medium-integrity topmost window covered the helper's inspection consent text with its own while the consent buttons stayed visible; `TDM_CLICK_BUTTON`, a posted Enter and `SetWindowPos` from that process all failed with error 5, and the consent stayed open until cancelled. This residual is recorded: the consent does not use a secure desktop. Receipts are listed in `.tmp/acl-validation/w3-journeys/receipts-round3.sha256` (`bad3d4b62f631d5ea75a1e4292f9a57dcf47d90868b1f68f6ac2c15ff9e8ccb5`). The guest's added volumes, folders and built-in Administrator account were removed or disabled. | Prerequisite: Authenticode signing of the installed helper and client through an owner-approved signing run, and the ARM64 lane, which needs an ARM64 Windows device with an interactive UAC desktop (hosted ARM64 runners refuse interactive input). Keep the route opt-in until both pass. |
| L3 G-LAUTH/G-UX/G-RESULT/G-PKG (GNOME graphical approval and dismissal) | On 21 November, Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 (kernel `7.0.0-43-generic`, polkit 124, GNOME Wayland on seat0) installed `1.0.0~dev.permissions.4` built from `f83255229` (`.deb` SHA-256 `17a55c5a6845e4a334359da160abd96891e1db0d94c3a3a49db7dd4e495e51c2`, tarball `a06aa01ed18504f77e554014c4230a726ef8b5e2c73d654ea784a4bfe5e32788`, AppImage `229dd10a5057eaad767eeb524aa6231f96f7c17ca2066ef0505db4b33ad3beae`). The earlier blank console captures came from a locked session; after an unlock, host captures show the GNOME dialog. FileCat ran as UID 1000 in a user-manager unit outside the logind session and reached GNOME Shell's agent. A root-run `/dev/uinput` test keyboard typed into the real dialog. Root-owned witnesses on a private ext4 image: inspection approved and dismissed; mode 0640 → 0600 verified on the same inode, owner and mtime; a dismissed apply left 0640 and reported one skipped item. The dialog showed the broker-built message with requester UID, exact target, single-file scope and the mode change; `line\u000Afeed.txt` appeared escaped and `$(id).txt` literally. The U+202E target appeared as `‮gnp.txt` in the message. FileCat launched from the Activities overview ran in `app-gnome-filecat-*.scope` outside any logind session, and its request reached the GNOME agent and was approved. GNOME 46 offers no account choice when the requester is an administrator, so a disposable non-admin `fcuser` (UID 1002) was signed in through GDM autologin. Its requests were approved by `benny` (UID 1000) typing that account's password: mode 0640 → 0600 verified, and an owner change 0 → 1002 on a 04640 file verified with mode 0640. The apply message listed the owner change, set-user-ID clearance and the warning that the change can grant access or change authority, and FileCat required explicit acceptance of the clearance first. The temporary users, sudoers rule, GDM setting, package and fixture were removed. On 22 November the same guest built `1.0.0~dev.permissions.5` and `.6` from `d859dc6d7`, `.7` from `24f6b20a8` and `.8` from `e84095735`. Approved through the GNOME dialog by `benny`: a group change from root:root to root:adm was verified on the same inode with the mode unchanged; the immutable flag was set and `lsattr` confirmed it; a named-user ACL entry was added and `getfacl` confirmed it. Adding read for others to a root-owned file with a second name failed before change without a polkit request. FileCat started as UID 1000 in a private mount namespace was refused before polkit; from `.7` the alert explains that the service sees a different mount, where it had shown the raw `EXDEV` text. A test-only `LD_PRELOAD` library inside the genuine installed client sent its own Inspect and then an Apply for mode 0666 that FileCat never reviewed; the GNOME message showed "Mode 0640 → 0666" with the warning that the change can grant access, and dismissal left the file at 0640. While the broker waited on the GNOME dialog, upgrading to `.6` stopped it and closed the dialog, and a fresh request then used the new broker. Removing the package with a pending dialog stopped the broker and removed its unit, D-Bus activation, polkit action and enable marker. A same-user `pkttyagent --process` registered for FileCat received the inspection request with the broker's message and asked for the approver's password while GNOME's dialog never appeared: the per-process-agent residual, demonstrated. On the Ubuntu 26.04.1 snapshot (kernel `7.0.0-38-generic`, glibc 2.43, polkit 127, GNOME Shell 50.1, systemd 259) `.7` inspected and dismissed through the GNOME dialog, but every change failed before polkit: the 26.04 user manager gives each desktop process `CAP_WAKE_ALARM` in the inheritable set, and FileCat's process check refused the permissions journal (and disk analysis and the archive worker). Commit `e84095735` accepts an inheritable-only set; with `.8` the 26.04 desktop verified mode 0640 → 0600 and a named ACL entry, and refused the hard-link change before polkit. Receipts are listed in `.tmp/acl-validation/l3-gnome/receipts-round3.sha256` (`e1d5bc04cd7f80ae09632822c74d10f889e0ede9d6c52ce5efd63d522d9b4f4b`). The 24.04 guest was purged and returned to its prior state; the 26.04 snapshot run was discarded. | No essential L3 subcase is open. Release enablement follows the G-PKG row. | N1/W2/D1/L1 G-MOUNT/G-WIN/G-MAC/G-LINUX/G-BIND/G-RESULT (device loss on all three systems, removable NTFS) | On 22 November, a 512 MiB NTFS data disk was hot-added to the running Hyper-V Windows 11 Pro x64 guest (build 26200, SDK 10.0.101, runtime 10.0.1). As the ordinary medium-integrity user `aclw2`, a real reviewed DACL change on a document there flushed its call intent to the protected journal on the system volume. The host then flushed the data volume's cache and hot-removed the disk. The recheck on the held handle failed, so the object reported `FailedBeforeChange` with no native call or error, and the journal held the intent and its outcome as complete history that needs no recovery review. After the disk was reattached, the document's stored DACL equalled the one recorded before the job. An earlier run without the cache flush lost the document's creation with the device. The component of that result carried no detail; a recheck that fails after the intent now gives the same reason as a failed first check (`b750edd1a`, fixture test). On the workstation, the opt-in removable reader (`FILECAT_QUALIFY_WINDOWS_REMOVABLE_PERMISSIONS`, `eb85b7b83`) read a task-owned file on the designated USB test stick (NTFS, removable media) exactly as stored, while the default reader refused the volume. Edits, reviewed changes and the administrator route refused the removable binding as inspection-only. The test removed its folder from the stick. Receipts `.tmp/acl-validation/hotremove/receipts/windows-hotremove.xml` (`438ca57a20473ea305d5fa9b5db565a0014ff84d0a0310029a7c584639c5440e`), `reattach.txt` (`5163bdf5a22f0ddcea0377a603557e5fa7743baa1d7c6085b42104deaa09310b`) and `.tmp/acl-validation/removable/windows-removable-g.xml` (`872e20915791393e32669a5fcb2e405b5b14e62b04bb272f6b534a2f691ea428`). On macOS 27.2.1 arm64 (`26B131`, SDK 10.0.101, UID 501), a test-owned APFS image holding the reviewed document was force-detached with `hdiutil detach -force` once the parent-relative mode change's intent was durable. The recheck failed, the object reported a failure with a reason and no native call, the journal on the test volume held complete history, and the reattached image kept mode 0640. Receipt `.tmp/acl-validation/darwin-device-loss/mac-device-loss-core.trx` (18 passed, 0 skipped, `748d67b2189c6acf460d680e23c457258c0e725d525713d21c71468bb9045828`) also covers the fixture recheck reason and the macOS kill boundaries. On Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 (kernel `7.0.0-43-generic`, SDK 10.0.101, UID 1000), an ext4 volume on a device-mapper linear target was switched to an error target once a mode change's intent was durable. The kernel still accepted the held-descriptor `fchmodat2` and answered the read-back from memory, so the job used to report such a lost change as verified. The writer now asks the object's own filesystem to write its pending changes before it confirms a change (`bb105b004`): `syncfs` failed with EIO, the result was `Uncertain` with that reason, and after the mapping was restored the mode on disk was still 0640. All 49 executed Core permission tests passed with the flush. Receipt `.tmp/acl-validation/linux-device-loss/ubuntu-device-loss-core.trx` (`80583d34c7af2d7c23edc8ec0c4fd2237525dca6040225d40d907580410934bb`) and `result.txt` (`53b6b4ea31572bb32d16f20bbc259e14962e51d707d83a4426660a7b1ad808c2`). | The hot-removed Windows disk is a fixed SCSI volume, and the macOS case is a forced detach rather than a physical removal. Physical removal of removable media during a job and removable writes and their verification remain open. The Windows and macOS writers now confirm durability as G-RESULT records. |

The unprotected cases matched the projected DACL. The protected cases placed the newly added allow ACE ahead of the existing explicit allow ACE. All cases preserved the selected folder's owner and group and the observed discretionary descriptors of the existing nested directory and the file held open under normal sharing. | W2 G-WIN/G-TREE/G-BIND/G-UX (DACL protection and parent merge) | The Windows folder editor previews the rules affected by protecting a DACL, removing inherited entries, or enabling inheritance. Copy retains native order and converts inherited rules to explicit rules. Removal lists each lost occurrence. Enable uses Windows to derive inheritance in memory from a readable exact parent DACL, compares the explicit entries byte for byte in order, and rechecks the parent's identity and revision before dispatch. Windows 11 Pro x64 build 26200 with .NET 10.0.1 verified all three conversions under the ordinary desktop token on disposable fixed NTFS. Copy and removal changed control `8404` to `9404`, and enable changed `9404` to `8404`. The tests compared the complete root DACL, owner and group, confirmed inheritance on an eligible child and preserved a protected child. A diagnostic showed that NTFS merged two identical explicit rules after a copy. FileCat now refuses conversion when multiple resulting explicit rules share the same principal, ACE type and flags. The rerun confirmed refusal before dispatch and retained the original descriptor. It also refused a changed or unreadable parent. The App exercised the copy and enable previews, separate propagation consent and verified root results. The final run passed 43 platform and 17 App cases, with zero failures and 4 and 54 fixture or OS skips, respectively. The independent unread audit and owner witnesses passed, and both audit receipts had SHA-256 `4232e5c046d2323574b948ababf867a202fe31c9b1791fd365c6602d293f445e`. Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 built the final overlay with zero errors and warnings. The maintainer scripts clear the override and marker and reload systemd before stopping the old instance. `FILECAT_LINUX_PERMISSIONS_QUALIFICATION=1 bash eng/package-linux.sh 1.0.0-dev.permissions.2 linux-x64` built the corrected artifacts from isolated source snapshot `7de6c5ce36e7df9a7afc9c074889520dc3494b03` (source archive SHA-256 `240454b9bd6dff8b9740a290b2a14f8a5aa01dffe18b1c0255ceeb8cc934dd09`). A UID 1000 requester activated the exact dpkg-installed root broker. Live polkit loaded both actions. Unpack stopped the broker and immediately rejected reactivation. Configure let the first new request activate a fresh process. Removal stopped it and withdrew the bus service. With the exact .deb installed, the Xvfb/AT-SPI FileCat panel and command palette opened the permissions inspector and offered authorization. The actual tarball and AppImage opened the ordinary inspector in the same environment and omitted authorization. The independent witness stayed at mode `0640`, UID/GID 1000. The original deinstalled package record, empty list, and postrm bytes were restored. Receipts in `.tmp/acl-validation/` are `ubuntu-l3-live-systemd.log` (`e834be8016dc35d124ab4868df3de8c7e3a94bfc03d1378afbdb77d99b487b3c`) and `ubuntu-l3-live-systemd-fixed.log` (`a87937666501d779a099df7e8b7d462b55a453ef9a8c024b321c724e06ba7449`). All three tested artifacts and GUI results are in `ubuntu-l3-live-artifacts/`, with their artifact hashes in `SHA256SUMS`. Exercise allowed and denied outcomes, agent cancellation and a second administrator. The package stays development opt-in pending the remaining L3 gates. |

| L3 G-LAUTH/G-UX/G-RESULT (authorized editor and durable job) | The ordinary permissions inspector offers a separate authorized read when the installed Linux broker is available and FileCat can pin a local ext4 object. The authorized view uses the returned owner, group, mode, ACL, protection and capability evidence. The editor reviews one typed change and submits it through the permissions job, which records its intent before applying. The client corroborates the held statx state before dispatch and after native read-back. Polkit dismissal is unattempted and a lost apply response stays uncertain. Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64, kernel `7.0.0-43-generic`, SDK `10.0.101` and .NET `10.0.14`. Four headless FileCat journeys on a dedicated ext4 image passed. They verified a mode edit, numeric ownership transfer with explicit acceptance of set-ID clearance, discarding an unsent draft, and polkit dismissal with no write. The native test broker used a private system-style bus with simulated authorization decisions. Thirteen ordinary inspector regressions passed with 33 platform or fixture skips. The image, private bus and Ubuntu build were removed. Source bundle `l3-editor.tgz` SHA-256 `6e57e5d8c27c2ba3bf6f2e632457d8ebfff4c80bc3ceb5c86a203951d73c49b8`. | L3 G-LAUTH/G-BIND/G-RESULT (managed D-Bus client) | The managed client consumes an ordinary ext4 inspection lease, duplicates the original descriptor and corroborates the unique mount ID. It checks the broker's unique name and UID on the system bus, echoes the descriptor challenge, sends typed Inspect and Apply calls with bounded payloads, and validates the returned identity, section mask and revision. An inspect cancellation releases the blocked read. Losing the connection after submitting Apply yields an uncertain result, and a fresh inspection is required. Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64, kernel `7.0.0-43-generic`, .NET `10.0.14`, SDK `10.0.101`. Four managed-to-native private-bus checks passed, including a verified mode change, an exact named ACL round-trip, stale review refusal and both cancellation branches. The live native test fixture used a root bus owner and pinned the managed test executable. It observed cancellation of both pending prompts. The Unix device suite passed 23 checks with two fixture-only skips. On macOS 27.2.1 arm64 with .NET `10.0.12`, the descriptor transfer and D-Bus framing checks passed. Both stages were removed. | L3 G-LAUTH/G-PKG (D-Bus request service and activation) | The native service accepts versioned Begin, Inspect, Apply and Cancel methods. It binds the challenge token to the unique bus connection, requires the pinned installed executable and runtime in production, verifies the transferred descriptor and native revision, and returns selected observations or a typed write outcome. One request per connection and eight in flight bound the service. A timeout, process exit, challenge-channel closure or bus departure cancels its pending prompt. | L3 G-LAUTH/G-BIND/G-RESULT (authorized inspection and single typed dispatch) | The polkit request consumes approval before the selected metadata read or one typed write. It checks requester liveness before dispatch and preserves unrelated inode flags. The mechanism rereads disputed syscall results and distinguishes a failed write with unchanged state from an attempted write it cannot verify. Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64, ext4, kernel `7.0.0-43-generic`. Twenty-seven checks passed with strict compilation and repeated under ASan/UBSan. Nine ordinary and five root-scoped checks covered access/default ACL set and removal, mode-000 repair, the hard-link guard, capability and set-ID clearing, immutable and append-only preservation, disconnect, and uncertain read-back. The bounded prompt includes the exact target, hard-link scope, owner and group changes, mode, named ACL and mask changes, and predicted privilege effects. The mechanism escapes controls, bidirectional formatting and invalid byte sequences. It refuses oversized edits. | L3 G-LAUTH/G-BIND/G-FLAG (native security evidence and revision) | The mechanism reads only requested security sections. A dedicated thread isolates its cwd, verifies the procfs alias against the held subject before and after reading, and reads the access and default POSIX ACLs and `security.capability` by fixed names. The decoder retains native bytes and distinguishes absence, unsupported or denied reads, opaque layouts and malformed evidence. It verifies that the access ACL agrees with the bound mode. Inode protection can be observed from statx when a readable descriptor is unavailable. Edits that depend on the full native flag word require that word to be known. The guard rebuilds a versioned SHA-256 revision with libcrypto.so.3 and checks it before rechecking the subject's write preconditions. Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64, kernel `7.0.0-43-generic`. | L3 G-LAUTH (peer and executable verification) | The C mechanism returns one endpoint of a fresh Unix socket pair to the initiating D-Bus sender. It binds the echo of a 256-bit challenge to the sender's credentials and retains the process descriptor supplied by the kernel on the reply. The retained request supplies the unique bus name for rechecks. The executable check uses the exact trusted path and held executable descriptor, and compares PID, user and mount namespaces and the process's real, effective, saved and filesystem IDs. Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64, kernel `7.0.0-43-generic`, D-Bus `1.14.10` and sd-bus 255. A private session passed eight cases with a regular build and the same eight with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer. The harness verified a matching process and rejected a different executable or path, an incorrect challenge, a different same-user responder, unexpected or truncated descriptors, expiration, later bus disappearance, and later process exit with the bus name kept live by an inheritor. It checked closing the received descriptors. Both receipts, `ubuntu-l3-peer-native.log` and `ubuntu-l3-peer-sanitized.log`, have SHA-256 `167bc0e9f1a5092cc1d5312976cf5a69ed8bb458ba9fc9e30015bcbdbcab2a14` in `.tmp/acl-validation/`. The temporary development headers, binaries and stage were removed. [Linux kernel SCM_PIDFD interface](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/include/linux/socket.h), [sd-bus credential lookup](https://www.freedesktop.org/software/systemd/man/255/sd_bus_get_name_creds.html) | Qualify initial PID reuse on the older bus, the Ubuntu kernel floor, an active graphical session, protected package and runtime identity, subject and namespace transfer, the immutable consent digest, the polkit action and installed package lifecycle. |
| L2 G-FLAG/G-BIND/G-RESULT/G-UX (Linux inode protection) | The bound reader reports immutable and append-only on the original inode. It uses a verified readable descriptor for the full flag word and can fall back to the protection bits from statx when a mode-000 file permits only a metadata pin. Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64, kernel `7.0.0-43-generic`, ext4, SDK `10.0.101`. UID 1000 without capabilities could see inode flags and was denied a native immutable clear. The gated setter used the same UID with only `CAP_LINUX_IMMUTABLE` in a private process and a test-owned durable journal. Six Core cases passed. They verified explicit clear, mode-000 read-back on the retained descriptor, access and default ACL changes, group assignment with accepted set-ID and capability clearance, preservation of unrelated flags, restrictive flags set last, and a partial result when unrelated flags changed after an already verified clear. The intent records the touched mask and requested value. An App test verified the staged preview and the native journal's refusal to dispatch when the application retains capabilities. Ordinary Ubuntu suites passed 40 Core and 26 App cases, with 100 and 31 platform or fixture skips. Ten APFS Mac editor regressions passed. The Ubuntu fixture and Mac image were removed. [Linux inode-flag ioctl and privilege rules](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/FS_IOC_GETFLAGS.2const.html) | Release settings keep the Linux flag writer disabled. Qualify L3 authorization and the Ubuntu release lanes. The ioctl takes the whole flag word, so the last fresh read and the setter leave a small interval in which an unrelated concurrent flag change can be lost. |

The owner editor opened on "Assignable owner SID, combo box" with the current owner, and Escape on the unchanged draft closed it without a discard prompt. The octal field showed the "mixed" placeholder for every subject, and Orca read "640. mixed." for a definite mode; the same commit sets it only for a mixed selection. The ACL lists virtualized their rows, so Orca announced "List with 2 items" for a five-entry ACL; `1fc8d0c3d` realizes every row of the inspector's and the four ACL editors' lists. The final run spoke the full path, the owner and group IDs, "Access now, List with 5 items" and the actions in one Tab cycle; opened the mode editor on "Owner read check box checked"; read "Permissions as octal entry 640"; announced the invalid-value message; and Escape raised "Discard changes?", whose Enter discarded the draft. The witness kept mode 0640 and its ACL.
