VMware reverted the supplied Windows guest to `updated #39` on 20 November 2026. Resuming the snapshot failed because the host did not support its 3D checkpoint. Discarding the active execution state preserved the saved snapshot. Cold boots reached the Windows logo and stalled with 3D acceleration both on and off. VMware Tools remained unreachable. The guest was powered off with 3D acceleration off, and VMware still listed the saved snapshot. Windows guest qualification remains open. [VMware graphics checkpoint guidance](https://knowledge.broadcom.com/external/article/328636)

A follow-up restarted Workstation 26.0.0 build `25388281` while the Ubuntu guest kept its original VM process. The Windows checkpoint returned both graphics compatibility prompts. Choosing Continue on both completed the restore at the hypervisor, and the restored Windows guest immediately logged a bugcheck. The new renderer log showed the Direct3D device rejected by VMware and the 3D checkpoint unsupported. The guest reset into Windows Boot Manager and the Tools heartbeat timed out again. After verifying Tools remained unreachable, the stalled Windows guest was powered off. VMware still listed `updated #39` and Ubuntu was running. Receipt `windows-vmware-restart-checkpoint.log`. [Broadcom graphics checkpoint diagnostics](https://knowledge.broadcom.com/external/article/459168/vmotion-operations-fail-with-error-svga.html)

After the machines returned, the Windows 11 x64 guest (build `26340`, .NET `10.0.14`) accepted an authenticated guest connection. The W1 runner verified a medium-integrity desktop token and used a disposable non-system NTFS VHDX. The first run refused native permission admission because the volume lacked Cloud Files membership evidence. On a separate task-owned volume with a corroborated `CldFlt` instance, all 10 backend tests passed and both applicable App tests passed, with three Linux-only App skips. The detached volume did not affect the system disk. The App receipt covers the Windows preset and one advanced right. The temporary task was removed, and both Defender real-time protection and Windows Update returned to their original running settings. Receipts `guest-windows-w1-backend.xml`, `guest-windows-w1-app.xml` and `guest-windows-w1.log`. See the expanded scope run below for the distinct-owner, denied-write, external-drift and unread-SACL cases.

The W1 registered-provider sentinel passed under the same ordinary token on a second filtered NTFS fixture. The live provider supplied a dataless placeholder and an ordinary sibling inside the registered root. FileCat's permissions inspector withheld a binding and the editor for both. The provider received zero payload requests, and the placeholder stayed dataless. The test unregistered its provider, and the harness detached the volume and removed its scheduled task. The Windows backend bundle SHA-256 was `811a839637dbc51a29b81b8055850e33b2921434259e778fe3eeab5e01be1be4` (overlay `31765da7872fd747a5cff1d6b77b61eabf282dcecbfd5ff6c51f0a921a7cafe1`). Receipt `guest-cloud-windows-w1-cloud.xml` contains one pass with zero skips. A final guest check confirmed all three task-owned images were detached, Defender and Update were restored, and the fixture directory was removed.

The W2 selected-folder-only candidate reached `NtSetSecurityObject(DACL_SECURITY_INFORMATION)` on a held NTFS directory handle as the ordinary medium-integrity user on the Windows 11 x64 guest (build `26340`, .NET `10.0.14`). On a separate filtered NTFS VHDX, both protected and unprotected parent cases retained owner, group, DACL protection and the exact reviewed ACL bytes. Existing files, a nested folder, a protected child and a concurrently open child all kept their original descriptors. A newly created subfolder inherited the added ACE. In both cases the setter cleared `SE_DACL_AUTO_INHERITED` (`8404 → 8004` and `9404 → 9004`). Exact same-object verification therefore reported an uncertain result. This optional route was removed from the product path for fixed NTFS. Windows file editing continues to use its separate section setter. Native propagation, inheritance conversions and reviewed tree operations remain open. Evidence `guest-diagnose-w2-windows-w2-backend.xml` (SHA-256 `e6d9b6d3ddaae71e088fda862506fe7b42d9f4bb16496cf50c70f5b091e5abca`), `guest-diagnose-w2-ordinary.log` and the diagnostic source overlay `windows-w2-directory-diagnostics.tgz`. [NtSetSecurityObject](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/nf-ntifs-ntsetsecurityobject) [Descriptor control bits](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ifs/security-descriptor-control)

W2 adds a separate Windows audit ACL model that retains native bytes and entry order, reports success/failure flags on simple audit ACEs, and keeps integrity labels, resource attributes and other policy entries as opaque bytes. Its revision carries the audit-specific control and resource-manager byte. The ordinary Windows inspector reports that audit ACL access requires an authorized session with `SeSecurityPrivilege`. It opens only the existing discretionary security route and leaves the file DACL available for editing. On Windows 11 x64 build `26340` and .NET `10.0.14`, the Release backend group `WindowsPermissionInspectionTests` passed 13 with zero skips. The Release App group `PermissionInspectorTests` passed both applicable cases with 11 Linux or Darwin skips. The Windows editor case waited for the audit notice and then verified a real DACL patch. The Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 ext4 run passed 31 shared Core tests, with 15 Darwin-only skips and one Linux capability-fixture skip. The native unread-SACL test is recorded below. The authorized W3 routes remain open. Clean source overlay `2c532fc028715edbfdd1b751cc558adb0b5f3f479e67223cbc3fde0586d59ce5`. App layout test overlay `7d9dbdbff0e205fdb97cbd7116471f9a86f3fbd4f5ceb5bcb5a353a2022b1d45`. Receipts `guest-layout-w2-windows-w2-backend.xml`, `guest-layout-w2-windows-w2-app.xml` and `ubuntu-w2-core.trx`. The App receipt SHA-256 is `06e0efd3b3912e0fa4e3b73fec1d967e385ffaa42892c2acebaff15194838824`. The Windows fixture directory, its detached VHDX files and scheduled task were removed. Both Ubuntu build directories and their test fixtures were removed. [Windows SACL access right](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/secauthz/sacl-access-right)

W2 used `SetSecurityInfo(DACL_SECURITY_INFORMATION)` through a retained directory handle with explicit `READ_CONTROL | WRITE_DAC` and normal read/write/delete sharing on the Windows 11 x64 guest build `26340`, .NET `10.0.14`, on a task-owned, filtered fixed NTFS VHDX. The selected unprotected directory retained control `8404` and matched the reviewed DACL. An existing file and a nested file held open with normal sharing inherited the rule. The byte witnesses in a protected branch, a protected child file and a sibling outside the selection remained equal. In a separate tree, the native API returned success and changed the parent and an eligible sibling while leaving the child whose `WRITE_DAC` open was denied untouched. A successful root read-back therefore cannot establish complete descendant propagation. Scope admission for links, reparse points, mount boundaries and the volume root, and a matching-root repair trigger remain open. The production propagation route is unavailable pending those checks. Receipt `guest-native-scope-windows-w2-scope.xml` (SHA-256 `8fcfe0b40a56b7b2a6a2c29f8b7f60fe8bc6ab75e0f3f78e3566afb40bd8ff0c`). [SetSecurityInfo](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/aclapi/nf-aclapi-setsecurityinfo)

The elevated fixture setup seeded one explicit success-and-failure audit ACE with a protected SACL and a separate medium integrity label on the same disposable NTFS image. The medium-integrity runner's `ACCESS_SYSTEM_SECURITY` open returned error 5. It used the ordinary FileCat adapter and journal job to add a DACL rule and verified the new rule on the same file. Elevated read-back matched the audit ACL bytes, audit control `2810` and integrity label before and after. Both audit receipts `guest-native-scope-audit-before.json` and `guest-native-scope-audit-after.json` have SHA-256 `4deb02e456a32008eedd98f5630dc04237f102fbccdf7f1ea20386c59eba2fc4`. The initial scope run passed all three probes and all 13 backend regression cases with zero skips. The first Windows image, task and staging directory were removed. [Windows SACL access right](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/secauthz/sacl-access-right)

The expanded Windows run also verified propagation from a protected root with control `9404` preserved. Both root cases retained owner and primary group. On ordinary files, FileCat changed a reviewed owner from the built-in Administrators group to the medium-integrity user's assignable SID and verified that the DACL and primary group stayed equal. It refused to acquire a `WRITE_DAC` handle on the denied file and preserved its DACL. On a different file, an external DACL change after review produced a conflict before dispatch and retained the independent change. The expanded fixture repeated the unread-SACL check and passed all six scope cases and all 13 backend regression cases with zero skips. It used `FILECAT_TEST_PERMISSIONS_WINDOWS_SCOPE=1` and `FILECAT_TEST_PERMISSIONS_NTFS=1`, with the two Release xUnit class filters `WindowsPermissionScopeTests` and `WindowsPermissionInspectionTests`. Scoped fixture scripts are `windows-guest-w2-scope.ps1` and `windows-ordinary-w2-scope.ps1` under `.tmp/acl-validation/`. The final source overlay is `4b4061c0216a2355b28f458777c8d238a750a14ca212f7cdf0b965b7fd7fb927`. Receipts `guest-native-scope-final-windows-w2-scope.xml` (SHA-256 `3a035adb302e98177707c27a6505616d010dee29349495ac712af53b97f69f2e`) and `guest-native-scope-final-windows-w2-regression.xml` (SHA-256 `e056342044dc5ea32fa2206e6fc2cce9b9299f70d09cb815dd40a1a2f1dc995a`).

W2 now carries native propagation through the permissions executor and journal. Admission requires one explicitly reviewed Windows directory DACL change and independent root verification. An already-matching root is refused until a repair route is qualified. After dispatch, an unchanged root and a native error are insufficient to prove that descendants were untouched. FileCat preserves an uncertain outcome in that case. Live Operations, the completion notice, copied reports and interrupted history name the selected root and leave descendant effects unverified. The progress view skips descendant totals, percentages and estimates. A recorded pre-dispatch conflict has no descendant-effects warning.

A complete boundary observation supports a fresh comparison before dispatch. Windows can accept namespace changes while the native propagation call enumerates descendants. Windows documents that directory R and RH oplocks break without waiting for acknowledgment when enumeration contents change. Rename or deletion of the oplocked directory itself has a different acknowledgment rule. FileCat therefore keeps production native propagation unavailable where only capture, comparison or these advisory notifications are available. Reviewed traversal needs its own capture and per-object admission. [Microsoft directory oplock behavior](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/win32/api/winioctl/ni-winioctl-fsctl_request_oplock)

The W2 Windows boundary capture reopens an already screened, held directory with a directory-only listing request, then uses the native cursor and opens each named child relative to the held parent. Exact names, object IDs, kinds and volume identity must agree. The capture refuses hard links, reparse points, offline or recall-required entries, missing native identity and branches it cannot list. A second pass compares the reviewed names, identities and ancestry before dispatch. Discovery and comparison share the review budgets. A newly unsafe entry or a changed name or identity invalidates the review. The evidence records membership rather than a prediction of which descendants Windows would edit. [Native directory opens](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/win32/api/winternl/nf-winternl-ntcreatefile)

On the Windows 11 x64 guest build `26340` and .NET `10.0.14`, the disposable NTFS fixture refused an owned hard link, a junction and a mount point to a separate NTFS VHDX. It refused a directory that denied listing and detected arrivals, same-name replacements and renames. A late file created after review caused the real permissions job to report conflict before dispatch. The root and existing child descriptors stayed equal. Protected and unprotected fixture propagation both passed the read-only boundary comparison. The live Cloud Files provider test rejected its registered subtree from an ordinary parent, recorded zero payload fetches and confirmed the placeholder stayed dataless. The final run passed 14 scope cases, 13 regressions and the live Cloud Files case with zero skips. Receipts `guest-boundary-conflict-windows-w2-scope.xml` (`58ec81795f1fa423a633e0814fc23e74c31808891e69d27860554990d21d681b`), `guest-boundary-conflict-windows-w2-regression.xml` and `guest-boundary-conflict-windows-w2-cloud.xml` (`af9ad4fb9824a71dbb6e30c00febcd359fd91baede66e7b701e9003ecab38d08`). Final overlays `38256f8f0fa06a3cf3a8b95f8e79ede8b2aa7cc4bde7bb7ec38387c68aa2931a`, `082a8fa78402c1d8e6dfde9ca15ac7d21a4fa40df3cde54bbb0797a504e23028` and `4ca14fec20620e17039ebb84370637fb3fd1af684e826b6221316d9cd2158dd6`. Both fixture runs removed their folder mounts, dismounted both images and retired their scheduled tasks. [Windows mounted folders](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows-server/storage/disk-management/assign-a-mount-point-folder-path-to-a-drive)

The Windows 11 x64 guest build `26340` on .NET `10.0.14` ran both protected and unprotected NTFS root propagation cases through the actual FileCat job and protected journal, using a fixture adapter that held the root and issued one `SetSecurityInfo` call. Independent checks still found inherited changes on eligible children and unchanged protected witnesses. All six scope tests and 13 backend regressions passed with zero skips, including the unread-SACL witness. Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 ran the focused `PermissionJobTests` and `PermissionInspectionTests` (32 passed, 16 fixture or platform skips) and `OperationReportTests`, `PermissionInspectorTests` and `PermissionsDialogTests` (18 passed, seven other-OS skips). The Core case reproduced a native error with the original root digest and recovered the uncertain scope. The App case confirmed the distinction between a pending native call and a recorded conflict before dispatch. Receipts `guest-propagation-job-windows-w2-scope.xml` (`f9f0ec64eff64ff6f1e7a6351a6d394129495cebd2ef0deacb32386456ec7569`), `guest-propagation-job-windows-w2-regression.xml`, `ubuntu-w2-results-core.trx` and `ubuntu-w2-results-app-final.trx`. Source overlays `9c8a0a5691f747dc991f03b88953af910fdd1db6f447a752b2b1795cf218ef0b` and `714ee59ce16af00d51e1c532d6833e852e00744e9de7c8fedc6c1dcd26c17a4f`. Production folder admission remains gated on link, reparse-point, mount and namespace evidence.

L2 now prepares a bounded ext4 mode tree for the permissions executor. It captures names from held directories, checks each child against its recorded inode and full ancestry, and retains a descriptor for each admitted object. The review allows up to 96 captured names and stops discovery at 4,096 directory entries. It normalizes overlapping roots, deduplicates hard-linked files and counts excluded entries and inaccessible branches. Submission requires the captured manifest and children-first order. The job refreshes the original namespace and revisions before dispatch and never enumerates new mutation targets. `openat2` constrains each descendant open to its parent's mount, including bind mounts. [Linux constrained open](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/openat2.2.html) [Linux descriptor mode write](https://www.man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/fchmodat.2.html)

The Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 ext4 guest on kernel `7.0.0-43` passed the three new tree cases and 32 other tests under the Release `PermissionJobTests`, `PermissionInspectionTests` and `UnixArchiveMoveTests` filters with `FILECAT_TEST_PERMISSIONS_EXT4=1`. Sixteen platform or fixture cases skipped. The cases verified conditional and exact execute, children-first dispatch, overlapping roots, alias deduplication, an unreadable branch, a symbolic link and a later arrival. The job refused the original leaf after its parent was replaced even though its inode and link count stayed the same. Receipt `ubuntu-l2-tree-core.trx` (`e2a600f1b7b90b412b5304ec649d60b192c4cb64ca2b4ba4e4416506ee5be5fc`) and overlay `bcfb7120594cb0808b798b864ff1e38899574370318c7f9ebf692258f3c549fd` are retained under `.tmp/acl-validation/`. The guest directory was removed and its absence verified.

The permissions inspector can open the qualified captured-tree editor for one selected folder. The user chooses conditional or exact execute, reviews changed paths and affected ACL mask entries, sees counts for excluded and inaccessible branches and confirms any set-ID clearing before submission. The review warns that inode changes affect all hard-link names. The job and interrupted journal keep bounded capture counts for the copied report. Ubuntu Release passed 21 App tests with seven platform skips and 35 Core tests with 16 platform or fixture skips. The new panel tests verified cancellation, set-ID consent and an applied mask change while preserving a late file. The Core cases verified scope and omission counts after reading the interrupted journal prefix. Receipts `ubuntu-l2-tree-app.trx` (`5a65cead020e526cb125a11a1edd16f5b2111bfe09b0b0857a6aad8032c9ae74`) and `ubuntu-l2-tree-history.trx` (`ca0b82e957c94e7a701c9b03972ac846d65534fe094814b640334f6848f521b7`). Source overlay `632195671b65b1e5380c64d2701f428560eadc7f2510958ac9483b11f6213327`. Production composition keeps the editor gated pending release-floor and smoke qualification. Other platform and filesystem lanes still need their own recursive migration.

The native nested-mount case used a real ext4 bind mount in a private Linux mount namespace. The ordinary user verified that the nested mount shared the root's device ID and had a distinct mount ID. FileCat excluded the mount, changed the captured root and same-mount sibling, and left the mounted witness at mode `0600`. The case passed without skips. The namespace released the mount on exit and the task directory was removed. Receipt `ubuntu-l2-tree-bind.trx` (`04514913fd9554e6dc817a74fc8dcf2515fbd29fca53c3ce307067e3cebdb7ca`) and fixture-test overlay `f74bc16334d279f9db15f3f1d06f6aaea6d927c2a48a5f62fc23ed8a5bb39e98`.

L2 POSIX reset uses the selected folder's retained default ACL as the template for the access ACL on each captured descendant. The template folder has no setter, and descendant directory defaults are preserved. The conditional projection clears effective execute on previously non-executable regular files through their owner, group-class and other mode bits. Exact execute is an explicit choice. The review shows removed and changed rows with configured and mask-limited rights, requires approval for the replacement and possible access expansion, and requests separate set-ID clearance consent. The executor rechecks the source default ACL and each target before dispatch. Immutable and append-only descendants are excluded, including the subtree beneath a protected directory. The interrupted journal retains the template locator, revision digest and capture counts. [Linux POSIX ACL semantics](https://www.man7.org/linux/man-pages/man5/acl.5.html) [Linux inode attribute observation](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/statx.2.html)

Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 on kernel `7.0.0-43` passed 38 Core tests with 18 platform or fixture skips and 22 App tests with seven platform skips. Native cases verified conditional and exact reset, preservation of the template folder and descendant defaults, journal recovery, a late arrival, and a changed template rejected before any intent or write. The App case verified the removed rule in the diff and the approval required to submit. A separate ordinary-user run passed the native protected-entry case with no skips. Independent `lsattr` observations showed both protections survived the job and were cleared during fixture cleanup before the stage was removed. Receipts `ubuntu-l2-reset-core.trx` (`24aa0a9795c9614fed2a7a2650d9de677a239e1eae86f95eca050e9ee9ec4c5f`), `ubuntu-l2-reset-app.trx` (`70902bb747ccecfd73ffdc4ef78cc65cfdaba9c846073fbf0282f35b59b5cb8e`) and `ubuntu-l2-reset-protected.trx` (`67b36b4d6c8b8c29b910c3aba01a2efa44833c94664441a4d9d6ee861e2fe0bf`). Source overlay `88a1a60af9c5721cabd7f3f11c38f61b4c2e118d2b9b3953c021a2574b0338d9`. Reset has a separate production gate pending Linux release qualification.

The qualified Linux ext4 Permissions and times dialog now captures recursive mode edits through the same tree review as the inspector. An unchanged selected root can have changed descendants. The dialog keeps recursive timestamps independent, offers a separate timestamp submission while retaining the mode draft, and leaves selection marks in place if the captured scope or outcomes are incomplete. Review current state reopens the captured identities and shows a conflict for a same-name replacement. The Attributes and inspector capture budgets include their existing inspection leases. The verified Attributes route reviews one mounted filesystem per job.

On Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 with kernel `7.0.0-43`, Release passed 39 Core tests with 18 platform or fixture skips and 24 App tests with seven platform skips. App tests covered the matching root, conditional and exact execute, separate timestamp application, a replacement conflict with retained marks, verified marks clearing and reinspection of captured descendants. The Core test refused a batch exceeding the remaining lease allowance and admitted the same selection with sufficient room. The remaining filesystem and native-operation subcases stay open. The Ubuntu 26.04 smoke is recorded separately above.

L2 current-process access checks use `faccessat2(AT_EACCESS | AT_EMPTY_PATH)` on the retained ext4 object. The report shows the check time, mount instance, effective and filesystem IDs, supplementary groups, effective capabilities and observed mount restrictions. A directory gets separate listing, search, write, and combined write-and-search checks. The route is gated in production. On the same Ubuntu guest, the native Core fixture distinguished search from listing and refused a same-name replacement. The panel checked read, write and execute rights on a mode-0400 file without submitting a job. Release Core passed 29 with 15 Mac-only skips and App passed 23 with 7 other-OS skips. The App overlay SHA-256 was `a963445baefd300185a37f6c166d77e2058706e160bcc7e75cd60ca9e0064267` and the additional Core test overlay was `62bd22e45c1a6a2d2565818ac393056f10d80c2cacd5423e53f623fa9363c32f`. Receipts `ubuntu-permissions-l2-access-core.trx` and `ubuntu-permissions-l2-access.trx` are in `.tmp/acl-validation/`. The Ubuntu test directory was removed. Other mount types and minimum-kernel qualification remain open. [Linux access check implementation](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/fs/open.c)

The additional Linux filesystem gate admits XFS and Btrfs through the existing held-descriptor permissions adapter. It corroborates each selected mount with its kernel mount ID and filesystem magic. On Btrfs it uses the held mount root's subvolume device and stops when a path enters an implicit subvolume with a different device ID. A Btrfs mount reporting `noacl` presents Unix mode and an explicit unavailable status for access and default ACLs. Its mode revision covers owner, group and mode, and the writer rechecks the mount's ACL policy before dispatch. The ordinary inspector offers the mode editor on this route. [Linux filesystem identifiers](https://www.man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/fstatfs.2.html) [Btrfs ACL mount options](https://btrfs.readthedocs.io/en/stable/btrfs-man5.html)

Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 on `7.0.0-43-generic` passed six focused native cases. The XFS and Btrfs ACL fixtures verified mode-000 repair, current-process access and a `nosuid` observation, combined mode and timestamp writes, access ACL replacement, and default ACL inheritance using independent `getfacl` checks. The Btrfs tree excluded an implicit subvolume. The mode-only case kept mode `0600` after unmount and remount and preserved the dormant ACL bytes. A separate `noacl` to `acl` remount kept the same identity and caused a pre-dispatch conflict with no intent or write. The mode-only inspector showed the disabled ACL status and applied a verified mode change with its protected app-state journal on ext4. The ext4 regression passed 39 Core and 24 App tests with 23 and eight fixture or platform skips, respectively. Receipts `ubuntu-l2-fs-variants.trx` (`49c12825c4355ee23048cd3f02a33ac1717051e2f77afa1d2bab23ebd0366b77`), `ubuntu-l2-fs-noacl-core.trx`, `ubuntu-l2-fs-noacl-app.trx` (`54c565bac2deb00595bf7e13e6259a5c3149e2b4c059676ac76c932323cbef01`), `ubuntu-l2-fs-remount.trx` (`8dbe737142da230db03f2fdbe3d59baad7816eb4e10fd186576367d345cb05fc`), and `ubuntu-l2-fs-ext4-core.trx` and `ubuntu-l2-fs-ext4-app.trx`. Overlay `5d6d84c4a49e80814f738b36e942bb77d841ddaf53c5c45b58196b92616c9676`. All three images, their loop devices and the temporary XFS module were removed. The XFS and Btrfs target routes remain production-gated pending release coverage. That run left private Btrfs journal storage open.

R1 uses a source build of SSH.NET 2026.0.0 at commit `7b2fd3dbf2c86a80a7b06cea020aa5f821c9902e`. The project uses the existing cryptography and logging dependency versions. The retained reply flags distinguish a missing field from a reported zero, including epoch timestamps and unsigned UID/GID values. The inspector acquires one authenticated lease per folder and reads each selected path with raw LSTAT. It shows server account labels from version 1 of `users-groups-by-id@openssh.com` where available. The typed lsetstat request accepts one component per call, with an explicit UID/GID pair or the native mode and retained file-type bits. The app inspector remains read-only while server-path admission, review and recovery are developed. [Source changes](../../src/ThirdParty/SshNet/THIRD-PARTY-CHANGES.md) [OpenSSH protocol](https://github.com/openssh/openssh-portable/blob/master/PROTOCOL)

On Windows 11 Pro x64 build 26200, the ordinary interactive token pinned the host key and connected to both inbox Windows OpenSSH 9.5p2 (9.5.5.1) and Microsoft Windows OpenSSH 10.4p1 preview restricted to loopback. FileCat observed the file mode, explained that Windows OpenSSH is inspection-only and refused the permission review. The native file contents remained unchanged and both temporary SSH listeners were stopped. Receipts in windows-11-fresh/receipts include windows-sftp-inbox.trx (32c4ec569f31c7b93239a3197008b1faa31a78daee910d8c576ecd50b12cf380) and windows-sftp-ordinary-rerun.trx (980ada02acf924fe38514a7e50cc7d08e417d11f8766ca972596916543edeb3a).

On Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 with kernel `7.0.0-43-generic` and OpenSSH 9.6p1, the locked Release Remote suite passed 89 cases with one fixture skip. The owned server returned the symbolic link's own attributes. The typed wire request repaired a mode-000 file, and the original bytes, UID/GID and mtime matched the prior observations. A server with lsetstat disabled still served LSTAT and refused the typed change before dispatch. The wire fixtures covered omitted fields, zero, high unsigned IDs, six mode masks, the UID/GID pair, and bounded account-name reply parsing. The Release App suite passed 14 cases with seven platform or fixture skips, including the panel's mode-000 and symbolic-link observations and a real OpenSSH edit-session commit. The macOS 27.2.1 arm64 run with OpenSSH 10.5p1 passed 12 focused cases without skips.

The framework-dependent Linux x64 preview payload generated an SPDX 2.3 document for 74 files. The document records `FileCat.SshNet` 2026.0.0 as MIT AND ISC and includes both license files. Existing transfer metadata equality and dependency versions were verified. Receipts `ubuntu-sftp-r1-remote.trx` (`5456f4858c3af77495b2ea5665d939f38aa11428900de4cdf9436274212d8fc9`), `ubuntu-sftp-r1-app.trx` (`55f30d3ec0976bf72ff281d734333c3c7867537b515920f4695e3b74927d4e54`), `mac-sftp-r1-remote.trx` (`267d3519342c6b4fb36f1761313b096844a7e5d837c4802411954f75373f6ae7`) and `ubuntu-sftp-r1-preview.spdx.json` are in `.tmp/acl-validation/`. Source overlay `05330a63f08f26a2eb9f745037538d1deef4f79df8bae33758c74c45477f8567`. Lock overlay `98253b565d1fd02780e8430eafdbce4474b128586292480fac92219595b93c99`. The Ubuntu and Mac stages and owned servers were removed. Windows OpenSSH and installed package qualification remain open.

R1 write review now has an admission and revalidation path. The first server profile uses the portable OpenSSH 9.6p1 identification and the complete versioned SFTP extension set from its subsystem. Windows OpenSSH and other unqualified subsystem profiles expose inspection. The review retains the authenticated lease and rechecks the host-key and profile authority. It reads the root and each ancestor by exact LSTAT, requires a complete non-root leaf tuple, and corroborates the leaf owner against the server's start directory and account lookup. Observable changes in the leaf, ancestor, or authority invalidate the review. The server-path residual in §5 still applies. [OpenSSH 9.6p1 SFTP extension table](https://github.com/openssh/openssh-portable/blob/V_9_6_P1/sftp-server.c#L672-L698)

On Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 with kernel `7.0.0-43-generic` and OpenSSH `9.6p1 Ubuntu-3ubuntu13.20`, all 25 admission cases passed. The native fixture kept its files in the ordinary user's private runtime tree. It admitted mode-000 files and folders to review, refused group-writable or sticky ancestors, symlinks, FIFOs and a server without lsetstat, and detected observable directory replacement and session or profile changes. Repeated stale listings returned their leases. The locked Release Remote suite passed 114 with one fixture skip, and the App suite passed 14 with seven platform or fixture skips. Receipts `ubuntu-sftp-admission-remote.trx` (`0f29b5dafae41b13ff54605a4c9e33aa0c851906a22ace859bc272f7d482d051`) and `ubuntu-sftp-admission-app.trx` (`34cf919f12d3efa8f6c14f19aae8946fe11d98420ed4c8a01ac435996b94f266`) are in `.tmp/acl-validation/`. Source overlay SHA-256 `4092a60d9772ddcc586b560643ebfeafeb98870cc9dc69ff2d9202ad1e122619`. The source stage and the private runtime fixture were removed after the tests. The app permissions surface remains inspection-only while the review controls and remaining write cases are developed.

R1 reviewed SFTP mode jobs run through the shared permissions executor. The executor verifies private journal storage and flushes each intent before rechecking the reviewed authority, path and original field values, then dispatches one typed lsetstat request. Unedited mode bits are preserved. Results identify server-path verification without asserting inode identity. Interrupted receipts preserve the disposition and a path evidence digest. Reinspection requires the original SFTP connection. When a write lease closes, a replacement lease can only make observations after corroborating the reviewed authority and ancestry.

On the same Ubuntu/OpenSSH fixture, six native mode job cases passed. Mode-000 files and directories were repaired with independent mode and mtime checks. A failed journal storage recheck and mode drift after the intent flush refused dispatch. A forced loss of the original lease after component verification and before the final read opened one observation lease. Final mode drift produced a partial result and retained the journal. The locked Release Remote suite passed 120 with one fixture skip, the focused Core suite passed 39 with 30 platform or fixture skips, and the focused App suite passed 22 with seven platform or fixture skips. The Ubuntu source, history, and private runtime fixtures were removed after the run.

The receipts in `.tmp/acl-validation/` are `ubuntu-sftp-write-remote.trx` (`d8921332e479229d78c2a70b201289e2bd079afb525d750f60d3f8f25935a575`), `ubuntu-sftp-write-core.trx` (`ec7530d7b982ea2cb744361c5b65e69ec7b34d44b0938a9f5ef6834db1ad87e6`), and `ubuntu-sftp-write-app.trx` (`c314bb9c7d52dff8de480bf9122253eb48068fed2cec49cc2ae46c637becac65`). Source overlay SHA-256 `f82ec00e9cd77f59e98cd9589108da22733b6e2f7b73f35090ed95ceaa448fdb`.

R1 group and compound jobs remove reviewed rights first, apply the group, read the new tuple, and apply reviewed mode grants before the last explicit set-ID step. Existing set-ID bits must be addressed in the review. Those bits are cleared before the group request and restored only where expressly requested. On Ubuntu OpenSSH `9.6p1 Ubuntu-3ubuntu13.20`, the plaintext fixture trace verified that the group-only request sent the paired unchanged UID sentinel and the requested GID. Other accepted subsystem profiles use the observed UID until their sentinel behavior is qualified.

Ten native group and compound cases passed on the Ubuntu fixture. Independent stat checked owner, group, mode and unchanged mtime. After a lost success reply, FileCat recorded the observed new GID and stopped the remaining mode grants. When a denied reply was dropped and the original tuple remained observable, FileCat recorded uncertainty. Each test forwarded the original request to OpenSSH, recorded the actual status at the subsystem, then withheld that reply. Neither case sent a replacement write. The locked Release Remote suite passed 130 with one fixture skip, focused Core passed 39 with 30 platform or fixture skips, and focused App passed 22 with seven platform or fixture skips. The source archive SHA-256 is `108d37d905f5e8a29f6c26682ec16ac87d45daad53598b2084cda0a27b6e6bed`. Ubuntu staging, protected history and runtime fixtures were removed.

Receipts in `.tmp/acl-validation/` are `ubuntu-sftp-group-remote.trx` (`ea80ca7837c354038291f9705ca1d6b8899c040ed99c235daa622d1949e387ed`), `ubuntu-sftp-group-core.trx` (`dde244090ac8aae3b448d3f46283068cc5312e997fa6810a4a9d35c10469b693`), and `ubuntu-sftp-group-app.trx` (`5e104de48bd769c1789767d02626a50288f768879baca044fd9dd859b0760cf7`).

File → Inspect → Permissions and ownership now offers **Review SFTP permissions** on the qualified Ubuntu OpenSSH build. FileCat compares the original inspected authority and complete tuple against a newly retained lease before opening the editor. The review shows the exact server path, authenticated account, ordered mode changes, and the paired UID for group assignment. Set-ID effects require explicit acceptance. Apply rechecks the original path and connection settings, transfers the lease once to the permissions job, and displays the result. Review current state reads the same server path under the original authority. Unsupported builds and special items remain inspection-only. The editor documents which server principals and processes can still redirect a path request.

Seven headless panel cases passed against OpenSSH `9.6p1 Ubuntu-3ubuntu13.20` on Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64. They exercised mode-000 repair and result refresh, a group and mode change with explicit set-ID consent, rejection of an overflowing group ID, dirty-draft discard, mode drift before the editor and before Apply, a changed profile, and a subsystem without lsetstat. The Release App suite passed 29 with seven platform or fixture skips, Remote passed 130 with one fixture skip, and Core passed 39 with 30 platform or fixture skips. On macOS 27.2.1 arm64 with OpenSSH 10.5p1, the focused Remote wire and loopback suite passed 17 with two Linux-only skips. This Mac run covered transport regression while the Ubuntu run covered the write editor. Both machines' source stages and fixtures were removed.

The source archive SHA-256 is `09647ae9f5ec947961441e66f4aacda90d282edf710773966da8a190acc3440d`. Receipts in `.tmp/acl-validation/` are `ubuntu-sftp-ui-app.trx` (`a54d6a7e0e3e190f9635476a7d6a27f2e07cf8e50b07ccba01ec4ff57928f606`), `ubuntu-sftp-ui-remote.trx` (`27e1dbc87d08b6996649b2715a203abcea75542d265692b4af058456e3bc2f78`), `ubuntu-sftp-ui-core.trx` (`3b45f9d3d84deb1c5e184350e6064a764d4d42a967126b739e2c8f33a410b3ba`), and `mac-sftp-ui-remote.trx` (`830d9989e4962b6272c94d3f4432e00761914ffaa5f8946fc3d1c3d84a22366d`).

R2 adds read-only FTP and FTPS observations to the item inspector. FileCat reads the selected names from one fresh, bounded listing per folder. The MLSD parser uses the raw fact list before the pathname and checks octal mode, including explicit zero. It shows reported FTP action codes, owner and group facts, and the opaque server identifier as separate observations. Qualified Unix LIST rows can supply mode with LIST provenance. Duplicate names and mismatches with the captured listing are refused. Existing FTP consent and FTPS certificate checks govern the connection. [RFC 3659](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3659.html)

On Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64, the Release FTP integration suite passed 20 cases without skips against task-owned loopback servers. The new cases exercised plain FTP, explicit and implicit FTPS, raw mode and account fields, explicit zero, a misleading pathname suffix, missing and malformed fields, duplicate names, CR/LF refusal and Unix LIST provenance. The command logs contained MLSD and no SITE CHMOD. The existing pinned-certificate check refused a changed certificate. The Release App inspector suite passed 12 cases with six platform or fixture skips. The actual panel command displayed mode, owner and transfer-action codes with the read-only explanation and submitted no job.

Receipts `ubuntu-ftp-r2-remote.trx` (`06a68a493573f43ba9e8a2630218866c157b8d39f125f132731daf37e65e8315`) and `ubuntu-ftp-r2-app.trx` (`a96412e748b6e5f8373c94a99a4cadeec23407471989eb975be26d4cea29e5b7`) are in `.tmp/acl-validation/`. Source overlay SHA-256 `20cd200bd7a44749fdb4d05614643476f7493cfbd371a893c5c5fe20e0b57e8c`. The owned listening socket and processes exited before the Ubuntu stage was removed. Installed package and other server or parser qualification remain open.

The protected permissions journal now admits Btrfs on x64 Linux. The writer and recovery scanner open the same private directory. Before the first intent, the writer flushes both the new file's directory and the directory that contains it. Recovery verifies the journal's name against its held file after reading. [Linux fsync documentation](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/fsync.2.html)

On Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64, kernel `7.0.0-43-generic`, the Btrfs journal passed seven Release Core cases under each of `acl` and `noacl` as UID 1000, with zero skips. The fixtures checked owner-private modes, live-writer locking, same-name replacement, symlink refusal and retention of uncertain results while completed records were pruned. Four abrupt child-process exits bracketed the real bound mode setter and its journal writes. Recovery found the expected begin, intent and outcome records after each exit and offered no continuation. The app verified a mode edit and reopened its stored history with both app state and the target on Btrfs `noacl`. The ext4 regression passed 39 Core and 24 App cases with 30 and eight fixture or platform skips. The macOS 27.2.1 arm64 APFS regression passed four Core cases with zero skips. The crash probes exercised process termination while the OS continued running. Btrfs target mutation remains behind its separate release gate.

Receipts in `.tmp/acl-validation/` are `ubuntu-l2-journal-{acl,noacl}-{binding-crash,retention}.trx`, `ubuntu-l2-journal-noacl-app.trx`, `ubuntu-l2-journal-ext4-{core,app}.trx` and `mac-l2-journal-apfs-core.trx`. Source overlay SHA-256 `5fb13c110e82e34c82542dd54d727d00fe4564470f11fd464a4a45c3ae8983de`. The Ubuntu stage, image and loop device were removed, and the Mac stage was removed after collecting its receipt.

The separate L2 POSIX ACL estimate now evaluates recorded discretionary rules for a supplied UID and group list. It uses owner, named user, matching group and others precedence, applies the ACL mask and requires a single matching group to grant all bits in a compound request. Missing group context yields unknown where it can change the result. The dialog shows the observation time and keeps actual access unknown because parent permissions, mount restrictions and other policy can affect it. Ubuntu 24.04.5 ext4 Release passed 30 Core tests with 15 Mac-only skips and 24 App tests with 7 other-OS skips. The new cases verified mode-only fallback, masked named-user precedence, split group grants and the native panel with an incomplete group list. Overlay `186f6fddb8e9c1d151374d9da7a1741467ff64b1a0cdec63c5fd984ba4245941` plus the App initialization fix `c4e2baf699452ecd190e33539417f0c9c8e40669338f830fa4913e849350871d`. Receipts `ubuntu-permissions-l2-estimate-core.trx` and `ubuntu-permissions-l2-estimate-app.trx`. The Ubuntu fixture was removed. Group and mount variants and the minimum-kernel release gates remain open. [Linux POSIX ACL access rules](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man5/acl.5.html#ACCESS_CHECK_ALGORITHM)

The Ubuntu L2 native group-change probe used `fchownat(AT_EMPTY_PATH)` as UID 1000 with the original inode pinned by `O_PATH`. It changed the group to a supplementary GID on mode-000 and regular entries, refused a GID outside the process groups, cleared both set-ID bits on a group-executable regular file and preserved set-group-ID on the owned non-group-executable regular file and directory. A rename control verified that the descriptor still addressed the original inode. The disposable fixtures were removed. Receipt `ubuntu-permissions-l2-chgrp-native.log`. [Linux chown implementation](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/fs/open.c) [Linux set-ID clearing rules](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/fs/attr.c)

The L2 group editor now offers the process's assignable GIDs and accepts an optional mode edit in the same scoped job. It previews and verifies restrictive mode, the bound group assignment, then final reviewed mode, with set-ID consent and file-capability read-back. The job checks effective and filesystem credentials and exact security sections before dispatch and between calls. Ubuntu 24.04.5 ext4 Release passed 32 Core tests with 15 other-platform skips and 25 App tests with 7 other-platform skips. The new tests verified a denied nonmember GID, ordered restrictive chmod and group assignment, unchanged directory defaults, and actual set-ID and capability clearance on a private file. The App test applied the reviewed directory sequence through the ordinary panel. Overlay `fc7a5fe1690c20ef9a0552642f8c5b53e90600e70dba0b97cf38c8c467e0738e`. Receipts `ubuntu-permissions-l2-group-core.trx` and `ubuntu-permissions-l2-group-app.trx`. The Ubuntu fixture and capability test file were removed. The group writer remains gated pending release qualification, including the independent racing-group control, minimum kernel and mount variants.

The G-MODEL and editor-detach receipts are in `.tmp/acl-validation/gmodel/`. Windows skips are the ReFS, SMB and volume-root fixtures. Ubuntu skips are the macOS, Windows, Btrfs and privileged fixtures. macOS skips are the Linux, Windows, HFS+ and journal-flag fixtures.

| ID | Requirement and representative lane | Status and evidence at baseline | Remaining action / reopening trigger |
|---|---|---|---|
| G-MODEL | Lossless native rows, statuses, mixed selection and bounded identities | Passed for the listed subcases on Windows 11 x64, Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 and macOS 27.2.1 arm64 on 22 November. No-op: a component that already matches writes no intent and makes no native call, and an already-matching object in a mixed ext4 or APFS selection is reported as such. Cancel: cancellation during a call keeps the read-back and stops later components, and a cancelled macOS tree leaves unattempted members unchanged. Opaque round trip: POSIX named-zero and opaque payload bytes, Windows unknown ACE bytes, opaque neighbors and trailing bytes, Darwin unknown rights and flag bits, and SIDs without name lookup survive projection and patching. Duplicates: repeated Windows ACEs and audit entries and repeated Darwin UUIDs keep their order, a patch changes only the reviewed occurrence, and the POSIX parser refuses invalid duplicates. Absent and denied: observations keep zero, unloaded, denied and absent apart, Windows DACLs and SACLs distinguish absent, NULL, empty and malformed, and Darwin jobs and trees keep an absent ACL distinct from an empty one. Mixed updates: one mode delta across a mixed ext4 or APFS selection changes only its touched bit on each object, and untouched mixed octal stays valid. Overflow: the POSIX parser refuses overflow, the Windows patch refuses an ACL beyond its 16-bit size field, a macOS tree refuses an over-capacity review, and ext4's native ENOSPC refusal is reported as failed before change. See the G-MODEL receipts below. | | Windows editing is single-object, so mixed selection does not apply there. Reopen a model's subcases on its serializer or canonicalization change. |
| G-BIND | Original → review → job subject, hardlinks, mount and authorization transitions on each binding family | Passed for the listed subcases. On 22 November each one mapped to passing native tests. Redirected ancestors: the Windows junction boundary and crossed ReFS junction, ext4 retargeting and SFTP shared or symbolic ancestry are refused. Final links and reparse points: Windows reparse screening, ext4 link and FIFO refusal, Darwin FIFO refusal and tree link exclusion, and SFTP special items. Replaced names: NTFS, ext4, the Darwin parent route before and after dispatch, SFTP observable changes and the captured App editor refuse or report them. Renames: a Windows rename with a same-name replacement reads stale, the Darwin descriptor route keeps the moved object with folder drift, and a pure ext4 rename between review and dispatch is a conflict before any intent (`19124ab95`, Ubuntu `ubuntu-gbind-core.trx`, 49 passed, `a2912535e3fc3bac0a9c3ffe0d21fb28242ce00c088dc81d4c40527d7b50c254`). Reused or remounted roots: the Linux CIFS remount, FAT detach and reattach, Btrfs remount, the hot-removed Windows disk and the force-detached macOS image. Hard-link aliases and overlap: the Windows boundary and reset, ext4 tree deduplication, the Darwin ACL tree and the journal. Stale security with an unchanged modification time: Windows external DACL drift, the ext4 reset template ACL, and SFTP and Darwin drift after review. Helper identity: the W3 broker receipt and reviewed replacement, and the L3 helper namespace. On macOS 27.2.1 arm64 the parent-relative writer reached and changed a document through a search-only (`O_SEARCH`) ancestor, and a home document reached through the `/Users` firmlink bound the same data-volume object as its `/System/Volumes/Data` path, with a verified change through the firmlinked path (`63e8e5b32`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gbind/mac-gbind-core.trx`, 7 passed with zero skips, `eead22575982ba447ba49479a2814292170bd0bf258d61164ff6c07770f03f56`). | Reopen the binding family whose acquisition, mount evidence or authority transition changed. |
| G-UX | Real command/detached behavior, persistent editor, accessible validation, themes and readability | Open. `DialogViewportTests`, `DialogFocusOwnershipTests`, `ReportWindowOwnershipTests`, `BackgroundContextMenuTests`, `FindClosedOwnershipTests`, `ThemePickerTests`, `ViewerThemeLifecycleTests` and native accessibility harnesses. On 22 November NVDA passed the Windows inspector, ordered access editor and owner editor after `51356e8aa` (U1 row): copy targets are named and editors open on their first field. Orca passed the Linux inspector and mode editor after `9694b37e2` and `1fc8d0c3d` (U1 row): copy targets and ACL list sizes are spoken, and a definite mode is not read as mixed. VoiceOver on the macOS inspector showed that it does not follow keyboard focus into in-window dialog bodies (U1 row). Orca passed the Linux access ACL editor (U1 row). On 22 November `PermissionEditorRenderingTests` rendered the inspector and the Windows access, audit and mode editors in a dialog card with the product styles, in every palette and both densities: every visible text met 4.5:1 (3:1 for large text) against its composited background once TextBox placeholders were drawn at full opacity (`d3cbd0f77`; Fluent's half-opacity placeholder read at about 2:1 everywhere). A theme and density change kept an invalid draft, the selected rule and an open choice list, and a 420-pixel dialog wrapped long identities and twelve rules inside its width. Through the real Windows editor dialog, the unavailable Apply showed its reason, Enter on a rule option did not apply, Enter in the mask field applied once, the editor stayed open on the verified result, a repeated Enter submitted nothing and the footer offered Close (`1c1776302`). `0e6770e4d` extended the rendering check to the owner choice, a reviewed-changes list and the POSIX and Darwin ACL editors, and at font sizes 21 and 28 in a 640 by 420 window the editor dialog scrolled only vertically and keyboard focus revealed its last field. `875603ee9` drove the background menu, item menu and hidden-mark choice through a deterministic reader: each reached the object it names, and a marked item hidden by the filter was read only when "Include all" was chosen. `5c59848fe` added the Find route: results inspected the original, and closing Find with its inspector open closed it and submitted nothing; a selection changed while the reader was working showed no inspector and said why. On 22 November the Attributes subcases were mapped: invalid visible octal ("9", five digits, letters, a sign) leaves the draft without a change while a four-digit value carries the special bits (`Untouched_mixed_octal_is_valid_and_explicit_empty_input_is_not`, `ecdfb60d5`), and the rendered mode editor keeps the invalid text with its error across palettes; macOS modification time, creation time and Finder Hidden preserve the mode and unrelated flags in the shared dialog (`Mac_qualified_attributes_applies_mode_times_and_Finder_Hidden_in_one_reviewed_job`, `Mac_mode_is_read_only_and_independent_times_and_Finder_Hidden_preserve_permissions`, `nodump` kept by the mode-zero repair job); bound submissions keep their original identity and report change or refusal (`Boxes_and_octal_apply_one_bound_job_and_respect_the_original_marks`, `Item_menu_mode_editor_keeps_the_captured_subject_and_refuses_a_replacement`); and a protection compound stops after a failed earlier stage on ext4 and Darwin. On 22 November the last Attributes and Details subcases passed natively. A mode change applied from the inspector that the File-system details report opened verified on Ubuntu ext4, and both panel marks stayed, the changed item's included. With no reviewed binding, the Attributes dialog kept mode editing unavailable and named the reason, for objects on a task-owned tmpfs in an ordinary process and for any object in a root process. Timestamps then applied to a file, a folder and the file inside it, and every mode stayed. On macOS 27.2.1, another process changed the mode after review, so the combined mode, times and Finder Hidden job made no call. The modification and creation times and the Finder Hidden flag stayed, and the item stayed marked. The dialog required a creation time with the earlier modification time, because APFS would otherwise move the creation time (`25a4ea8c5`; Ubuntu receipts `.tmp/acl-validation/gux/ubuntu/logs/`, run on the GA kernel and listed in `.tmp/acl-validation/gtree/ga-floor/receipts.sha256` (`fd45e9eb3b9f35c73edc5b7598253d922d70266a60f60ad606408b239e40cb3a`); Mac `.tmp/acl-validation/gux/mac-gux-app.trx`, 4 passed, `cdcca2f2bd0b862ec6a25322f8fcaf786711fbb2385c66ae6d29f58a61411556`). On 22 November Orca 46.1 heard the Linux group and default-ACL editors in the logged-in GNOME Wayland session on the Ubuntu 24.04.5 VM. The self-contained App ran with both writers switched on for that run only. The new `FILECAT_QUALIFY_LINUX_*` switches let qualification runs compose each L2 lane, as the Windows switches do, and they stay off by default. The runner, `tests/FileCat.NativeAcceptance/linux-orca-group-defaults.py`, uses physical uinput keys and AT-SPI focus for one action or field at a time. The folder inspector offered "Edit default ACL…", and the editor opened speaking "Default ACL entries", "List with 5 items" and its first row. An invalid account ID was announced, and Escape raised "Discard changes?", whose Enter discarded the draft. The file inspector offered "Change group…", and the editor opened on "Assignable group ID combo box". Down spoke the chosen group (4), and Escape offered and discarded the change. The witnesses kept their owner, group, mode and ACLs, and all 18 steps passed (`59cac0b45`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gux/orca/orca-group-defaults-report.json` `c6e989469be0e1bfa5fe835c57431b49be28a8dbc1b194c56c96125caa656f8d`, speech log `orca-debug.log.gz` `aa0ce53e5a0361d09752412f998c58cf407dd3393c11cc0d2d3806058ceec491`). On 23 November the reason VoiceOver stayed silent in macOS in-window dialog bodies was found. FileCat's macOS live-region bridge walked a dialog body's automation parents while Avalonia was still attaching it, and Avalonia then dropped the link from the body's content to the dialog for good, so every body control reported a parent chain outside the window and an empty frame, and VoiceOver ignored focus there; the dialog's scroll area was ruled out. `c40dde730` tracks live regions after the attachment and relinks a focused element whose parents miss its window; two headless tests reproduce both halves. VoiceOver on macOS 27.2.1 then spoke the inspector's copy buttons with the exact path, owner 501 and group 20, its ACL row and the footer Close, and the Copy review's Destination and Options, and keyboard focus stayed in each dialog. With the off-by-default `FILECAT_QUALIFY_MAC_*` switches (`049f2ec66`) it spoke every control of the mode, access ACL, group, file protection, folder-tree mode and ACL-tree editors, the reviewed-change rows, the set-ID consent and "Discard changes?". The run exposed an error when a mode change was drafted for a document with an extended ACL; `8c0db83ba` fixes it, and the editor then asks before discarding. The macOS build of those changes passed 146 of 205 cases in 14 classes with 59 skips, and the Windows host passed the focus and permission dialog classes. The cursor pulling focus behind a dialog was not reproduced (`.tmp/acl-validation/gux-vo/receipts-gux-vo.sha256`, `81f7715732f2f2342e77d2b8165a74a130581d7d3e55e43c7e8a616b6a0fbd1c`). On 22 and 23 November NVDA 2026.3.1 heard the authorized-inspection flow, the elevated helper's consent dialogs and the Edit audit rules editor of an installed FileCat 1.0.6 (unsigned setup from `2cbfe5b16`) on the VMware Windows 11 VM, keyboard only, with `tests/FileCat.NativeAcceptance/win-nvda-audit-consent.ps1` (`307d82b9d`). It found four defects: the waiting card never said that Windows asks for approval, the "Authorized inspection unavailable" alert never spoke its reason, the audit mask was announced as "Audit rights mask" with no format and a different visible label, and after "Apply with authorization" focus fell to the window. `0a2e7aa65` speaks the waiting sentence and every alert's message from the focused button, names the mask "Audit rights mask (hexadecimal)" as shown, and keeps focus in a dialog while its action runs; four headless tests cover the fixes and fail without them. With that commit's FileCat.dll in the installation, an elevated portable NVDA, standing in for an installed NVDA's UIAccess, heard the helper's title, instruction, requester, steps and footnote, and by Tab its Inspect security, Apply security change and Cancel links; it also heard the Auditing list with its label entry, walked the editor's fields and review lines, and heard "Verified changed 1", and the stored SACL gained exactly the reviewed entry. A cancelled approval returned silently to the file row, and the unavailable alert, raised by changing the object's metadata during consent, spoke its reason. A portable NVDA at the user's level spoke only the helper's title and focused button and received no keys while that dialog was in front. The VM elevates administrators without a UAC prompt, so Windows' own UAC prompt, its secure desktop and a UAC decline were not heard (`.tmp/acl-validation/gux-nvda/receipts-gux-nvda.sha256`, `64bcb3e9a0b6e00094eeb92411a68c478f53a2276d0b9c2a656908d67f64cbad`). Confirmations such as "Discard changes?" spoke only their title and focused button in both readers; `ab7e2588c` gives that button the message, and a headless test covers both default buttons. The toolbar test's missing icon for Permissions and ownership was added (`f500ccadb`). | Working sets share Find's result-set route; the detached record and Details routes need a bound reader and passed natively (U1 row); the elevated refusal passed live (G-PRIV). On macOS, VoiceOver does not speak an ACL row's position or count, and in about 2 of 6 opens the group editor or "Discard changes?" opened with focus on its card instead of its first field or Discard; Down in the group chooser twice moved focus off it. Reopen affected subcases on dialog, capture, visual-resource, ownership or UI-framework pin changes; no exhaustive OS × palette × state cross-product. |
| G-RESULT | Accounting and recovery under local/remote errors | Passed for the listed subcases. J1 recovered records at four process-termination boundaries around a real bound mode change on Btrfs with ACLs enabled and disabled. The same four boundaries recovered the recorded prefix around a real Windows DACL change on NTFS and real macOS mode changes through the parent-relative and descriptor writers on APFS. A real ext4 ENOSPC refusal is reported as failed before change with its native error, and the review's later time component is not attempted. The installed Linux mode and chown jobs wrote CRC-checked private intent and outcome records with `Verified` and `SameObject`, including final object and terminal records. W2 retains native propagation scope in the job and journal, verifies the selected root and reports descendant effects as unverified after dispatch. `OperationReportTests`, journal/UndoRunner and storage selection precedents. A read-back answers from memory, so the Linux writer now flushes the object's filesystem before confirming a change; on a failed ext4 device the flush failed and the change was reported uncertain, not verified. A recheck that fails after the intent carries the same reason as a failed first check. On 22 November the Windows and macOS writers gained the durability confirmation Linux already had. A read-back answers from the file system's cache, so the Darwin writers now ask the device to commit with `F_FULLFSYNC` (fsync where there is no full flush) through the held descriptor or the held parent folder. The ordinary Windows writer cannot flush an object it may only re-permission (a guest probe on build 26200 refused FlushFileBuffers without write-data access), so it flushes through the containing folder opened with add-file access and keeps the file system's view when it may not. A failed flush leaves the change uncertain with its reason. Injected failures for one chosen object turned a read-back change uncertain on both Darwin routes and on Windows, while real flushes still verified: macOS 27.2.1 passed 103 permission job cases (88 native Darwin) on an ownership-enabled APFS image and the Windows guest passed 37 scope cases with the seeded audit and owner witnesses intact (`36a053342`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gresult-mac/mac-gresult-core.trx` `af4122031afb5f77c0d942dc923ac70d51f97484905c4138c288032a1aa7e899`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gresult-windows/receipts/windows-gresult-scope.xml` `9cc51de378b9cb049ec16be2de9d20c78b03bba2e2b695a8e1b0c3c72c7c1cf9`). The other listed subcases map to passing tests: a change after the last write invalidating verified components and the final reconciliation (`Final_read_reconciles_all_reviewed_components_after_the_last_write`, partial with the history retained); a denied read-back (the Unix and Btrfs native journal cases keep the result uncertain and the history; the native writers read back through the handle they hold); lost connections (SFTP reconnect before the final read, drift on reconnect and lost replies); crash and torn tail (process kills at four boundaries on Windows, ext4, Btrfs and both Darwin routes, and a torn outcome record that reads as damaged with its intent kept); a delayed native return under cancellation; cancellation in a captured subtree on all three systems; and re-review without automatic undo (interrupted history in the App, verified-origin unmarking only while the marks are unchanged, and no write after a journal fault). On 22 November a local writer's native error after an earlier verified component ran on Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 ext4 (kernel `7.0.0-43`) under a seccomp filter that failed utimensat with EPERM and allowed every other call. A reviewed mode and modification-time change verified mode 0640, and the kernel then refused the time with errno 1. The result was partial: the time component failed before change, its read-back named the same object, and the modification time did not change. With no end record, the journal kept both calls, their outcomes and the error for recovery review, and the history was not damaged (`6ce5a39a5`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gboundary/receipts/ubuntu-gresult-times.xml` `6dad70578521ab2d22eccace59d1b6dbeacb5cbe9aeb8fdf7b0f1fc9f7d780f4`). Also on 22 November, real devices failed between the native call and the flush, with the flush hook used only to pause the job at that point. On macOS 27.2.1 arm64 the test force-detached the APFS image holding the reviewed executable after the parent-relative fchmodat or the descriptor fchmod had changed its mode. Both writers then reported the change as uncertain with verification unavailable, because reading the held volume failed with errno 45 after the flush. The reattached image held the new mode, so the result was uncertain, not wrong (`c444b1d67`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gresult-mac/flush-receipts/mac-gresult-flush.trx` `9ca59c13bda64ff3256345e566a6a7f4e4f01997f5302191c11aeba3493fffb6`, with the after-intent detach case, 3 passed). In the Hyper-V Windows 11 Pro guest (build 26200), the ordinary medium-integrity user `aclw2` ran a reviewed DACL grant on a hot-added NTFS data disk. The host flushed the volume cache once the call's intent was durable. After SetSecurityInfo returned and while the job waited in the flush hook, the host hot-removed the disk without another flush. The flush and read-back on the removed volume failed, so the job reported the change as uncertain with verification unavailable. The reattached disk still held the original DACL, so the change that had read back in memory never reached the device (`39bd2a93e`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gresult-windows-flush/receipts/windows-flushloss.xml` `03e9b83c331d9e214729502c91bbdc185f7f9d563847602df301c7d049b9f4dc`, `result.txt` `f7128f1c709c1a1d072413be7a370450a26035d936669f40db456185b9369a26`, `reattach.txt` `5163bdf5a22f0ddcea0377a603557e5fa7743baa1d7c6085b42104deaa09310b`, `host-timeline.txt`; the disk image was deleted). | Reopen on an incompatible journal/outcome protocol or retention change; additive record kinds follow the reopening rule. |
| G-TREE | Native propagation, traversal and reset are distinct | Passed for the listed subcases. The Windows x64 fixed NTFS probe covers direct, nested, protected and denied-write descendants under native propagation. Held-handle boundary capture refused hard links, a junction, an attached NTFS volume, Cloud Files, denied listing and post-review namespace drift. The opt-in product editor passed native scope consent, the protected and unprotected folder job, and late-arrival refusal. Ubuntu ext4 captured mode work verified children-first order, excluded links and bind mounts, hard-link deduplication, overlapping roots, denied listing, later arrivals, replaced ancestry, review cancellation and journal scope counts. The ext4 reset covered a matching template root, conditional and exact execute, preserved defaults, late arrivals, source revision conflict and real immutable and append-only exclusions. On 22 November the same ext4 guest added the remaining captured-mode subcases: a matching root reported as already matching, a folder that lost search after review (it and its member conflict without a call while the rest applies), cancellation once the first intent was durable (every member unattempted and unchanged), and a file and a folder with its file that disappeared after review. Immutable and append-only members are now left out of mode trees with the branch beneath a protected folder, as reset and Darwin already did, and a protected selection is refused. Linux directories now bind with their alias count, so a subfolder created or removed after review no longer turns the reviewed folder into a conflict (`58359f186`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gtree/ubuntu-gtree-core.trx`, 55 passed, `bc29fb33ff6f4217c7ad3b65defea83f318b66fa4d055c199222342064895740`). The isolated Windows 11 Pro x64 guest (build 26200, SDK 10.0.101) then ran the captured file rule as the ordinary medium-integrity user. A file deleted after review, and a file deleted with its folder, were each reported as a conflict ("The held entry is being removed.") without a native call, while the kept file received the rule. A job cancelled once its first intent was durable left both files unattempted with unchanged descriptors. The whole scope class passed 35 cases with five skips for the second-volume fixtures, and the seeded audit SACL and owner witnesses matched afterwards (`5cbda5629`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gtree-windows/receipts/windows-gtree-scope.xml`, `1986f4eadb6c377095fbf0b35e3e11ec5fc9e258d10368ef933c3985634af4e5`). Lost parent search has no Windows counterpart, because the ordinary token bypasses traverse checking and the job holds every captured object. Darwin APFS/HFS+ captured BSD mode trees verified protected and unreadable exclusions, real nested mounts, known directory drift, disappearing and substituted members, matching roots, conditional execute, and cancellation after a durable intent. Darwin ACL refresh and reset also matched native inheritance on both binding families, preserved protected branches and later arrivals, and refused a changed ACL source. On 22 November the Linux release floor ran the current tree suites. Ubuntu 24.04.5 booted the GA kernel `6.8.0-151-generic` with glibc 2.39; the kernel was installed for the run and removed afterwards. As UID 1000 every ext4 captured mode tree and reset case passed: children-first order, a matching root, lost search, cancellation after a durable intent, disappearing members, protected exclusions, replaced ancestry, the lease limit, conditional and exact execute, preserved defaults and a changed template. The XFS and Btrfs variants (captured mode tree, ACL reset, group change and removal) passed too. In a private mount namespace made by root, the tree left out a nested ext4 bind mount and an implicit Btrfs subvolume (`a64e86b6d` and `25a4ea8c5`; Core 60 passed with platform and fixture skips, 5 variant cases and 2 fixture cases passed). The same boot passed the Linux access, boundary, result and race runners. Receipts are listed in `.tmp/acl-validation/gtree/ga-floor/receipts.sha256` (`fd45e9eb3b9f35c73edc5b7598253d922d70266a60f60ad606408b239e40cb3a`). Windows and Darwin traversal and reset are recorded above; the remote routes offer no tree changes. | No listed subcase remains open. Reopen a model when its traversal or setters change. |
| G-WIN | Windows file, directory, removable NTFS, ReFS and volume root | Passed for the listed subcases. Windows x64 ordinary fixed NTFS reads, scope, object writes, retained audit metadata, exact fixed NTFS volume-root inspection and selected-root DACL writes, and a native mount boundary have passing evidence. A ReFS 3.14 Dev Drive passed the opt-in read path and junction refusal. `WindowsOwnershipTests` and `WindowsFileRecordsTests` establish narrower reads. On 22 November the inspector stopped refusing items on volumes without a Cloud Files filter instance (`a7514e949`), and the root route admits format-created roots without `SE_DACL_AUTO_INHERITED` (`24f6b20a8`); both passed natively. On 22 November a survey found DACLs stored without `SE_DACL_AUTO_INHERITED` (legacy form) on every format-made root and on everything created below one: the host's data volumes, this repository and `Documents` carry no inheritance marks. `GetSecurityInfo` reads such an unprotected DACL differently from how it is stored: it marks the entries that match the parent's current inheritable rules as inherited, lists them last, and reports the DACL as protected once a non-propagating parent change stops the match. `SetSecurityInfo` adds `SE_DACL_AUTO_INHERITED` to any legacy DACL (and re-marks and reorders an unprotected one) while an owner-only write leaves it alone, so an ordinary or authorized file edit changed bits the review never showed and then failed verification. Commit `a430c0295` reads the stored descriptor with `NtQuerySecurityObject`, writes legacy file DACLs as descriptors with `NtSetSecurityObject` on the ordinary and authorized routes, admits unprotected legacy folders on the folder-only and captured-folder routes, keeps reset on automatic inheritance, and offers no native propagation or inheritance change for a legacy folder; the inspector names the legacy form. Native tests on the host passed: protected and unprotected legacy files kept their stored control and order with only the reviewed entry added, a parent change left the child's revision unchanged, a legacy folder kept its form and its child through a folder-only edit, and a legacy folder offered no propagation. In the isolated guest the installed candidate upgraded in place to 1.0.4 from `a430c0295` (helper replaced, install-root ACL unchanged, 30 helper-protocol tests passed). As the medium-integrity requester on a disposable fixture volume, the 73 selected scope, legacy-form and inspection cases passed with 11 fixture skips, among them native propagation, folder-only and captured-tree writes, reset, root writes and inheritance on automatic DACLs. Through the broker the requester added a read rule for Users to an Administrators-owned legacy file that denies it `WRITE_DAC`: FileCat verified the change, and the stored descriptor kept control `0x8004` and its four entries in order with the new one appended. The original fixture folder `R:\acl-fixtures` refuses the medium requester's deletes and new files despite its Modify grant, so the run used a VHD that was then removed. Receipts are listed in `.tmp/acl-validation/w3-journeys/receipts-legacy.sha256` (`350f6868247d4ca951b1791758b50cec83554c40249ebd5005c8c55c0d6e6bd8`). On 22 November, a hot-removed fixed NTFS data disk failed a reviewed DACL change before dispatch with complete journal history, and an opt-in removable-media reader passed inspection on a real USB stick. On 22 November the designated USB test stick (Silicon-Power 8G, serial checked, NTFS on removable media) attached to the Windows 11 VMware guest qualified a new ordinary writer for files on removable NTFS. It needs `FILECAT_QUALIFY_WINDOWS_REMOVABLE_WRITES` with the qualified reader (`90787b783`, `6c9f5bf45`). Folders, trees, roots and the administrator route on removable media stay inspection-only, and the reviewed binding's rechecks are those of fixed volumes. At medium integrity a reviewed DACL change to a task-owned file there verified, with its journal on the system volume, while the default service and an unqualified prepare refused. Then a person pulled the stick twice during a job. Pulled after the change's intent was durable, the job reported failed before change with no native call; the journal held the intent and the outcome and recovery had nothing to review; after reinsertion the document kept its original DACL. Pulled after the native call and before the folder flush, the job reported uncertain with verification unavailable (`SetSecurityInfo` DACL, one flush), and after reinsertion the stored DACL showed the reviewed grant had reached the device. The test folders were removed from the stick and the guest (`.tmp/acl-validation/gusb/receipts-gusb.sha256`, `fa5d12bc871221ca631e09b460c7321728794a0392c35b0ad81b5a62eb16bc4f`). The authorized audit route now writes a SACL stored without `SE_SACL_AUTO_INHERITED` as a descriptor that keeps that form, verified live with an inherited-marked entry (G-AUDIT, `2cbfe5b16`). The same day the ReFS writer was qualified for files (`c668f9ac8`). `FILECAT_QUALIFY_WINDOWS_REFS_WRITES` with the qualified ReFS reader lets the ordinary route keep a held binding for a file on ReFS, and the review, namespace and flush walks admit ReFS only for a ReFS binding. Folders, trees, roots, audit and the administrator route on ReFS stay inspection-only. In the isolated guest, on a disposable sparse 51 GiB Dev Drive (ReFS 3.14, trusted developer volume protected by the antivirus filter), the medium-integrity user `aclw2` verified a reviewed DACL change to a task-owned file; the stored DACL kept its automatic form with the new explicit entry ahead of the inherited ones, while the reader alone and an unqualified prepare refused, and a folder there stayed inspection-only. The ReFS reader and its junction refusal passed again. The image and stage were removed (`.tmp/acl-validation/grefs/receipts.sha256`, `4b90dd122916d5d598bea9c5a99cb12e3b534cd28b249a481b724936cbbea2db`). The same day the two Windows setters were compared on twin files under one parent with inheritable rules in the isolated guest, as the medium-integrity user `aclw2` (`WindowsSetterComparisonTests`). Each shape was stored identically on both twins, then one twin got the new DACL from `SetSecurityInfo` and the other from `NtSetSecurityObject`. Both stored the automatic, protected, conditional-and-object, non-canonical-order and protect-conversion shapes as given. `SetSecurityInfo` replaced a moved file's stale inherited entry with the parent's entries, turned a legacy DACL into automatic form with the parent's entries, filled NULL and emptied DACLs with the parent's entries, re-inherited on unprotect, and propagated a folder's new inheritable rule into both sentinel children; `NtSetSecurityObject` stored every shape as given and left the children alone. FileCat's ordinary route used `SetSecurityInfo` for automatic file DACLs, so editing a moved file replaced its inherited entries unreviewed and reported an uncertain outcome. Commit `ae1f6894c` writes every object-only DACL change, ordinary and administrator, as a descriptor through `NtSetSecurityObject`; native propagation and owner changes keep `SetSecurityInfo`. FileCat then verified the moved-file edit with the reviewed stale entry kept, and the native Windows permission classes passed (92 cases, 52 needing other fixtures skipped). The journal kill test sometimes found its journal still held by another process right after the kill (a sharing violation); the recovery scan skips a held journal by design, so the test now rescans for up to five seconds (`69a8d273e`, three clean repeats). Receipts are listed in `.tmp/acl-validation/gsetters/receipts.sha256` (`35ef52601461dcc4e9ba4af3f1a73a92847a39c38beaae0a2189949bc5a31b99`). The legacy form then got its three remaining pieces (`f379e0ae6`, `6c784b5cc`). For a legacy DACL on a file or folder, inspection reads the parent through a fresh walk over the same ancestors and shows, beside the stored entries, how Windows tools read them: `ConvertToAutoInheritPrivateObjectSecurity` marks the entries the parent's inheritable rules produce as inherited and lists them last, or reads the DACL as protected. In the guest that reading equalled `GetNamedSecurityInfo`'s entries and protection, before and after a non-propagating parent change. A new reviewed conversion stores exactly that reading in automatic form as a descriptor (control `0x8404`), for files and qualified folders, only while the parent still produces it; a parent change after inspection made it refuse. A converted folder kept its children and then offered native propagation. The folder-only route now accepts a legacy DACL whose trailing entries carry inheritance marks: it added the reviewed rule before them and kept the marks and control `0x8004`, as `NtSetSecurityObject` did for the same shape in the setter comparison. The native Windows permission classes passed as `aclw2` (98 cases, 52 needing other fixtures skipped), and the App lists the reading under "Windows tools read this DACL as" (`.tmp/acl-validation/gsetters/receipts-legacy.sha256`, `1e2c517fdd962011335f19e34427790a06fe6009bd55725e04520157c18046c7`). The fetch sentinel then ran on more on-demand providers in the guest as `aclw2` (`6195a3c50`). The test Cloud Files sync root served no data under the partial, progressive and full hydration policies, and its placeholder was inspected, admitted and recorded with no data request each time; the always-full policy refuses a placeholder that is not downloaded (`0x8007017C`), so it has no such case. A second provider class, the Projected File System (as VFS for Git uses), was enabled for the run and a test provider projected one 5,000-byte file and counted every request for its data. FileCat inspected the projected file, the virtualization root and the folder above with no data request. ProjFS hides the root's reparse point, so the root reads as an ordinary folder whose own DACL can be edited, while the projected file carries `RECALL_ON_DATA_ACCESS` and is refused, and no propagation boundary can be captured through the root or the folder above. Providers that need a signed-in account (OneDrive and others) were not run. The feature was disabled again (`.tmp/acl-validation/gsetters/receipts-providers.sha256`, `98ebc2e98a3ffca541efa6a3298522920a447a24c5ecd487141932bc7364f69e`). On 23 November the ARM64 native smoke ran on the Windows 11 Pro on Arm guest (build 26300.9457, UTM on the Apple silicon test Mac) as admin at medium integrity. The native win-arm64 self-contained test host from `2dc299795` (`ec05037a8` added a temporary switch that admitted native Arm64) ran three passes. On a disposable "FileCat ACL Fixture" NTFS volume with the audit and owner witnesses, the scope class passed 36 of 41: folder-only, captured file and folder trees, native propagation, reset, inheritance changes and the propagation boundary. The five skips need a second disposable volume. On the system volume the inspection, legacy-DACL reading and conversion, setter-comparison, access-estimate and journal classes passed 54 of 68; the 14 skips are the removable, ReFS, SMB, volume-root, hot-removal and High-label fixtures and the x64 host check, and the journal kill test was left out because a self-contained package has no shared dotnet host for its child. Without the switch native Arm64 still refused both the reader and the writer. Commit `f26e40f2b` then admits native Arm64 by default, and an x64 copy running emulated on Windows on Arm is refused with a reason that names the Arm64 build.
