The isolated Windows 11 Pro x64 guest (build 26200) compared the AuthZ evaluator with real opens by the same token: a group made deny-only stopped granting, a token restricted to Everyone needed both passes (read allowed, write refused), and an OWNER RIGHTS rule replaced the owner's implicit READ_CONTROL and WRITE_DAC. A conditional rule on a user claim left every estimated right incomplete with its reason (`776a049d4`). A probe showed that AuthZ ignores a mandatory label even when the evaluated descriptor carries it: a medium process was granted 0x001F01FF on a High no-write-up file it could not open for writing. The estimate now reads the bound object's label and the token's integrity level and mandatory policy, and withholds the rights measured on that build: no-write-up removes write data, append, write EA, delete child, write attributes, DELETE, WRITE_DAC and WRITE_OWNER; no-read-up removes read data, read EA and READ_CONTROL; no-execute-up removes execute. The report names the refused checks and the panel shows both integrity levels. Because ext4, XFS and Btrfs all report inode protection, the incomplete Linux removal answer was produced at the system call: a seccomp supervisor hid the immutable and append-only bits from statx's attributes mask and value. On Ubuntu 24.04.5 ext4, hiding both bits, either bit of the entry, or the folder's append-only bit made the removal answer incomplete, while read and write were still answered by the kernel. With nothing hidden, or with only the folder's immutable bit hidden, removal was allowed, because the kernel's write and search check on the folder already refuses an immutable folder (`ef1b311c6`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gboundary/receipts/ubuntu-gaccess-incomplete.xml` `8270622e601f2d49851687cd0fdbd6d63d525c0fe03a5bce1e908b8825e2815a`). | No listed subcase remains open. The utility applies no role restriction: on a Preboot-role volume added to a disposable image's container (`addVolume -role B`), both verbs succeeded and changed the policy. FileCat reads the policy afresh from `fstatfs` on each inspection. While root switched the policy, both Darwin writer routes showed the new policy. A mode review prepared while ownership was honored made no call after the switch, and the change was offered again once ownership was honored (`c22a24afe`; `.tmp/acl-validation/gvol/mac-gvol-core.trx`, 2 passed, `5f8984c9ec9e9f9fce2e854455fb7b6dec3cc39441d2f459f40c5e10382799b9`; `mac-gvol-run.log` `55f739ef87e3bd3d57a02d26582f6e5f092435c0a14bb74915155dd59a553a9d`; `mac-gvol-role.log` `7e0c6a440def49e619791cb5b4ac6b35b89f3a9ad98b13b874ed01855cee7a50`). The images and staging were removed. | (authorized, D3) In-app consent, cancelled authority and setter. The setter must refuse role volumes itself, because the utility accepts them. It must bind the volume UUID together with the device and mount instance and recheck them around the call, because device identifiers are reused. It must refuse a UUID that names more than one attached volume, because the utility then picks one. Fail closed if exact binding or durable meaning is unavailable. Reopen on OS/utility/role change. |
| G-MOUNT | Server vs client projection, unsupported and unavailable media | Passed for the listed subcases. The opt-in Linux inspector passed native FAT, read-only FAT, exFAT, bindfs FUSE, kernel CIFS, GVfs SMB, a read-only NFSv4.1 export and three OpenZFS datasets. Independent server reads established the CIFS and GVfs mode differences, and the CIFS client still received the server's denial with `noperm`. The original CIFS subject reported gone after detach and stale after remount. The NFS export enforced root squash, and the panel withheld writes on FAT, CIFS, GVfs, NFSv4.1 and OpenZFS. The OpenZFS cases corroborated the effective POSIX ACL state against the kernel and dataset configuration. The disconnected Linux NFS fixture refused both a previously bound and a first-time path, preserving the prior observation date only for the bound subject. A read-only FAT image detach and reattach retained the dated observation and refused path and device reuse. A Windows NTFS data disk hot-removed after a durable intent received no write and kept complete history, and an opt-in reader inspected removable NTFS on a USB stick. A macOS APFS image force-detached after a durable intent likewise received no write. An ext4 device failing after a durable intent no longer reads back as a verified change: the writer's filesystem flush failed and the result was uncertain, matching the unchanged mode on disk. A loopback SMB share on Windows 11 build 26200 exposed that the redirector answers NtQuerySecurityObject with a ReturnLength of 0 for a descriptor that fit the buffer (140 bytes on the probe; local NTFS reports 140), so every Windows SMB inspection failed as an invalid descriptor length. The reader now validates the self-relative descriptor within the buffer and takes its stated length, and validates every descriptor within its length; the UNC projection case then passed (`23bb16edd`). On 22 November the other claimed Linux families were disconnected while a subject stayed bound, on Ubuntu 24.04.5 (kernel `7.0.0-43`). A root harness disconnected each mount and waited until an independent read as UID 1000 failed. It ended the session's gvfsd-fuse for a GVfs SMB share, killed a bindfs daemon, and stopped the private Samba server behind a kernel CIFS mount (soft, noperm). The captured subjects then read as failed with the client's error: errno 107 for GVfs and bindfs, and errno 112 for CIFS, after the client gave up in 5.9 s. The server-backed GVfs and CIFS cases named the mounted client. Each kept its observation date and admitted no review. A daemon that is only terminated unmounts cleanly, and the subject then reads as gone (`ece59743b`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gmount/logs/ubuntu-gmount-disconnect.xml`, 3 passed, `9f0a8141fe43af34d5a410861fa8eaf416c1883b1b7bedecaf36f4202a2d1151`). On 22 November a person pulled the designated USB stick from the Windows 11 VMware guest during two reviewed DACL jobs on it: after the intent (failed before change, no call, complete journal, document unchanged) and between the call and the folder flush (uncertain, verification unavailable; the grant had reached the device). See G-WIN. | No listed subcase remains open. Windows SMB inspection matched client descriptors through UNC and a mapped alias, and the product showed the projection without offering changes. No mounted family claims a write, so there is no server-side write to verify. Reopen for a new server dialect, adapter or mount policy. |
| G-REMOTE | SFTP server-path changes, changed fields and partial effects | Passed for the listed subcases. The source-built adapter passed exact-path LSTAT, wire presence and unsigned-ID checks, server account lookup, and one native mode-000 repair. The inspector passed for a mode-000 item and a symbolic link. The OpenSSH 9.6p1 review gate corroborated the subsystem, account, path and retained session. Mode, group and compound dispatch passed on Ubuntu OpenSSH, including private journal refusal, ordered grants, and reconnect after successful and denied lost write replies. The app reviewed and applied mode and compound changes against the qualified Ubuntu server, and refused missing extensions and stale reviews. Inbox Windows OpenSSH 9.5p2 and the Microsoft 10.4p1 preview passed live inspection and refused change review. Release packaging is tracked in G-PKG. On 22 November the listed protocol subcases were mapped to passing tests, rerun on Ubuntu 24.04.5 against the owned OpenSSH 9.6p1 server (glibc 2.39, kernel `7.0.0-43`; 51 passed, the Windows OpenSSH case skipped; `08ad5c624`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gremote/ubuntu-gremote.trx` `29f866f361adb82125bc644f00b49c46e8046f8e73b7c4e7c70afa846337a41c`): packet flags and the unsigned UID/GID pair (`Lsetstat_serializes_*`), high unsigned IDs and presence distinct from zero, no unrequested fields, special octal and explicit set-ID ordering with the final state (`clear-before-group`, `keep-set-id`, `explicit-set-id-restore`, `compound`), an unchanged modification time, exact paths read root first, replaced leaf, ancestor, session and profile refused, server denial and lost replies after the first component, mode-zero repair through LSTAT without opening, link and FIFO leaves refused without an open, group-writable (including Ubuntu's 0775 user-private-group folders), sticky, other-owned and symbolic ancestors (a 0755 symbolic link as a macOS server reports it as well as 0777), UID-0 and owner-mismatched leaves, a root login, an uncorroborated account, and a group-plus-mode change checked against FileCat's own read between requests. On 22 November the remaining server configurations and races ran on Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 (kernel `7.0.0-43`). Through this VM's qualified sshd (OpenSSH 9.6p1 Ubuntu-3ubuntu13.20) with a `ChrootDirectory` start directory, a reviewed mode change verified: glibc 2.39 used `fchmodat2`, with no `/proc` in the jail. Then a seccomp filter failed `fchmodat2` with ENOSYS, as a kernel before 6.6 does. In the same chrooted session without `/proc` the server replied FAILURE with an unchanged LSTAT. The job reported that the server could not apply the mode change, the session then refused further mode reviews, and inspection continued. With `/proc` mounted in the jail the change verified. The sshd was also chrooted into Ubuntu 20.04 and 22.04 trees whose sftp-server was upstream 9.6p1 built against each tree's own C library (source verified by apt, SHA-256 `910211c07255a8c5ad654391b40ee59800710dd8119dd5362de09385aa7a777c`). glibc 2.31 failed the change both with and without `/proc`. glibc 2.35 failed it without `/proc` and verified it with `/proc`. Upstream builds name themselves `OpenSSH_9.6` and stay unqualified; only Debian and Ubuntu builds add `p1` to the banner. Two temporary UID-0 aliases, with a nologin shell and a home only inside the jail, signed in to the qualified sshd. Their chrooted start directory was owned by another user. With users-groups-by-id the owner mapped to that user, and admission refused the item. With the request refused (`internal-sftp -P`), FileCat now gives the same refusal ("could not corroborate the leaf owner") instead of a permission error (`1e4d25aac`). A proxy in front of the stock sftp-server swapped the reviewed file just before the lsetstat reached the server. The server changed the replacement and the original kept its mode. The job reported the change as uncertain, with verification unavailable. A second account owned a folder below root-owned folders, and with protected hard links off it linked this account's private file into its leaf's place. That leaf was inspection-only before the link (its owner differed from the start-directory owner) and after it (an ancestor was owned by another account), and the private file kept mode 0600. On the GA kernel `6.8.0-151-generic` with glibc 2.39, the owned 9.6p1 server passed 52 SFTP admission, job, race and wire cases, with 3 harness skips. The App passed 9 SFTP panel cases, whose review discloses that accounts with ancestor write rights through server ACLs can still redirect a request (`b63ca4f6b`). The kernel was removed afterwards, and the alias accounts, the linker account and the account files were removed or restored. Receipts are listed in `.tmp/acl-validation/gremote/receipts.sha256` (`d10cbc0ceab688793e1b73c17fa11b90368f9f4a57684b7ae368e0e5e8d571ba`); its two `uid0` receipts from the upstream-built sshd record that unqualified-release refusal. On 23 November an ancestor with NFSv4-native ACLs ran. FreeBSD 15.1 (the official BASIC-CI image under QEMU on the Ubuntu VM) exported a ZFS dataset (`acltype` nfsv4, `aclmode` and `aclinherit` passthrough) over NFSv4.2, mounted below the qualified sshd. Its folder `shared` showed mode 0755, owned by the signed-in account, while an NFSv4 entry let UID 1001 add and delete entries; the Linux client saw that entry only in `system.nfs4_acl`. UID 1001 created, renamed and deleted a file there and was refused in a sibling folder without the entry. Unlike Linux knfsd, which stores NFSv4 ACLs as POSIX ACLs and so raises the group bits that admission refuses, no ancestor's mode showed the grant: FileCat admitted the review of a file in `shared`, every ancestor showed no group or other write and none was owned by UID 1001, and revalidation passed. This is the residual the review discloses (server ACLs that SFTP does not show). QEMU, the mount, the temporary account and the packages the run installed were removed (`e6eaf0a06`; receipts in `.tmp/acl-validation/gremote-nfs4/receipts.sha256`, `70abba5941db937082b8ccc1f00f8af180b91455435870a77c1d958524184d61`). | No listed subcase remains open. Windows OpenSSH and a server without lsetstat stay inspection-only. Reopen on SSH.NET, protocol or server change. |
| G-FTP | Observed FTP permission facts; no setter | Passed on the Ubuntu x64 loopback fixtures. Plain FTP, explicit and implicit FTPS, MLSD facts, Unix LIST provenance and the panel read-only flow are recorded in the receipt above. Production server and package qualification remain open. On 22 November, 55 portable parser tests (`2b928a04c`) covered raw MLSD facts (`perm`, `unique`, `unix.mode`, owner and group IDs and names) with octal validation, repeated and malformed facts, partial or mismatched MLSD lines, Unix LIST mode letters including set-ID and sticky bits, exact LIST names and the names FileCat refuses (CR, LF, backslash, edge spaces). A LIST mode followed by `+` is now reported as incomplete access instead of a complete mode. The loopback run already asserts that no `SITE CHMOD` is sent. The same day closed a FluentFTP gap: FluentFTP 55 accepts any certificate an FTPS data connection presents (`FtpDataStream`'s constructor), so before the change a listing over data connections presenting another, untrusted certificate succeeded without a question (`100ba1bcd`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gftp/prefix/ubuntu-gftp-prefix.trx`, 199 passed and that case failed). FileCat now records the certificate the control connection was accepted with. Every listing, read, write and append data connection must be encrypted and present it, or it is closed before a byte crosses it, and a control connection opened again within the session must present the same certificate. A session accepted once is not asked again for its data connections. Permission inspection refuses relative, doubled-separator, `.` and `..` folder paths before any listing, including a working folder the server reports with a `.` segment. Ubuntu 24.04.5 x64 (pyftpdlib 1.5.9, pyOpenSSL 23.2.0) passed all 200 FTP cases and macOS 27.2.1 arm64 (pyftpdlib 2.2.0, pyOpenSSL 26.4.0) passed 198, with its two crash-harness cases passing in a build without a runtime identifier (`d7e3361d3`, `.tmp/acl-validation/gftp/fixed/`, Ubuntu trx `80e9693797c525f23f75458820b82df9198a4939e83d8474867f27258494c48e`, macOS trx `04eb0fe76e0b60ee811e3aa62a03259939ca6afa754fc9e2d831c4f65a361927` and `19ad461d7f121c3db6d2392d49a83f518d05a3c82801d4d2fb77604272cfdf89`). On 22 November four packaged servers on the Ubuntu 24.04.5 VM were asked for a folder holding a file and a folder with named-user POSIX ACLs (`getfacl` showed the entries and GNU `ls` the `+`): inetutils-ftpd 2.5, whose LIST runs its built-in BSD `ls`, vsftpd 3.0.5, ProFTPD 1.3.8b and Pure-FTPd 1.0.50. Each listed both entries as plain modes (`-rw-r-----`, `drwxr-x---`) without the marker. ProFTPD's and Pure-FTPd's MLSD facts carried only `UNIX.mode`, owner and group (ProFTPD also `perm`), and inetutils and vsftpd offer no MLSD. FileCat's reader takes such a mode alone and leaves access unreported, never complete (portable parser tests). The packages and stage were purged (`.tmp/acl-validation/gftp-live/receipts.sha256`, `3cc7c2604ca8bbfc36ea72e89d7134ffa039e43dc16308a688dde89b03ff34d3`). On 23 November a live server emitted the marker. FreeBSD's ftpd 6.00LS (the `freebsd-ftpd` port 20260512, whose LIST runs `/bin/ls -lA`) on FreeBSD 15.1 under QEMU on the Ubuntu VM served a ZFS dataset with NFSv4 ACLs. Its LIST marked a file and a folder that carry a named-user entry with `+` (`-rw-r--r--+`, `drwxr-xr-x+`) and not their plain siblings, and it offers no MLSD. Through FileCat's FTP reader all four kept their exact modes from Unix LIST; the marked two reported access as incomplete ("The server listing marks access rules beyond the mode that FTP does not describe."), the plain two left access unreported, and none offered a change (`d80b79957`, `FtpLiveListingTests`). QEMU, the image and the packages the run installed were removed (`.tmp/acl-validation/gftp-bsd/receipts.sha256`, `010ce64213d8ee9479fc2648249b9c956b0575607d1483ed1961837e6ac57b78`). | No listed subcase remains open. Reopen on FluentFTP/server/parser change (the data-connection check reads FluentFTP 55's private TLS stream field), or for a server offering a no-follow or identity-checked setter. |
| G-PKG | Per-package release assertions and installed/portable separation | Partial. The Ubuntu 26.04.1 x64 smoke independently verified .deb install, activation, unpack, configure and purge. The tarball and AppImage omitted the privileged payload and refused the authorization entry in the live GUI. ### Independent review disposition

The author-reported initial review decisions are retained below. A subsequent independent document/source/API review on 19 November 2026 accepted the follow-up corrections shown here. It did not reproduce the recorded Git/worktree state or historical native receipts; no runtime gate was promoted to passed. A third independent review later on 19 November 2026 (rows "Mainline and privilege" to "Migration and qualification") used three reviewers and checked HEAD, current mainline and primary XNU (`main`, macOS 26 sources), Apple Libc, OpenSSH `master`, pinned SSH.NET and FluentFTP, and Linux 6.8 sources. An adversarial fourth round (the "Adversarial" rows), a fifth (the "Round-3" rows), a sixth (the "Round-4" rows), a seventh (the "Round-5" rows), an eighth (the "Round-6" rows) and a ninth (the "Round-7" rows) re-reviewed the revised plan with fresh reviewers against the same sources plus Win32-OpenSSH, glibc, HFS+, polkit, Apple authorization and .NET runtime code. None ran native tests or promoted a gate.

| Review | Finding and disposition |
|---|---|
| Windows and privilege | Accepted. Require authenticated v2 IPC beyond v1 PID/path checks, distinguish installed-file trust from signer verification, and handle assignment to the requester SID under alternate-admin UAC. W2's unelevated Authz calculation checks each returned result and W1/W2 must pass a Windows cloud-provider fetch sentinel. |
| Unix and providers | Accepted. Removed the private Darwin directory-lease route. D1 explicitly separates directory, executable regular-file and already-bound descriptor lanes from the unresolved non-executable document case (superseded: the parent-relative lane resolves it). Apple's libc link/delete implementations constrain ACL removal and link eligibility. R1 distinguishes OpenSSH file/OPENDIR handles and fd vs path fallback in server builds. L3/D3 must bind actual reviewed scope to system authorization, including alternate administrators. |
| UX and architecture | Accepted. U1 captures marked drive tags and hidden-mark state at menu opening and keeps the capture through the Shell fallback. Find has its own registration and scoped dispatch. External Properties refresh occurs on workspace reactivation or explicit Refresh. D1 owns the basic bound mode writer; migrated legacy entry points share the same refusal decisions as the canonical command. |
| Follow-up Windows | Added W3's scoped backup-privilege DACL baseline acquisition without ownership/ACL mutation, its G-PRIV case, and the precise owner-assignment API citation. |
| Follow-up Unix/remote | Specified server-qualified ownership/mode ordering and final compound-state reconciliation; G-REMOTE/G-RESULT cover later invalidation of earlier verified fields. |
| Follow-up UX/delivery | Ordinary loading/refresh stays unelevated; mandatory macOS ordinary-document acquisition controls migration/completion. Package implementation state is distinct from shared release-gate subcases. |
| Final UX/UI | Accepted source/design refinements in §3, U1 and G-UX: persistent Apply and local Enter ownership, discoverable disabled-state errors, bounded review/list layout, safe identity display/exact copy, semantic color pairs, live readability/theme state and recovery-oriented messages. Three independent reviewers checked interaction, layout/accessibility and theme/style seams. No editor was rendered and no UI/native gate passed in this review. |
| Mainline and privilege | Accepted. Elevated FileCat processes refuse the command, and no administrator task or retry carries its job. The mainline Unix administrator action stays a separately labelled legacy route outside the trust model, retired per subject class by L3/D3. W3 writes a denying DACL under scoped `SeRestorePrivilege`. D3 is Blocked by DEC-03. Authorized SACL, flag and volume subcases move to W3/L3/D3. Its "decision outside this plan" deferral is superseded: §6 asks the owner to decide before U1 ships. |
| macOS and remote | Accepted. Ordinary documents use the parent-relative lane (no opens, requester-only folders, knote-detected swaps, stated residual). SFTP changes are server-path-verified through `lsetstat@openssh.com` after an SSH.NET dependency decision; FTP gets no setter this release. Linux `fchownat` and `CLONE_FS` statements corrected. Rejected: a client `O_RDONLY` document route for D1, since the lane opens nothing and also covers data-inaccessible items; D3 later replaced its client open with a daemon open using `O_UNIQUE`. |
| Migration and qualification | Accepted. U1 replaces the Attributes dialog's permission validation for every Unix subject; writers migrate per class; recursion stays legacy until G-TREE; combined-submission and partition rules; I1 owns the P0 readers and (read) subcases; the Qualified state; per-subcase reopening; U1's named dialog changes. |
| Adversarial delivery | Accepted. Legacy administrator action scope after migration; permissions-first combined job with joint unmarking and Finder Hidden off `Fs.SetAttributes`; subcase owner tags; Qualified requires every advertised route to pass; one DEC-03 exit; check box/option Enter rule; reopening for shared code and OS updates; ARM64/26.04 owners. |
| Adversarial remote/privilege | Accepted. SFTP ancestors must be non-link, owned by root or the leaf owner, without group/other write; only qualified OpenSSH portable servers (not Windows OpenSSH) and stated libc/`/proc` limits; raw exact-path LSTAT; the polkit message or daemon prompt is the binding consent against hooked genuine clients; no-standard-token message; legacy action decision before U1 ships; W3 single backup-intent handle. |
| Adversarial Darwin/Linux | Accepted. Lane admission adds entry ownership, mount owner, kauth-style rights evaluation, `NOTE_ATTRIB` and per-call re-checks, a call order without transient widening and an "applied; folder changed" verdict; HFS+ `exchangedata` limits descriptors; helpers refuse authority-granting changes on hard-linked aliases (superseded: any change that is not strictly restrictive); predicted set-ID effects; firmlink-aware `O_SEARCH` walk. |
| Round-3 delivery | Accepted. Implementation state separated from per-route qualification; listed routes essential unless marked optional; a subcase belongs to the package implementing its route (G-BIND/G-RESULT on write packages, Unix access explanations in L2/D2); finite reopening that holds back release claims; legacy items in mixed selections, and over-budget submissions, keep the legacy writer; D1 builds the `UF_HIDDEN` primitive. |
| Round-3 remote/privilege | Accepted. SFTP leaves need positive owner evidence (no root sessions) and later requests compare with FileCat's own read; qualified-server evidence named; macOS rights registered without shared credentials; polkit checked with the `unix-session` subject (superseded in round 5: polkitd aborts on session subjects, so L3 uses the bus-name subject and discloses the per-process-agent residual); untrusted consent values escaped; D3's daemon open with `O_UNIQUE` replaces the client open. |
| Round-3 Darwin/Linux | Accepted. One no-widening order for every compound change; hard-linked aliases admit only strictly restrictive changes; HFS+ descriptors require that no one else can obtain read and write; entry fsid equals the parent's; legacy mode write hardened to no-follow, with unbindable classes added to the §6 legacy-writer decision; preserved set-group-ID and minimal-ACL read-back. |
| Round-4 delivery | Accepted. One Permissions job per submission; times and Finder Hidden writers stated once (bound times for migrated items, D1's primitive for legacy Finder Hidden); row triggers govern reopening; hard-link gate rows match §6; the volume-ownership setter is optional; the §6 legacy-writer decision covers every legacy-writer class. |
| Round-4 remote/privilege | Accepted. SFTP refuses logins named root and requires start-directory ownership confirmed by `users-groups-by-id` where offered; the residual names root variants and server ACLs (SFTP shows only mode bits); OpenSSH 8.0+ evidence by release; polkit's session derived as polkitd does (superseded in round 5 as the polkit subject; round 6 keeps it for sender authentication); a G-PRIV case for an obscured Windows consent. |
| Round-4 Darwin/Linux | Accepted. Bound times by `utimensat` on the pin and Darwin attribute-list calls; directory link counts from `ATTR_DIR_LINKCOUNT` and no write-generation identity; the legacy hardening's parent open and Darwin link behavior; a named macOS floor. |
| Round-5 delivery | Accepted. The legacy-writer decision is stated once in §6 and recorded under §9 "Owner decisions"; subcases moved to the packages that implement them; the (read) tag defined; optional G-VOL subcases; "applied; folder changed" no longer blocks later components; UI-framework pin reopens G-UX; named Mac prerequisites; palette keyword ranking. |
| Round-5 remote/privilege | Accepted. L3 checks polkit with the sender's bus-name subject, because polkitd aborts on session subjects, and discloses the per-process-agent residual; untrusted values reach polkit only through the installed message's details; the macOS daemon never pre-authorizes; R1 delivers mode and group changes. |
| Round-5 Darwin/Linux | Accepted. The hardened legacy write derives the mode from its own no-follow read (not .NET's link-following `GetUnixFileMode`), verifies the resulting mode, discloses restored swaps and never uses the three-argument `fchmodat` syscall; the lane compares change time only with FileCat's own reads. |
| Round-6 delivery | Accepted. The administrator action stays offered for items the requester does not own and splits mixed selections; the legacy-writer decision covers FileCat running as root; per-root baselines for the legacy write; G-VOL display moved to G-MAC; tags, tracking cells and the new lane verdict made consistent. |
| Round-6 remote/privilege | Accepted. D3 refuses a client ref that already holds the right (least-privileged refs keep rights without timeouts); sender authentication derives the session as polkitd does; MLSD facts parsed from the fact list only; undecodable names shown as escapes; the startup-hook rule moved to the envelope; a G-PRIV case for hard links under propagation. |
| Round-6 Darwin/Linux | Accepted. Recursive legacy descendants get the same no-follow write; the lane's ACL condition applies to the parent and, for directory entries, to the entry itself; a reviewed grant is not a changed condition; HFS+ descriptor rule names root; legacy expected modes apply set-ID prediction. |
| Round-7 delivery | Accepted. Marked link roots keep today's skip; the administrator action is offered when a descriptor open lacks search or execute rights and returns to the open dialog; per-root baselines compare type and identity only; one owner-decision slot for development-signed D3 runs; readers and tags made uniform. |
| Round-7 remote/privilege | Accepted. D3 calls authd with `kAuthorizationFlagDestroyRights`, so the administrator's credential never stays on the client's ref; W3 never propagates non-restrictive changes into folders the requester can add entries to; `$APPIMAGE` substitution and the .deb restriction stated precisely; R1 queries `users-groups-by-id`; helper packages own the startup-hook build rule. |
| Round-7 Darwin/Linux | Accepted. The legacy recursive walk becomes a descriptor walk, since .NET reopens queued subfolders by path; the legacy administrator action and a root FileCat refuse hard-linked aliases; directory entries' write-security and change-owner grants count; later components after an admission-changing review need a separate Apply. |
| Implementation readiness, 20 November 2026 | Accepted two bounded corrections: legacy restored-swap tests distinguish detectable drift from matching-observation residuals; D3 qualifies all special-node open effects. Clarified the existing before-U1-release owner decision. I1 is ready to start. This review claims no current-mainline or native qualification pass. |

## 10. Definition of done and implementation launcher

A package is Qualified only as §8 defines it; in practice the normal FileCat entry point works on its claimed subjects, a no-op/cancel changes nothing, every dependent mutation has a preserved before-state and exact scope, and results show what was actually verified. Unread sections, denied access, unavailable helpers and partial tree effects have actionable in-app explanations. Claims include the exact platform, filesystem/provider, operation and package. Each required local platform has an assigned delivery package and any failed native gate remains visible. Completion of the cross-platform feature requires actual passing ordinary and privileged journeys on its advertised installed packages, including Linux and macOS. macOS privileged journeys wait on §6's DEC-03 exit; until D3 ships, macOS claims stay ordinary-only and the cross-platform feature is incomplete whatever the owner decides. A documented restriction can close an unsupported optional route, but it cannot stand in for an essential advertised edit.

**Implementation launcher.** Read this plan, the original planning prompt and any applicable repository instructions. Revalidate HEAD against current mainline, working changes, native/package versions and the relevant seams. Execute the next eligible §8 package (as §8 defines eligible), beginning with I1. Preserve unrelated work and still-applicable evidence. Update the package's §8 state and the same §9 ledger with subcase, baseline, result, receipt, remaining work and reopening trigger. Run the automated suites §9 requires, plus only the targeted native regression and named qualification gates, on authorized disposable fixtures. Keep unsupported lanes unavailable with their reason. Continue to the agreed scope's completion or stop at a concrete blocker with its failing evidence, affected claim and required prerequisite.
